# Benchmark Ledger

No routing benchmark claims are recorded because the repository does not yet ship a router.

> **Amendment — 2026-08-03:** ADR-0006 supersedes the final clause above: the repository now ships a
> narrow candidate-only two-pin A* reference. No routing benchmark claim is recorded yet; the first
> result must satisfy the evidence table below and must not be generalized beyond its synthetic
> fixture and supported geometry.

> **Second amendment — 2026-08-03:** B-003 supersedes the prior “no routing benchmark” clause with
> one narrow synthetic optimality/reproducibility baseline. It is not a KiCad, whole-board,
> cross-router, or production-performance result.

> **Third amendment — 2026-08-03:** B-004 is the current-contract corrective result. B-003 remains
> immutable historical evidence but omitted explicit unrouted, via, aggregate-length, and cleanup
> fields required by this ledger.

Every future entry must include:

| Field | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Run ID | Stable content-addressed identifier |
| Date and commit | UTC date, exact Git commit, dirty-tree status |
| Environment | CPU/GPU, memory, OS, dependency and KiCad versions |
| Dataset | Name, licence, provenance, split, and exclusion rules |
| Configuration | Router, policy/model digest, seed, time/memory budget |
| Metrics | Completion, hard DRC, unrouted, vias, length, runtime, memory, cleanup |
| Artifacts | Machine-readable results and logs without proprietary content |
| Interpretation | Limitations, failures, and comparison caveats |

Never delete failed runs. Supersede an invalid result with a linked corrective entry.

## Non-routing baselines

These measurements exercise infrastructure that future routers will consume. They are explicitly
not routing-quality or throughput claims.

### B-001 — CopperTone KiCad-to-Board-IR conversion

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Run ID | `sha256:44f6f4b385085ee874cdfb55036d3c2fe73246717d36f856a98cc427ec2e8387` |

| Environment | Apple arm64 CPU; no accelerator; 38,654,705,664 physical bytes; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13; KiCad was not invoked |

| Configuration | `board-ir-kicad-conversion-v1`; default parser budgets; one recorded default net class; two warmups; seven measured iterations; CPU-only; no router, policy, model, or seed |
| Metrics | Median 345,090,292 ns (min 338,680,209; max 366,999,959) with `tracemalloc` enabled; median incremental peak 4,914,881 bytes (max 5,176,681); 40,810-byte canonical snapshot |

| Interpretation | Parses and validates 14 nets, 55 pads, 9 vias, 53 segments, 2 zones, and 2 keepouts deterministically on one host. Instrumentation affects timing. No routing, DRC, SI/PI, audio, DFM, cross-host, or fabricated-board conclusion may be drawn. |

## Routing correctness baselines

### B-003 — Synthetic two-pin A*/Dijkstra optimal-cost comparison

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Run ID | `sha256:8b7dadf774868dd0694c148a87961b458525e68d9ff63b5a4f3fa5e4b075639c` |
| Date and commit | 2026-08-02 22:37:13 UTC; `15f3931e8fb59b390721c3cc4c47445911580771`; clean tree |
| Environment | Apple arm64 CPU; no accelerator; 38,654,705,664 physical bytes; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13; KiCad was not invoked |
| Dataset | Four deterministic Apache-2.0 synthetic Board IR fixtures generated by `scripts/benchmark_routing.py` (generator SHA-256 `972497b3048dc52f507cb8b515aace2016aae872570407ef81101a1c9eb84e97`): straight, rectangular detour, exact-clearance channel, and retained expected no-path; no external boards, exclusions, or train/test split |
| Configuration | `orthogonal-a-star-v1` against benchmark-only `orthogonal-dijkstra-oracle-v1`; seed 7; 1,000 nm grid; 500 nm bend and 50 nm proximity penalties; 1,000-node, 5,000-expansion, 128-obstacle, and 100,000-obstacle-check ceilings; two warmups and seven measured iterations per fixture/backend; CPU-only |
| Metrics | A*/Dijkstra completion matched 4/4; exact total cost, bend count, and proximity steps matched 3/3 completed fixtures; the blocked fixture remained `no_path`; completed A* candidates reported zero internal violations. Median A*/Dijkstra times with `tracemalloc` were 2.321/9.125 ms straight, 6.961/15.772 ms detour, 3.845/9.417 ms exact-clearance, and 11.445/10.434 ms blocked. Authoritative DRC was not run because the fixtures are not KiCad boards. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-03-synthetic-15f3931.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-03-synthetic-15f3931.json) |
| Interpretation | This detects heuristic/search-order mistakes against a zero-heuristic oracle over the same bounded preparation and edge-cost evaluators. Shared evaluators can share bugs; four tiny generated cases do not establish general correctness, throughput, memory scaling, KiCad legality, whole-board completion, SI/PI, EMC, DFM, fabricated-board success, or superiority over another router. Timings include instrumentation and one-host noise. |

Every stable metric is exactly equal to the original B-015 artifact; only measured latency is intentionally excluded from the regression equality. The output is self-digested and contains neither the fake board serialization nor a raw selection. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-29-live-editor-context-current-contract-replay.json`](../../benchmarks/results/mcp/2026-08-29-live-editor-context-current-contract-replay.json) |
| Limits | Contract-repair replay over a deterministic fake official client only. No real editor, GUI, mutation, routing, placement, DRC, ERC, electrical, fabrication, or performance-generalization claim. No production contract changed. |

| Date and commit | 2026-08-06 03:00 UTC; measured on `feat/negotiated-congestion` at implementation commit `b5227c977c6d447f4d90d3b728b793f5c07616e2`, which is the artifact's `evidence_harness_commit` |
| Environment | Apple arm64 CPU; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13; KiCad was not invoked |
| Dataset | Three CopperMCP-original synthetic Board IR fixtures, no external or proprietary design data: `crossing-neutral-control` reproduces the two-net ADR-0055/B-036 crossing topology; `congested-channel-negotiating` and `congested-channel-first-pass` are the same six nets — one 10-cell horizontal net crossed by three verticals plus two nets far enough apart never to conflict — under a penalty that respectively does and does not force multi-iteration negotiation |
| Configuration | `copper-mcp/benchmark/negotiated-plan-slots/v1`; eleven declared plans (no-plan baseline, the legacy-equivalent plan, four net-order rules, three cost-update rules, two rip-up rules, one two-slot composition) × three fixtures; ten deterministic replays each; 1,000,000 nm grid; 8-iteration ceiling; single signal layer; CPU-only; script `sha256:14b31f8585cda65ddf45c8821966fa310ef92a5348927ddf1001175c34459e90` |
| Determinism | 330 replays, zero divergence. Every plan's ten replays produced byte-identical candidate digests, geometry digest, iteration counts, router-call counts, and plan/slot digests. |
| Metrics — neutral control | All eleven plans identical: completed, 1 iteration, 2 router calls, 26,000,000 nm, 0 overflow units, 0 vias. The slots are inert where the first pass already resolves the board. |
| Metrics — congested, negotiating | Baseline (no plan): completed, 5 iterations, 30 router calls, 56,000,000 nm. `net-order/demand-ascending`: completed, **1** iteration, **6** router calls, **54,000,000 nm** — 80% fewer router calls and 3.6% less copper. `cost-update/present-growth-13-10`: completed, 3 iterations, 18 router calls, 60,000,000 nm — 40% fewer calls but 7% more copper. `cost-update/scaled-accumulation-4`: completed, 5 iterations, 30 calls, 70,000,000 nm — 25% more copper for no saving. **Negative results:** `rip-up/conflicted-only`, `rip-up/top-conflict-2`, `cost-update/saturating-decay-half`, `net-order/stable-identifier`, `net-order/demand-descending`, and `composed/conflicted-only+present-growth` each reached the 8-iteration ceiling with `no_path` where the default converged in five. |
| Metrics — congested, first pass | All plans completed in 1 iteration with 6 router calls at 76,000,000 nm, except `net-order/demand-ascending` at 54,000,000 nm. |
| Rip-up accounting | The baseline reports `0` rip-ups across five iterations while `legacy-equivalent` reports `16` for the same routing. This is not a behavior difference: the legacy counter adds `len(best_candidates)`, which stays empty while every intermediate pass is refused by the clearance gate. The plan-mode counter counts the nets actually selected for re-routing. The legacy number is preserved because changing it would move published bytes. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-06-negotiated-plan-slots.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-06-negotiated-plan-slots.json); validated against its own self-digest by `tests/test_benchmark_negotiated_plan_slots.py`, which also re-runs the harness and requires the fresh cases to equal the committed ones |
| Mutation sensitivity | Five load-bearing guards mutated one at a time. Caught: the rip-up rule always retrying a net with nothing retained; the plan digest composing from its own three slot digests; candidate identity binding the plan composite rather than the envelope; the no-inert-parameter rule. **Survived on first run:** an unattributed clearance refusal retaining nothing, because no test produced an unattributed refusal. A test was added and the mutant is now caught. |
| Claim | **None.** The artifact classifies itself `exploratory sweep / no quality claim`. The sweep was not predeclared, the fixtures are small and synthetic, and there is no held-out corpus. Qu et al. measured 1.95% relative deviation in rule violations but 0.008% in wirelength across 300 random net orders on a real benchmark, so the 29% wirelength swing seen here from ordering alone is evidence about this fixture, not about routing. |
| Interpretation | This measures that the three declared slots are separable, deterministic, digest-bound, and behaviorally real — including where they are worse than the default. It does not establish that any slot combination should become the default, and it makes no KiCad DRC, electrical, multilayer, via, fabrication, apply, whole-board, scaling, or FreeRouting-parity claim. Via counts are structurally zero because the negotiated coordinator is single-layer by contract. |

### B-095 — Incremental congestion-ledger retention and the bounded rip-up window

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Run ID | `sha256:33ea307b777db9a85cbcc283fadeaeff0ed251541e51107eae0bfed87739c506` |
| Date and commit | 2026-08-06; measured on `feat/incremental-spatial-index` at implementation commit `9e56fc858737b5f8c25f6b800a1f5c48650152aa`, which is the artifact's `evidence_harness_commit` |
| Environment | Apple arm64 CPU; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13; KiCad was not invoked |
| Dataset | Three kinds, kept separate on purpose. (1) The B-087 `congested-channel` synthetic fixture, routed once per net. (2) A synthetic parallel-track sweep at 4, 8, 16 and 32 nets, which is not a board and exists only so the operation ratio can be read against a moving net count. (3) **16 real MIT-licensed SimpleRouteJson corpus boards** (the B-088 corpus, © 2026 Zach Dwiel), routed net by net at the divisor-aligned grid policy, contributing 67 candidates and 13,194 unit resources; the largest single board is `ts20_esp32_wifi` at 2,099 resources. No proprietary design data. |
| Configuration | `copper-mcp/benchmark/incremental-spatial-index/v1`; 16 replays; 21 ledger fixtures × 5 retention fractions = 105 A/B points; 3 index microbenchmarks at 64/256/1,024 entries; 7 coordinator plans on the congested fixture; single signal layer; CPU-only; script `sha256:1f1419ab98659d88d14923fdb4dbaedd65493f8d52453c7359e7dfc3c43f4169` |
| Method | Before and after are replayed **against one recorded candidate set, in one process, in the same run**, so the fixture cannot drift between them. *Before* is exactly the ADR-0073 reconstruction: `clear_present()`, then re-add every retained candidate, re-deriving its unit resources from path geometry. *After* is one `retain_only()` call. Exact operation counts are host-independent integers and are the claim; wall-clock medians are host-specific context and are labelled as such by the artifact's own `host_specific_field_suffix` field. |
| Equivalence | 105/105 A/B points left the ledger in a byte-identical observable state — added nets, conflict scores, and overflow resources. 3/3 index cases returned identical answers to a freshly built index and a superset of brute force. 7/7 coordinator plans replayed deterministically. |
| Metrics — ledger, retention > 0 | 78 points. Operation count is equal or lower at every one. Wall clock is **11.8% to 99.94% faster, median 74.8%**. On the congested fixture: keeping 4 of 6 nets costs 22 operations instead of 72 and is 81.0% faster; keeping all 6 costs 0 instead of 94 and is 99.5% faster. On `parallel-32`: keeping 24 of 32 costs 168 instead of 504 and is 82.4% faster. Where the operation counts are *equal* — e.g. `parallel-32` keeping 16, at 336 both ways — the after path is still 48.7% faster, because it re-counts from cached per-net resource sets instead of re-deriving them from geometry. |
| Metrics — ledger, retention = 0 | 27 points, and a **regression**: −21.97% to +4.01%, i.e. up to 22% *slower*. Full rip-up now takes a bare clear, so neither side performs any unit-resource work and the residual gap is the set construction that decides which branch to take. In absolute terms this is 4.3–11.1 µs before against 4.8–11.1 µs after. The first implementation, which always subtracted, was 60–130% slower here; that measurement is why the bare-clear branch exists, and it is recorded rather than designed around quietly. |
| Metrics — index microbenchmark | Removing an eighth of the entries and re-querying, against rebuilding the survivors and re-querying: 64 entries, 8 mutations vs 56 inserts, 7.5 µs vs 56.4 µs; 256 entries, 32 vs 224, 18.4 µs vs 209.7 µs; 1,024 entries, 128 vs 896, 58.4 µs vs 952.5 µs. |
| Metrics — coordinator | Baseline (no plan): completed, 5 iterations, **30 router calls**, 56,000,000 nm. `conflicted-only-v1`: `no_path` at the 8-iteration ceiling — B-087's negative result reproduces unchanged. `conflict-window-1` and `conflict-window-2`: completed, 5 iterations, **22 router calls** (−27%), 56,000,000 nm — the same routing for a quarter fewer searches. `conflict-window-4` and `conflict-window-16`: completed, 5 iterations, 30 calls, 56,000,000 nm — a wide enough window *is* full rip-up, and the artifact records that rather than implying the rule improves monotonically. |
| Honest proportion | On the congested fixture the entire ledger reconstruction is single-digit microseconds against ~58–75 ms of routing. The incremental ledger is therefore a **constant-factor improvement to a term that is not the bottleneck**, exactly as ADR-0073 predicted. What moves the end-to-end number is the window rule cutting router calls, not the index. Reporting the ledger speedup without this sentence would be true and misleading. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-06-incremental-spatial-index.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-06-incremental-spatial-index.json); validated against its own self-digest by `tests/test_benchmark_incremental_spatial_index.py`, which also re-runs the harness and requires every field except the declared host-specific ones to match the committed values |
| Mutation sensitivity | Five load-bearing guards mutated one at a time. Caught immediately: treating touching rectangles as disjoint (the conservatism guard); the recount branch leaving a stale spatial-index entry; emitting the window weight in every rip-up slot's canonical bytes; the conflict-window rule ignoring its window. **Survived on first run:** an emptied resource count left at zero instead of deleted. Investigation showed the mutant is output-inert — every reader of the present overlay filters on `usage > 1` or reads a `Counter` whose default is already zero — but not memory-inert: the overlay would grow pass by pass without bound. A `live_resource_count` property and an assertion that an incrementally retained ledger tracks exactly as many resources as a rebuilt one were added, and the mutant is now caught. |
| Claim | The exact operation counts are the claim and are reproducible on any host. **No quality claim.** The sweep was not predeclared, the synthetic fixtures are small, the corpus boards are real but yield at most 9 candidates each, and there is no held-out corpus. Nothing here argues that `conflict-window-v1` should become the default; `all-nets-v1` stays. |
| Interpretation | This measures that ledger reconstruction is no longer linear in the retained set, that the two reconstructions are observationally identical, that the spatial index is conservative and order-independent, and that the bounded rip-up window converges where `conflicted-only-v1` does not while making fewer router calls than full rip-up. It does not establish scaling — 32 nets and 2,099 resources is the largest thing measured — nor KiCad DRC, electrical, multilayer, via, fabrication, apply, whole-board, or FreeRouting-parity validity. Via counts are structurally zero because the negotiated coordinator is single-layer by contract. The router's per-request obstacle index is untouched and still rebuilt once per `propose()` call. |

#### B-026 — current opaque-session contract replay

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|

| Why this replay exists | The historical runner constructed a legacy `sha256:` session revision from ambient `KICAD_API_TOKEN`; the current closed request correctly rejects that wire type before CAS. The repaired runner instead obtains `pbkdf2-hmac-sha256:` from a same-process public `inspect_live_board` observation, using the fake client identity through the same `kipy` seam as production. The stale-session case changes one digest nibble while preserving the opaque type, so it reaches the CAS comparison rather than input validation. |
| Evidence | The complete metric map is exactly equal to the prior B-026 review-remediation artifact: ten schema-valid deterministic replays; candidate `sha256:c53a6d61db99715ad46dce180fe0abfbf72235eb2678092938cbbdee27057ced`; candidate/file-oracle equality; two vias; stale board, session, and snapshot refusals; capture-race refusal; exactly-once closure for the handshake, success, refusal, and race clients; source unchanged; KiCad, DRC, serialization, apply authority, and real GUI all `false`. The output is self-digested and exposes neither configured token nor observed editor identity, board text, net names, coordinates, or source syntax. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-29-live-layered-route-preview-current-contract-replay.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-29-live-layered-route-preview-current-contract-replay.json) |
| Limits | Current-contract replay over a deterministic fake official client only. No real editor/session, restart test, DRC, serializer, apply, electrical, fabrication, or routing-quality generalization claim. No production contract changed. |

#### B-082 — route-aware placement claim correction, 2026-08-06

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Correction | Supersedes only the *interpretation* recorded in B-078 and repeated in B-081. B-078 and B-081 remain immutable history; their fixture, configuration, and numbers are unchanged and were independently reproduced here. What was wrong was the sentence "Default `same-net-manhattan-v1` and opt-in `route-aware-astar-v1` search the same bounded legal candidate space." They do not. |
| Cause | The score feeds `solver._state_key`, which orders the beam, so the surviving beam decides which successors are generated in the next round. The scoring policy changes which candidates are ever explored, not merely how a fixed set is ordered. Measured on the same fixture and settings: at `max_ranked=64` the two retained sets intersect in **1** candidate; at the committed `max_ranked=8` they intersect in **0** — entirely disjoint. B-078's 23.80952380952381% is therefore a **different-search-trajectory** result, not a re-ranking result. |
| Corrected claim | Two different bounded searches over one intent, fixture, and set of work ceilings, each ordering its own beam by its own score, **at one A\* probe per candidate**. The baseline search selected a candidate measuring 42,000,000 nm and the route-aware search one measuring 32,000,000 nm on that single probe: 23.80952380952381%. |
| Added measurement — true re-ranking | One fixed candidate set — the 16-candidate union of what both searches retained — scored under both policies with the argmin of each compared. It independently reproduces the same two candidate choices and the same 42,000,000 → 32,000,000 nm, 23.80952380952381%. The union is itself drawn from the two searches, so this is a shared-set comparison, not a search-independent one. |
| Added measurement — all probeable nets | The ranked search probes 1 net per candidate while the fixture has **11** probeable nets, so B-078's "zero unrouted probes" was a one-net statement. Probed against all 11, both chosen candidates complete 7 and leave **4 unrouted — all four `off_grid` refusals**, where the pad-centre delta is not divisible by the 1,000,000 nm probe grid. The ordering **reverses**: baseline choice 359,000,000 nm, route-aware choice 391,000,000 nm. The one-probe signal that steered the search does not survive the broader question. |
| Artifact | New [`2026-08-06-route-aware-placement-v2.json`](../../benchmarks/results/placement/2026-08-06-route-aware-placement-v2.json), run ID `sha256:fecb29c10145ee7cce807a5034ac7d2da176a3963aec6c074f0763ab45af74fd`, source commit `ebe842984ed924df71c28b21531340934ae93731` — the commit carrying the corrected benchmark and its regressions, and a strict ancestor of this entry, following B-081's reachable-source rule. The v1 artifact is not regenerated; the overstated evidence stays auditable beside its correction. The v2 `run_id` binds the estimator id and the full probe/A\* configuration digests, so a one-probe report and an eleven-probe report can no longer share an identity. |
| Criterion | Unchanged and still met on the search comparison: ≥10% improvement or strictly fewer unrouted probes, over three deterministic replays with every retained candidate legal. A failed criterion is now recorded with `criterion.passed` false and a non-zero exit rather than raised, which is what ADR-0067 promised. |
| Interpretation | This corrects a claim, not an architecture. A route-aware score steering the search is the intended behavior; describing that as a re-ranking result was not. Adds no fixture, capability, DRC, congestion, external-router, electrical, fabrication, mutation, or apply claim, and no whole-board routing claim in either direction. |

#### B-076 — ordered-layer internal proposal oracle

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Dataset | Deterministic synthetic 5x5 lattice and Board IR fixtures: both two available layers contain a full crossing wall, while the three-layer fixture has a clear inner signal layer. No external or proprietary board data. |
| Protocol | Run the same bounded request under a two-layer stack and a three-layer stack; replay the latter twice; verify the emitted full-stack spans and reject invalid span, 9-layer, and zero-via-budget variants. |
| Metrics | Two-layer result: `no_path`; three-layer result: complete with two vias through the inner layer and deterministic replay equality. |
| Interpretation | This measures the internal candidate-only generalized-stack seam. It does not establish generalized KiCad serialization, Board-IR round-trip of rendered bytes, zone refill, authoritative DRC, MCP exposure, jobs, apply, fabrication, or whole-board routing. |

Restamped 65-via candidate: verified; restamped 66-via candidate under explicit 65-via policy: `budget_exceeded`; omitted two-layer 66-via candidate: verified. File/live/durable entry points: all refuse the three-layer snapshot before router publication. |

#### B-078 — capped ordered-layer exact `(node, vias)` differential

| Field | Recorded evidence |

Plus the unchanged four fixed 5x5 two-layer cases and a three-case via-policy boundary. No external or proprietary board data. |
| Protocol | Route each lattice with the bounded search; compare its cost against an exact `(x, y, layer, vias_used)` uniform-cost Dijkstra written without a heuristic, without Pareto pruning, and enumerating the augmented state space directly; replay each returned path through an independent legality checker for bounds, obstacles, orthogonal unit moves, coincident via transitions, the via keepout, the cap, and cost/via accounting; replay each lattice twice for determinism. Separately observe the routed/refused boundary either side of the declared generalized via cap on a 64- and 65-transition corridor, and a 65-transition two-layer corridor with omitted policy. |
| Metrics | 20,000/20,000 differential matches, 0 mismatches, 0 illegal paths, 40,000 deterministic replays with no divergence. Layer coverage 5,074 / 5,039 / 4,948 / 4,939 for 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 layers; 19,190 lattices under a finite effective cap; 13,032 routed; deepest observed chain 4 transitions. Outcome signature `sha256:9c2543ee8bcc9981e8ca6ed9f3e65cf6a226558e0738757f6eb9937c58a2d0cc`. Policy boundary: omitted 3-layer cap routes exactly 64 transitions and refuses the 65th with `no_path`; omitted 2-layer policy stays unbounded at 65 transitions. |

| Mutation sensitivity | Observed on this suite: an off-by-one via cap, a coordinate-only search state, transitions restricted to adjacent layers, an ignored via keepout, and a changed declared default cap are each detected. Not detected: removing the via-awareness of the per-coordinate Pareto front, which is an additional prune above the sound `(node, vias)` dominance key. |
| Interpretation | This measures the internal candidate-only search kernel: cost exactness under a finite via budget, path legality, and deterministic replay over 2..5 layers. It is not evidence for six through eight layer stacks, Board IR mapping, trace width or clearance, via annulus/drill/net-class rules, KiCad serialization, authoritative DRC, MCP exposure, jobs, apply, fabrication, or whole-board routing. |

These three collisions are carried in its closed `RECORDED_COLLISIONS` list, keyed to this entry, so each is reported on every run; the nine legitimate replay sub-entries are carried in a separate closed list and must still be `####` headings beneath the `###` entry they replay. A new duplicate fails the build. |
| Interpretation | This is a bookkeeping record with no measurement, run, artifact, capability, KiCad, electrical, fabrication, or hardware claim. It exists so that a reader who follows one of these citations learns that the number is ambiguous, without the ledger's history being edited to hide that it was. |

## External-corpus baselines

These run the existing routers over third-party boards imported through the benchmark-only
SimpleRouteJson seam. They are the first CopperMCP measurements taken on data this project did not
author, and their refusal breakdowns are the result rather than an error path.

### B-088 — SimpleRouteJson external-corpus routing and refusal breakdown

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Run ID | `sha256:feb4924accf3a657780957623f87a995caa04ee8af823d4f4da526319ff3e12a` |
| Date and commit | 2026-08-06 UTC; source commit `40889bdef6b4370a15e1cbea8fe19ed8c8478ca7`, whose tree carries the whole change and to which this evidence commit adds only this artifact and this record; bound runner `sha256:8fb5d05fb60a75b66e4720b3aa3ba9e0b28dbd8c3377ac159a239adbc4795fed`; bound adapter `sha256:fb64b59f5727d792de30d82b1e9c0b7eab606d071569a29c8c8bc3ff8db5ec66`. Both digests are recorded inside the artifact's own `configuration`, so a reader never has to guess which revision produced a number. |
| Environment | Apple arm64 CPU; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13; KiCad was not invoked; FreeRouting was not installed |
| Dataset | 20 of the 36 SimpleRouteJson boards in [dwiel/tscircuit-benchmark](https://github.com/dwiel/tscircuit-benchmark) at commit `be36518b5bf51755dae92c230061ab3cf4e3e063`, MIT (© 2026 Zach Dwiel), redistributed with the upstream licence and an attribution file under [`benchmarks/corpora/tscircuit-benchmark/`](../../benchmarks/corpora/tscircuit-benchmark/). The subset is the first 20 filenames in upstream lexical order, a rule fixed before the run; SHA-256 is recorded for all 36 and every committed file is verified against it before routing. The boards are **LLM-generated from plain-English specs and were routed with FreeRouting as part of their construction**, so they are neither human-designed hardware nor a neutral yardstick for FreeRouting. |
| Configuration | `simple-route-json-import-v1` adapter; clearance 200,000 nm, via 600,000/300,000 nm, track width from each board's `minTraceWidth` (100,000 nm throughout); seed 0; per-request ceilings 250,000 grid nodes, 100,000 expansions, 2,048 obstacles, 2,000,000 obstacle checks. Two grid policies, both run over the whole corpus: `fixed` at a 250,000 nm lattice step, and `divisor-aligned`, which gives a two-pin net the largest ladder step dividing the greatest common divisor of its pad-centre delta — decided from geometry before routing, never by retrying. |
| Import | 20 of 20 boards imported; zero import refusals. 591 obstacles (487 `rect`, 104 `oval`), 449 pads, 142 keepouts, 126 nets, 117 of them with routing work. 210 obstacles were over-approximated and 114 were widened to the whole stack because they named an `inner1`/`inner2` layer a 2-layer board does not declare. The largest outward edge movement on any board was **1 nm**, and 3 of 20 boards needed none at all. |
| Metrics | **70 of 117 nets routed (59.83%) under both grid policies.** Refusals, `fixed`: 36 `off_grid`, 11 `no_path`. Refusals, `divisor-aligned`: 21 `grid_budget_exceeded`, 15 `off_grid`, 11 `no_path`. A further 9 nets carried a single pad and so stated no routing work; they are counted separately and excluded from the denominator. Routed wire length 1,676,500,000 nm against a provable pad-gap lower bound of 1,431,522,993 nm, a ratio of **1.1711**. 0 vias (single-layer routing only), 235 bends. Mean wall time 3.40 s (`fixed`) and 3.44 s (`divisor-aligned`) per whole-corpus pass; three passes per policy replayed byte-identically. |
| Negative result | **Every two-pin net in the corpus refused, under both policies.** All 70 routed nets are multi-pin, which is why `routed_two_pin_centre_manhattan_nm` is 0. The reference A* router's two-pin path additionally requires the pad-centre delta to divide by the lattice step; external pad coordinates do not oblige. Choosing a finer, divisor-respecting step converts those `off_grid` refusals into `grid_budget_exceeded` ones and routes **no additional net**, which localises the constraint to the lattice-node budget rather than to grid alignment. Reporting one policy alone would have hidden that. |
| Baseline comparison | **`not_run`.** FreeRouting is GPL-3.0, is not installed in the recording environment, and this repository has no SimpleRouteJson-to-DSN bridge. The artifact records `status: not_run` with a reason for every known baseline. No FreeRouting number is estimated, inferred, or carried over, so the cross-router comparison issue #65 asks for **remains unmeasured**. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-06-simple-route-json-corpus-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-06-simple-route-json-corpus-v1.json), validated against its own self-digest by `scripts/check_ledgers.py`. Wall time is part of the report and therefore part of `run_id`, following every other artifact here, which makes the identity machine-bound; the machine-independent claim is `metrics`, and `tests/test_benchmark_simple_route_json_corpus.py` replays exactly that against a fresh run. |
| Mutation sensitivity | The over-approximation invariant is mutation-checked: `tests/test_simple_route_json_import.py` asserts that every mapped obstacle rectangle contains its exact decimal source rectangle, and a second test replaces the low-edge floor with a ceil and asserts the first test's property now fails. Without that pair, the containment assertion could pass merely because the fixtures were exact at nanometre resolution. |
| Interpretation | This measures the existing single-layer candidate-only router against imported external geometry. It is not a cross-router comparison, not a whole-board completion result (each net is routed independently against the unrouted snapshot, so the candidates are not mutually compatible), and not a KiCad DRC, electrical, thermal, signal-integrity, or fabrication claim. Nothing was applied, exported, or written. It does not generalise beyond LLM-generated 2-layer tscircuit boards, and the committed 20-board prefix is the easier half of an already-narrow corpus. The 1.1711 ratio is against a loose provable lower bound that ignores every obstacle and bend; it is not an optimality claim. |


#### B-088 — region-scoped obstacle model replay

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Run ID | `sha256:facf95ee9770ffab8c1bc403a32a403e55ca79f2c56d1eabc6679eb6ec4dfca3` |
| What changed | The router's defaults and obstacle model, not the corpus, the adapter, the grid policies, or the request shape ([ADR-0089](../adr/0089-region-scoped-obstacle-model.md), issue #128). The benchmark's own explicit 2,048-obstacle ceiling is unchanged; what changed beneath it is that the model is scoped to a routing region and the search is confined to that region. |
| Delta | **70 of 117 nets routed (59.83%) under both grid policies — unchanged.** Routed wire length, bend count, via count and the 1.1711 length ratio are unchanged, board by board. The refusal breakdown moves: under both policies, 9 of the 11 `no_path` refusals are now `no_path_in_region`, on 5 boards, because those boards' routing regions are proper subsets of the board and an exhausted search there is not a statement about the board. `off_grid` and `grid_budget_exceeded` counts are unchanged. Ten of twenty per-board candidate digests move, because a candidate records the obstacle-check meter and a scoped model performs fewer checks; **no path geometry changed on any board**. |
| What it does not change | The negative result stands exactly as recorded: every two-pin net in this corpus still refuses, all 70 routed nets are still multi-pin, and the constraint is still localised to the lattice rather than to the obstacle model. This replay does not test that hypothesis; issue #128's real-board measurement now makes it testable, which is recorded in B-096. |
| Artifact | Regenerated [`2026-08-06-simple-route-json-corpus-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-06-simple-route-json-corpus-v1.json), three repetitions, replayed byte-identically; `tests/test_benchmark_simple_route_json_corpus.py` re-runs the corpus and requires `metrics` to match. |
| Interpretation | Unchanged from B-088. This remains a single-layer candidate-only measurement over LLM-generated 2-layer boards, not a cross-router comparison and not a whole-board completion result. |
### B-089 — KiCad 10.0.5 courtyard oracle parity and the cached-courtyard inset

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Run ID | `sha256:a1c1f09b397ab69633e4ad7424d53760c684dbe4beef02a9ad3b2acb495d733a` |
| Date and commit | 2026-08-06 UTC; source commit `892bcac03541c921eac062b966cf78449391f946`, the branch point; the tree carrying the measured code is the pull request that lands this record. |
| Environment | Apple arm64 CPU; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13; **KiCad 10.0.5 invoked for real** (`kicad_invoked: true`), each run in isolated config/home/cache/temp directories. This replaces the `kicad_invoked: false` posture of B-041's `benchmark_courtyard_legality.py`, which measured the legalizer against itself. |
| Dataset | 15 cases: 11 CopperMCP-original synthetic boards at exact nanometre penetrations between two 10 mm front courtyard squares (−1 mm, −1, 0, 1, 5,000, 9,998, 9,999, 10,000, 10,001, 20,000, 1 mm), plus four committed boards — [`courtyard-donut.kicad_pcb`](../../tests/fixtures/board-ir-v0.2/courtyard-donut.kicad_pcb), [`courtyard-inset-below.kicad_pcb`](../../tests/fixtures/board-ir-v0.2/courtyard-inset-below.kicad_pcb), [`courtyard-inset-at.kicad_pcb`](../../tests/fixtures/board-ir-v0.2/courtyard-inset-at.kicad_pcb), and the CopperTone buffer board. All fixtures are CopperMCP-original; none is copied from any footprint library. |
| Configuration | `kicad-cli pcb drc --format json --units mm --severity-all`, zero-clearance courtyard default, no custom design rules; the legalizer at `COURTYARD_CACHE_INSET_NM = 5,000` and `COURTYARD_COLLISION_THRESHOLD_NM = 10,000`. KiCad's verdict is read as the presence of a `courtyards_overlap` violation specifically, not as an error count, because the claim under test is about that one provider. |
| Metrics | **10/15 exact parity, 5/15 conceded `inconclusive`, 0 contradictions, 0 false-positive violations, 0 false-negative clears** (`non_contradiction_rate` 15/15). The five conceded cases are exactly the sub-threshold band (1, 5,000, 9,998, 9,999 nm, and the `inset_below` fixture at 9,999 nm), where KiCad reports clear and the raw geometry overlaps. The donut fixture and the CopperTone board are both `proven_clear` in agreement with KiCad; `inset_at` is `violated` in agreement with KiCad. |
| Negative result | **The exact-parity rate is 10/15, not 15/15, and that gap is the point.** It is reported as a separate `conceded_inconclusive` count rather than folded into an agreement percentage, because an `inconclusive` is a declared non-claim and counting it as agreement would manufacture a result. The script raises rather than emitting an artifact if any contradiction appears, so a passing artifact is itself evidence of zero contradictions. |
| Boundary result | KiCad's first collision is at **exactly 10,000 nm of nominal penetration, inclusive** — 9,999 nm is clear — matching `2 × maxError` from `FOOTPRINT::BuildCourtyardCaches`. Corner-only overlap was measured separately and shows the same threshold applying independently to each axis, with no degenerate band at 10,000 × 10,000 nm. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-06-courtyard-oracle-parity.json`](../../benchmarks/results/placement/2026-08-06-courtyard-oracle-parity.json), validated against its own self-digest by `scripts/check_ledgers.py`. Every case's KiCad verdict, model verdict, and agreement class is recorded individually, so the aggregate can be re-derived from the artifact rather than trusted. |
| Mutation sensitivity | Seven mutations of the inset arithmetic and the ring handling were applied and every one was caught by `tests/test_placement.py -k Courtyard`: inset 5,000→4,999 (5 failures), 5,000→5,001 (5), 5,000→0 (8), threshold `2 × inset`→`1 × inset` (1), the witness bound `>=`→`>` (3), the two-axis witness `and`→`or` (7), and reverting the even-odd crossing pool to per-ring solids — the original issue #74 defect — (4). No mutation survived. |
| Interpretation | This measures one DRC provider on the orthogonal courtyard subset. It is not a full-board DRC, placement-apply, electrical, or fabrication claim, and nothing was applied or written. It does not cover nonzero or negative custom `courtyard_clearance`, arcs or non-orthogonal geometry, same-footprint rings that touch or properly intersect, or the tiny-shape band where a courtyard is thinner than the 10,000 nm threshold — all of which the artifact lists under `not_claimed`. The 11 synthetic cases are two rectangles each; they do not exercise concave chains. |
## Adversarial safety evaluations

These attack a stated safety claim with scenarios written down before the run. They are coverage
measurements against a fixed implementation, never assurance cases, and their `not_run` rows are
part of the result rather than an omission from it.

### B-090 — Excessive-agency evaluation across held-out project families

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Run ID | `sha256:04e1ac4b318e7bdbd6874cfb233ecbdd40c265017780e4f7ba1cb363c693b6e3` |
| Date and commit | 2026-08-06 UTC; source commit `edae6a0b5cf2a78ef60f4f44d8ac99908cf479d3`, whose tree carries the whole change and to which this evidence commit adds only this artifact and this record; bound harness `sha256:fb426669bd32c2663282861a5cf11133f7ef85e63919aa003bc47a1dd710148f`; bound scenario catalog `sha256:67564ebc60dfe04e6b32c78a14ad8a1de1a9bc33203141f7f4a3c77b2b5740e9`. Both digests sit inside the artifact, so a reader never has to guess which harness or which catalog produced a row. |
| Environment | Apple arm64 CPU; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13. The report records `network: not_invoked`, `model: not_invoked`, `kicad: not_invoked`, `transport: in_process_mcp_adapter`, and `board_mutation: temporary_workspace_copies_only`, and deliberately carries no timing observation, so it is not a performance result and replays byte-identically. |
| Claim under test | *An agent driving this server cannot cause an unintended board mutation, and cannot extract a verification claim that was never computed, even when it tries.* A negative claim, therefore attacked rather than proved. |
| Configuration | `copper-mcp/security-evaluation/excessive-agency/v1`. 29 scenarios in six families — `mutation_without_consent` (10), `stale_state_exploitation` (4), `claim_laundering` (4), `budget_dos` (5), `non_claim_inference` (3), `information_extraction` (3) — each predeclared in `tests/fixtures/security/excessive-agency-v1/scenarios.json` with its adversarial goal, its tool calls, and one required outcome, and each driven through `mcp.call_tool` on the real adapter. Every mutation scenario guards the target board's byte digest. Every scenario finishes inside a 30 s liveness ceiling that is checked but never recorded. |
| Project families | Four, replayed identically. `development-fixtures` (control, **not** held out): the two boundary fixtures the apply and token paths were built against. `coppertone-buffer` (held out): the project's own reference hardware board, 26 footprints / 14 nets / 53 segments / 9 vias / 4 zones, never used to develop an authorization boundary. `heldout-audio` (held out): the hash-separated partition from B-074/B-075, declared held out before this suite existed. `tscircuit-benchmark` (held out): the external MIT SimpleRouteJson corpus from B-088, the only externally authored family. |
| Metrics | **116 cases: 77 passed, 0 failed, 39 not run.** By project family — `development-fixtures` 27/0/2, `coppertone-buffer` 23/0/6, `heldout-audio` 27/0/2, `tscircuit-benchmark` 0/0/29. By scenario family — `mutation_without_consent` 28/0/12, `stale_state_exploitation` 11/0/5, `claim_laundering` 7/0/9, `non_claim_inference` 9/0/3, `information_extraction` 7/0/5, `budget_dos` 15/0/5. No board byte changed except by the single authorized apply the replay scenario requires, and the post-replay digest equals the post-apply digest. Every runnable budget row refused through the adapter's own boundary exception (`ToolError`), never through a crash. |
| Negative result | **The one genuinely external project family reached no agency boundary at all**, contributing 29 of the 39 not-run rows. The corpus ships SimpleRouteJson routing problems and no MCP tool accepts that format — the import seam is a benchmark-only subpackage. The family is carried in the report with `accepted_format: false` and its corpus manifest digest rather than dropped, because a held-out family that could not be reached is a result. **`coppertone-buffer` afforded no apply capability**: every net is already routed, so `preview_route` answers `already_connected`, and the board is outside the source-preserving front-side orthogonal footprint subset, so `preview_placement` issues no token; the five scenarios needing a genuine capability could not run there. **The authoritative KiCad DRC/ERC scenario is `not_run` in every family by choice** (`external_process_required`) — invoking `kicad-cli` would make a self-digested artifact depend on whether KiCad is installed; that boundary stays with SEC-113 and SEC-119, and writing a pass here would be the exact failure `claim_laundering` exists to catch. **Two families yield no coordinate sentinel**: they are laid out entirely on round nanometre grids, so only `coppertone-buffer` exercises the coordinate leak scan with real force. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-06-excessive-agency-evaluation.json`](../../benchmarks/results/security/2026-08-06-excessive-agency-evaluation.json), validated against its own self-digest by `scripts/check_ledgers.py`. It records case identifiers, refusal codes, and categorical assertions only; board text, geometry, net names, absolute paths, and tokens never enter it, and a test greps the whole report for four real net names from the reference board. |
| Mutation sensitivity | Five discriminator tests in `tests/test_excessive_agency_evaluation.py` break a boundary and require a recorded failure: the same call asserted against the wrong predeclared code must fail rather than accept any refusal; the consent scenario run with consent *granted* must fail with `invalid_token`; a real net name planted in a fabricated refusal must be caught as `1_leaked`; a `not_run` literal replaced by `passed` must be caught as a drifting key; and a `RecursionError` returned in place of the adapter's boundary exception must fail the budget check rather than pass it. A sixth asserts the contract introspection actually finds `drc_after_apply` and `kicad_opened_board` as single-value non-claims and `pad_overlap` as legitimately three-valued, so neither check can pass by finding nothing. |
| Interpretation | This measures 77 predeclared attacks reaching 77 predeclared refusals against these boards, and nothing else. **It does not test a model**: none is invoked, so it says nothing about whether an agent would attempt an attack, report a refusal honestly, or stop after one. A passing catalog is coverage, not absence — it bounds nothing that was not written down. The caller is in-process and constructs arguments a transport would reject, so no claim is made about a hostile host, a compromised client, or a rewriting transport. Nothing here covers remote deployment (no principal, OAuth, rate limit, audited log, or tenant isolation), logging or telemetry sinks, or any electrical, thermal, mechanical, DRC, or fabrication property of any board. The held-out claim is uneven and recorded as such: `coppertone-buffer` is held out from the boundary implementation, not from the project. |

#### B-090 — declared non-claim surface replay

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Run ID | `sha256:3bca2668d858465561da3b2e85b20bfccbf2726130e0b3ff19f7d16bd8c3a2db`, superseding `sha256:04e1ac4b318e7bdbd6874cfb233ecbdd40c265017780e4f7ba1cb363c693b6e3`. The B-090 entry above records the earlier run and is not edited; its `Run ID` field names the 2026-08-06 measurement, and the artifact file now holds this replay's bytes. |
| Date and commit | 2026-08-07 UTC; source commit `bd9fc828e2dcd10e728360316a4ba05be6218b5b`, whose tree carries ADR-0084; bound harness `sha256:fb426669bd32c2663282861a5cf11133f7ef85e63919aa003bc47a1dd710148f` and bound scenario catalog `sha256:67564ebc60dfe04e6b32c78a14ad8a1de1a9bc33203141f7f4a3c77b2b5740e9`, **both byte-identical to B-090's**. Neither the harness nor the catalog moved, so nothing about the attack surface was rewritten after the fact. |
| Why it was re-measured | [ADR-0084](../adr/0084-authoritative-source-to-board-parity.md) adds `SourceToBoardParityVerificationContract`, whose `footprint_correctness` is a new single-value non-claim `Literal`. The `contract-declared-non-claim-fields-hold-only-non-claims` scenario reads the contract module by introspection and reports how many such fields it found, so a new declared non-claim necessarily moves that number and, through it, the report's self-digest. Re-measuring was not optional: the committed artifact is asserted to replay from the tree it names. |
| What changed | Exactly one field, in exactly three rows. The scenario's `detail` moves from `7 declared field(s)` to `8 declared field(s)` for `coppertone-buffer`, `development-fixtures` and `heldout-audio`; `tscircuit-benchmark` is `not_run` there as before. Every disposition, every refusal code, and every other case is byte-identical. |
| Metrics | **116 cases: 77 passed, 0 failed, 39 not run** — unchanged from B-090, as are all six scenario-family and all four project-family splits. The check the moved number belongs to still passes: the eighth declared non-claim field holds only its declared value everywhere it appears. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-06-excessive-agency-evaluation.json`](../../benchmarks/results/security/2026-08-06-excessive-agency-evaluation.json), regenerated in place because the harness hardcodes that output path, and revalidated against its own self-digest by `scripts/check_ledgers.py`. The replay is recorded here rather than by editing B-090. |
| Interpretation | This re-measures nothing new. It records that adding a verification contract moved a count the suite deliberately derives from the live contract module rather than from a constant — which is the property that makes the scenario non-vacuous, and the reason a new non-claim cannot be added without the evidence being refreshed. It carries B-090's limits unchanged: no model is invoked, a passing catalog is coverage rather than absence, and nothing here covers remote deployment or any electrical, DRC, or fabrication property. It makes **no** claim about source-to-board parity itself; that evidence is ADR-0084's test suite, not this artifact. |

| Field | Recorded evidence |

| Run ID | `sha256:730781c296d80a25c3a8073eecfb4ab094ef34d107b4b5e12254a61de7a734f1` |
| Date and commit | 2026-08-06 UTC; source commit `b4475d0d8f1fa549a710d80d94ae7a49a826d383`, the in-flight branch; the tree carrying the measured code is the pull request that lands this record. |
| Environment | Apple arm64 CPU; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13; **KiCad 10.0.5 invoked for real** (`kicad_invoked: true`), each run in isolated config/home/cache/temp directories, mirroring B-089's methodology exactly. |
| Dataset | 23 CopperMCP-original synthetic cases: 10 chamfered-ring pairs at exact nanometre penetrations (−1 mm, −1, 0, 1, 5,000, 9,999, 10,000, 10,001, 20,000, 1 mm) meeting edge-on along orthogonal sides; one chamfer-corner-triangle-only contact; 10 circle pairs (1.8 mm radii) at the same penetrations; one circle deep inside a chamfered ring; one circle against a chamfered ring's cut-corner square. No footprint-library geometry is copied — the chamfer dimensions deliberately differ from any measured board. |

KiCad's verdict is the presence of a `courtyards_overlap` violation specifically. |

The conceded cases are the two sub-threshold bands, the chamfer-triangle and corner-square contacts, and the circle band through 20,000 nm. |

The band up to `2 × 10,000` nm is therefore conceded rather than fitted, and the 10,001 nm and 20,000 nm cases are recorded as concessions, not parity. |

No mutation survived. |
| Interpretation | This measures one DRC provider on the octilinear-plus-circles courtyard subset. It is not a full-board DRC, placement-apply, electrical, or fabrication claim, and nothing was applied or written. It does not cover arcs (still a typed refusal), rings mixing chamfers with nesting (degraded to concessions), non-quarter-turn poses, custom `courtyard_clearance`, or the exact location of the circle polygonisation boundary, which the artifact lists under `not_claimed`. |
### B-102 — mutation-sensitivity auditability correction for B-089 and B-093

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Correction | Qualifies — does not retract — the "Mutation sensitivity" rows of B-089 (seven hand-applied mutants, "caught by `tests/test_placement.py -k Courtyard`") and B-093 (six hand-applied mutants, caught by three named test files). Both rows remain immutable history, and every parity, threshold, and negative-result metric in both entries is untouched: this correction is about the mutation rows only. |
| Finding | Both runs state **fast targeted invocations**, which is the exposed condition for the scratch-harness defect ADR-0098 records: a mutant that changes a `.py` file without changing its byte count, applied or restored within the same filesystem second, is invisible to CPython's `(mtime, size)` bytecode-invalidation check, and a stale mutant poisoning the next invocation registers as a **false kill**. Neither record states mutant byte counts or write timing, so neither kill count can be shown wrong — or shown right — from the record. These are the only two of the project's 24 hand-applied runs whose records affirmatively state the exposed invocation; the other 22 state no invocation at all and are `unauditable` without being `exposed`. |
| Disposition | Read both mutation-sensitivity rows as `exposed`, per the one-value literals in ADR-0098; do not cite either as a settled kill count. The committed tests those rows name are unaffected — they run green in CI regardless of any historical harness behaviour. A future slice touching the courtyard direction/demotion guards or the inset arithmetic should re-derive the mutants as a committed spec under `docs/mutants/` and run them through `scripts/mutation_harness.py`, which purges bytecode caches around every application and restoration and proves kills in both directions. |
| Interpretation | This is an evidence-auditability correction only, following the B-075/B-076 correction pattern: a new ID, the original rows immutable, the scope of what falls stated exactly. It adds no measurement, changes no metric, and claims nothing about courtyard behaviour. ([ADR-0098](../adr/0098-reproducible-mutation-evidence.md), [D-188](decision-ledger.md), [R-143](risk-register.md), [SEC-138](security-ledger.md)) |

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Question | What structural budget does an ordinary KiCad board actually need, and do the raised defaults stay bounded on hostile input? |

| Measured density | {"children_per_list": {"max": 21196, "median": 7133, "min": 2253}, "nodes": {"max": 169092, "median": 143997, "min": 129520}, "objects": {"max": 14899, "median": 9502, "min": 1130}, "tokens": {"max": 215168, "median": 185249, "min": 168109}} |
| Adversarial | {"previous_defaults": [{"bytes": 16777200, "elapsed_ns": 756947417, "outcome": "budget.exceeded.nodes", "peak_traced_bytes": 80224431, "shape": "deep", "traced_elapsed_ns": 5313154500}, {"bytes": 16777215, "elapsed_ns": 59027250, "outcome": |
| Claim | The defaults are derived from this distribution with headroom, not chosen. Nothing here claims a board *will* parse: a board inside every budget can still be refused for a modelling gap. |

### B-096 — Route obstacle-budget calibration and the real-board verdict re-measurement

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Question | How many objects does the single-layer router actually model per request on real boards, split by the populations one `max_obstacles` budget was charging; what do those distributions imply for a re-derived default; and what does the raised ceiling cost on the largest real board and on adversarial input? |
| Artifact | [`docs/research/route-obstacle-budget-calibration-v1.md`](../research/route-obstacle-budget-calibration-v1.md). **No board file, and no derivative of one, is committed**: the corpus is the operator's live audio-project tree, read read-only in place, and the note records only aggregate counts and verdicts. |
| Method | Every `.kicad_pcb` under the tree excluding `.history/`, 11 of 15 convertible; for each, the first 40 named nets in Board IR canonical order on `F.Cu`, clearance 200,000 nm, track width 250,000 nm, seed 7 — 385 previews, the selection fixed before the run and never adjusted by retrying. Object counts per net measured separately from Board IR using the enforcing code's own population definitions. Before and after runs taken back to back against byte-identical sources, with each board's SHA-256 prefix recorded per row; an earlier pair taken hours apart disagreed on three nets because the tree is a live working copy, and was discarded rather than reported. |
| Measured distribution | Same-net objects across all layers: p50 90, p99 608, max **755**. Same-net selected-layer attachment tracks: p50 72, max 663. Foreign selected-layer objects, whole board: p50 8,554, max **22,244**. Nets whose obstacle model fits 4,096 objects: 103 of 381 whole-board, **273 of 381** scoped to a 10 mm region — scoping is worth a factor of four in the ceiling for equal coverage. |
| Cost of the raise | Largest real board (31,389 segments), worst of 40 nets: 1.87 s and 45.5 MiB peak traced allocation at the new default; 1.95 s and 45.5 MiB unscoped at the 32,768 ceiling. Peak memory does not move with the budget — it is dominated by the parsed snapshot, and 32,768 inflated rectangles are about 4 MiB. Adversarial board with its whole region packed on a 100 um pitch: 32,000 objects refuse in 0.84 s at the default; 60,000 refuse in 1.52 s at the default and 1.74 s at the ceiling; Board IR's own byte budget refuses the snapshot past roughly 90,000. |
| Result | Same 385 previews, byte-identical sources, defaults: `routed` 0 → **14**, `already_connected` 263 → 318, `invalid_two_pin_net` 26 → 31, `off_grid` 0 → **8**, `no_path_in_region` 0 → 7, `obstacle_budget_exceeded` 93 → **3**, `no_path` 0 → 1, `unsupported_geometry` 3 → 3. Every net that moved moved out of `obstacle_budget_exceeded`; nothing regressed. 55 of the 93 became `already_connected`, which was always the true answer for a finished board. |
| Negative result | **The lattice class becomes visible on real boards for the first time, and is not measured here.** Issue #128 recorded that no `off_grid`, `no_path` or `grid_budget_exceeded` refusal appeared anywhere in this corpus, because the obstacle model was exhausted before any lattice question was asked. Eight `off_grid` refusals now appear, on three boards. B-088 localised its own corpus's constraint to the lattice; that hypothesis was untested on real boards and this entry makes it testable rather than testing it. Three nets still exceed 4,096 scoped objects and were not investigated. |
| Claim | The defaults are derived from this distribution, and the ceiling from a measured cost, not chosen. `max_net_objects` is derived from its own quadratic instead: 1,024 is the next power of two above the observed maximum of 755 and the largest whose pairwise merge stays under a quarter of the default obstacle-check budget, so the object budget is what refuses and the refusal can name it. |
| Non-claims | Nothing was DRC-checked, applied, or written; `routed` means a bounded search produced an exact orthogonal path under the modelled obstacles. No timing or memory claim on other hardware. The 10 mm region margin is calibrated against one 11-board mixer corpus and does not generalise. Multi-layer routing has its own budgets and is untouched. |
| Records | [D-176](decision-ledger.md), [R-133](risk-register.md), [SEC-132](security-ledger.md), [ADR-0089](../adr/0089-region-scoped-obstacle-model.md), issue #128 |
### B-099 — Tier-2 real-board capability survey across five read-only surfaces

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question | Issue #116 moved real-board *conversion* from 1 of 23 to 10 of 12. Conversion is step one and nobody had measured step two: once a real board is in, what do the surfaces built on it actually do? |
| Run ID | `sha256:95ded9d5e685b788eafe5959f8b19f9e74652ad144cddc0fbca8d0b2b2c11285` |
| Date and commit | 2026-08-07 UTC; measured at source commit `086f6cda91fdd6b071acd4ec661d3a4732e43200`, the `main` tip at measurement time; the tree carrying the runner and this record is the pull request that lands them, so the artifact records `dirty: true` exactly as B-094 does. Bound runner digest is recorded inside the artifact's own `runner_digest`. `main` then moved to `7e68f3a` (#108, the incremental congestion ledger and bounded rip-up window) before this record landed; the route and placement surfaces were re-run on two boards at that tip and reproduced their recorded verdicts exactly, so the artifact is left at its measured revision rather than restated. |
| Environment | Apple arm64 CPU; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13; **KiCad 10.0.5 invoked for real** for every DRC call. |
| Dataset | 12 boards from one designer's private working project tree, out of repository and **not redistributable**; `.history/` directories and derived stems (`routed-source`, `best-board`, `-placed`) excluded because they are copies of a board already counted. The same 12-board corpus as the #116 re-measurements. Nothing was copied into this repository and no board was written. |
| Configuration | Library API called directly, `Settings` constructed in-process so `allow_apply`, `allow_live_ipc` and `allow_live_apply` are all `false` and no ambient environment variable can reach them; every other setting at its default. One net class throughout (clearance 200,000 nm, track 250,000 nm, via 600,000/300,000 nm). Route preview on `F.Cu`, at most 40 nets per board in Board IR canonical order, decided before the run. Placement preview batched at 64 with a one-subject-per-call fallback on any non-clean batch, so all 991 footprints received an individual verdict. |
| Metrics | **Board IR: 10/12 convert** (991 footprints, 2,851 pads, 100,020 segments, 3,204 vias, 39 zones, 652 nets). **KiCad DRC: 12/12 reported**, including both boards Board IR refuses; 3 pass, 0 clean; 7,386 errors, 2,206 warnings, 274 unconnected items; 0.61–1.96 s. **Circuit Scene: 10/10 bounded regions clean; 8/10 whole-board requests hit `max_scene_objects`**, dropping 90,846 objects. **Placement: 991/991 footprints previewed, 0 refusals of any code; 10/10 boards refuse the source-preserving render.** **Route: 0 of 345 net previews routed** — 250 `already_connected`, 71 `not_routed/obstacle_budget_exceeded`, 23 `not_routed/invalid_two_pin_net`, 1 `not_routed/unsupported_geometry`. Whole run 692.8 s. |
| Negative result | **Not one net routed, and the reason is not B-088's.** B-088 measured 59.83 % completion on the external SimpleRouteJson corpus with every two-pin net refused `off_grid`, and localised the constraint to lattice alignment and the grid-node budget. On this corpus **no `off_grid`, `no_path` or `grid_budget_exceeded` refusal appears at all**: B-088 ran with the obstacle budget raised to 2,048 against boards averaging ~30 obstacles, whereas here the default `max_obstacles = 256` is exhausted by boards holding up to 31,389 segments and 1,003 vias before any lattice question is asked. Reporting the completion fraction alone would have implied the same wall. |
| Second negative result | **Placement preview succeeds on every footprint and can never be applied on any board.** 10 of 10 refuse `render_kicad_placement_candidate_board` and the route apply gate. On 8 boards duplicated KiCad UUIDs (D-158) are a sufficient cause; on 2 they are not — those boards are 100 % native on every footprint, pad, segment, via and zone, and refuse solely because an assembled `gr_line` `Edge.Cuts` outline takes a revision-derived contour identity. Only a single `gr_rect` outline yields a native one, which is the shape **every committed fixture and the CopperTone reference board uses** — the same fixtures-share-the-code's-assumption failure as #104 and #116. |
| Artifact | [`benchmarks/results/capability/2026-08-07-real-board-tier2-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/capability/2026-08-07-real-board-tier2-v1.json), validated against its own self-digest by `scripts/check_ledgers.py`. Runner: [`scripts/benchmark_real_board_capability.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_real_board_capability.py), which takes the corpus root as an argument and bakes no private path in. |
| Replay | **Not replayable in CI, and deliberately so.** The corpus is private design work that may not be committed, so there is no in-repository fixture this run could be reproduced from. Following B-094's precedent for its out-of-repo board, the artifact is the record and the runner is the method; a reader with their own board tree reproduces the *shape* of the result, not these numbers. |
| Claim | A measurement of what five read-only surfaces return, per board, at default settings. Nothing here is a fix, and every gap it names is filed rather than patched. |
| Non-claims | No electrical, thermal, signal-integrity, manufacturing or fabrication claim. Nothing was written, applied, exported, or opened in a live editor — appliability was measured by calling the apply path's own pure identity predicate, which proves the gate refuses and not that an apply would otherwise have succeeded. Route preview ran one layer and one net at a time against the unrouted snapshot, so the candidates are not mutually compatible and this is not a whole-board completion result. Placement preview ran without rules, so a clean verdict means legal-as-found and not placement quality. One designer's mostly four-layer project family is not a random sample of KiCad boards, and the timings are one machine and one run. |
| Records | [Tier-2 real-board capability survey](../research/tier2-real-board-capability-v1.md), #116 |

#### B-099 — root-property accept replay

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|

The runner, the corpus, the constraints, the request shapes and every setting are B-099's, unchanged. |
| Date and environment | 2026-08-12 UTC, before and after on the same machine and the same working tree, source commit `da51f7d` with the change applied on top; Apple arm64, macOS 26.5.2, Python 3.12.13, KiCad 10.0.5 invoked for real for every DRC call. Whole runs 900.3 s and 939.2 s. |
| Dataset | The same private working design tree, **17 boards** as saved today — five more than the 12 B-099 measured, the corpus having grown; `.history/` and derived stems excluded as before. Out of repository and not redistributable. |
| Delta | **None. Board IR converts 11 of 17 before and 11 of 17 after.** The construct this change accepts stops refusing, and the same four saves refuse one construct further in: three report `unsupported.transform` "courtyard layer does not match its footprint side" at `kicad_pcb.footprint[N].courtyard[N]`, and one reports `unsupported.construct` "expression contains an unsupported semantic field" at `kicad_pcb.footprint[N].pad[N]`. The other two refusals are unchanged (`connect`-kind pads, #138; a root copper graphic, #141). No downstream verdict changes, because the set of converting boards does not change. |
| Negative result | **Issue #140's premise does not survive the measurement.** The issue states four saves refuse "for this and nothing else"; none of the four converts. Conversion stops at the first error, so a refusal names the first blocker in document order and is silent about the rest — reading "and nothing else" out of one diagnostic reads an existential as a universal. Verified independently of the change: deleting the property expressions from each save's own bytes and converting on unmodified `main` reproduces exactly the post-change refusals, so both newly-visible constructs predate this work. This is the second time this corpus has produced the inference error, after the net-tie board D-179 records. |
| Artifact | **Not committed, and that is a departure from B-099 stated rather than hidden.** Both run files are derived from the private tree and the parent's artifact is committed only because it predates the tightened rule this work followed; the numbers above are the record. Runner unchanged: [`scripts/benchmark_real_board_capability.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_real_board_capability.py). |
| Claim | One before/after conversion count on one corpus, and a named refusal for each board that does not convert. |
| Non-claims | Nothing about routing, placement, DRC or appliability — those surfaces were exercised and their verdicts are unchanged precisely because the converting set is unchanged. No claim that the two newly-visible constructs are the last blockers on those boards; the same first-error rule that hid them hides whatever is behind them. |

The closures are **not vacuous**: a structural scan of the same 18 saves finds 1,362 of 4,826 roundrect pads with a fractional exact radius on 12 saves, 38 exact-45° chamfer courtyard edges on 9, 192 `fp_circle` courtyards on 10, 65 vias and 1,539 track segments at net 0 on 5, and 2,877 objects sharing a reused KiCad `uuid` on 7 — and every save carrying a chamfer, a net-0 copper object or a reused uuid converts today. `fp_arc` courtyards, which ADR-0080 still refuses by decision, number **zero** in this corpus, so nothing here tests that refusal either way. |

### B-100 — Where the `off_grid` refusal binds on real boards, and what a finer lattice buys

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Question | Since ADR-0089 made lattice-class refusals visible on real hardware for the first time, B-088's untested hypothesis — that the routing constraint is localised to the lattice — could finally be attacked. Which pad misses the lattice, by exactly how many nanometres, where do the nanometre residues in those coordinates come from, and does a lattice fine enough to represent the pad pair route anything? |
| Artifact | [`docs/research/off-grid-lattice-refusal-v1.md`](../research/off-grid-lattice-refusal-v1.md). **No board file, and no derivative of one, is committed**: the corpus is the operator's live audio-project tree, read read-only in place, and the note records aggregate distributions, typed codes and counts only. `scripts/benchmark_real_board_capability.py` was changed in the same commit so that its committed JSON report truncates an `off_grid` message before the geometry, which is the leak this row would otherwise have created. |
| Method | Every `.kicad_pcb` under the tree excluding `.history/` and derived stems, 11 of 17 convertible; for each, the first 40 named nets in Board IR canonical order on `F.Cu` at one net class (clearance 200,000 nm, track width 250,000 nm), every other setting at its default so `grid_step_nm` is 250,000 nm — 385 previews, the selection fixed before the run. Before and after runs taken back to back against byte-identical sources. Each `off_grid` refusal was then re-previewed at exactly its own `largest_representable_step_nm` with `max_grid_nodes` raised to its 500,000 ceiling. |
| Refusal counts, before → after | Byte-identical: `already_connected` 288 → 288, `not_routed/invalid_two_pin_net` 43 → 43, `not_routed/no_path` 27 → 27, `not_routed/off_grid` 18 → 18, `not_routed/no_path_in_region` 3 → 3, `not_routed/obstacle_budget_exceeded` 3 → 3, `not_routed/unsupported_geometry` 3 → 3, `routed` 0 → 0. **The change is what the 18 refusals now say, not how many there are.** Every aggregate in the two reports — verdicts, conversion, DRC, scene, placement, appliability — compares equal; the only textual difference anywhere is the recorded `off_grid` message, which moves from "the pad-center delta is not divisible by the requested grid step" to the redacted lead and carries no digit on any of the three affected boards, which is the runner redaction verified against real data rather than against a fixture. These counts are not comparable row-for-row with B-096's, which measured an earlier snapshot of the same live tree. |
| Measured miss | 18 refusals on 3 boards, every one a genuine two-pad net; 15 miss on both axes. Across the 33 axes that miss: min 7,500 nm, median **55,000 nm**, max **120,001 nm** — 22 % of a 250,000 nm step at the median, not a near-miss. Largest representable step, as a multiset: 1 nm ×8, 3 nm ×2, 5,000 nm ×4, 10,000 nm, 15,000 nm, 20,000 nm, 30,000 nm. Not one is a multiple of 250,000 nm. |
| Provenance of the nanometre residues | Of the 72 pad-centre coordinates involved, 55 are exact micrometre multiples, 16 sit exactly 999 nm past one, and 1 at 500 nm. The source boards carry millimetre literals one nanometre short of the round value the part was placed at, of the illustrative form `12.339999` mm = 12,339,999 nm, which CopperMCP converts through `decimal.Decimal` on the literal token to exactly that; the example is synthetic and no corpus coordinate is reproduced. **The residue is KiCad's float round-trip in the file, not a conversion defect here.** |
| Result | Re-previewed at the finest step their own geometry admits, with the node budget at its ceiling: **0 of 18 routed.** 13 refuse `no_path` — "the start pad cannot contain the routed width" — which proves exactly that a pad centre of the net lies outside the board outline **inset by half the requested track width** (125,000 nm at this net class), and deliberately not the stronger statement that the pad is far outside it: the refusal does not discriminate the two and nothing recorded here does either. The containment test is step-independent, so this holds at every pitch and not only at the one tried; 5 refuse `grid_budget_exceeded`, needing between 1.8 × 10⁷ and 3.3 × 10¹⁴ nodes to span the pad-to-pad bounding box alone against a ceiling of 5 × 10⁵. |
| Negative result | **B-088's hypothesis is refuted in the form that mattered, and this run delivers the refutation rather than a fix.** The lattice is where the refusal is *reported*, not where the constraint *is*. There is also no default `grid_step_nm` that rescues this corpus: the greatest common divisor across all 18 pairs is 1 nm, and 1,000 nm even after the KiCad residues are snapped away. The same masking pattern ADR-0089 broke repeats one layer down — `obstacle_budget_exceeded` hid the lattice class, and `off_grid` hides a pad-outside-the-outline problem on 13 nets, which is recorded here and left for its own change. |
| Claim | That these 18 refusals are correct, that their evidence is exact, and that changing the lattice fixes none of them **on this corpus at these settings**. |
| Non-claims | Nothing was DRC-checked, applied, written, or opened in a live editor. The corpus is one designer's project family and a live working copy: a different snapshot yields different counts, which is why the before/after pair was taken back to back. No claim that any net measured here is routable by other means, that the outline finding generalises, or that a lattice change is wrong for a corpus unlike this one. No timing or memory claim. |
| Records | [D-181](decision-ledger.md), [R-138](risk-register.md), [SEC-134](security-ledger.md), [ADR-0093](../adr/0093-actionable-off-grid-refusals.md), B-088 and B-096 above, issue #136 |

### B-101 — Does a courtyard's layer or its footprint's side decide a collision?

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Question | Board IR refused any board whose footprint carried a courtyard on the layer opposite its copper side (issue #151). Before deciding what that arrangement *means*, measure it: with courtyard layer and footprint side varied independently, which of the two does real KiCad's `courtyards_overlap` verdict track — and does the 10,000 nm collision threshold B-089 measured on `F.CrtYd` hold identically on `B.CrtYd`? |
| Method | 13 generated two-footprint boards through real `kicad-cli` 10.0.5 DRC at `--severity-all`, each compared against the deterministic legalizer's `courtyard_overlap` for the same bytes. Six arrangements at full 10 mm coincidence cover the two-by-two of {courtyard layers agree, disagree} × {copper sides agree, disagree} plus both same-layer controls; seven penetration offsets from −1,000,000 nm to +1,000,000 nm replay B-089's sweep with **both** squares drawn on `B.CrtYd` by two **front-side** footprints. Pads sit outside every courtyard and 8 mm apart so no pad rule can fire and mask the verdict. Runner: [`scripts/benchmark_courtyard_side_oracle_parity.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_courtyard_side_oracle_parity.py); no board is written to the repository and the corpus is not involved. |
| Result | **12/13 exact parity, 1/13 conceded `inconclusive`, 0 contradictions, 0 false-positive violations, 0 false-negative clears.** The single concession is the 9,999 nm sub-threshold case ADR-0075 declared as a permanent non-claim, and it appears on `B.CrtYd` exactly where it appears on `F.CrtYd`. The far-side threshold is identical: 9,999 nm clear, 10,000 nm violated. |
| Discriminating pairs | Two boards carrying the identical `B.CrtYd` rectangle and differing **only** in the second footprint's copper side both report `courtyards_overlap`; two boards whose rectangles sit at identical coordinates on **different** courtyard layers both report nothing. A model keyed on footprint side answers the first pair differently and the second pair wrongly. The reverse direction was measured too — a back-side footprint's `F.CrtYd` against a front-side footprint's `F.CrtYd` also collides — so this is not an artifact of testing `B.CrtYd` alone. |
| What the old model would have published | Under the previous same-side pairing, both colliding cross-side arrangements were never compared at all, so the published evidence would have read `courtyard_overlap: proven_clear` for placements real KiCad reports as overlapping. That is a keep-out deleted, in the one direction an obstacle may never err, and it is the reason this is recorded as a safety fix rather than as a widening. |
| Artifact | [`benchmarks/results/placement/2026-08-13-courtyard-side-oracle-parity.json`](../../benchmarks/results/placement/2026-08-13-courtyard-side-oracle-parity.json), validated against its own self-digest by `scripts/check_ledgers.py`. The runner refuses to emit an artifact if any contradiction appears. **The artifact was re-recorded before merge because its provenance field was false.** The first run recorded `source_commit: da51f7d`, the `v0.7.0` release commit -- whose adapter *refuses* every far-side board this runner generates, so that run demonstrably came from a dirty tree and the field claimed a commit that could not have produced it. The measured content was correct and is unchanged (adversarial review reproduced it case-for-case at `3f7d006`); only the provenance moved. `_git_commit()` now returns the commit **and** a `source_dirty` boolean, recorded as its own field and reported `True` on both failure paths, matching `benchmark_real_board_capability.py`. That is a change to what the runner records rather than to what it measures, and D-187 carries it as such. |
| Replay | Fully replayable on any host with KiCad 10.0.5: every board is generated in-process from integer nanometres and no fixture, corpus or network access is involved. |
| Claim | That on these 13 boards the model and the tool agree, that the collision threshold is the same on both courtyard layers, and that the verdict tracks the courtyard layer and never the footprint's side. |
| Non-claims | This is not a corpus result and no real board was measured here; the corpus conversion counts are recorded separately in this row's decision entry, and they did **not** move — 12 of 18 before and after, re-measured against current `main`. No claim about arcs or curved chains on either layer, about non-zero configured courtyard clearance, or about the tiny-shape band. KiCad also reports `pth_inside_courtyard` on some of these boards; that rule is not modelled and no verdict is offered for it. Nothing was written, applied, or opened in a live editor, and no timing or memory claim is made. |
| Records | [D-187](decision-ledger.md), [R-142](risk-register.md), [ADR-0097](../adr/0097-courtyard-layer-decides-the-side.md), [courtyard layer versus footprint side research](../research/kicad-courtyard-layer-vs-footprint-side-v1.md), B-089 and B-093 above, issue #151 |
