### B-106 — Excessive-agency evaluation replayed with a reachable authorized path
| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question | The suite recorded in B-089 required a refusal or an honest non-claim on every one of its 29 scenarios. Does it also observe the server **permitting** the one request it is supposed to permit, does that permitted write stay inside the workspace copy, and would the suite notice if the authorized path stopped being exercised? |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-06-excessive-agency-evaluation.json`](../../benchmarks/results/security/2026-08-06-excessive-agency-evaluation.json), re-recorded in place (the established pattern for this artifact, which must replay byte-identically from the harness it names). B-089's numbers stand as the earlier measurement and are not amended. |
| Method | `PYTHONPATH=src:. python3 scripts/evaluate_excessive_agency.py --evidence-harness-commit <40-hex> --output benchmarks/results/security/2026-08-06-excessive-agency-evaluation.json --fail-on-scenario-failure`. 34 predeclared scenarios in eight families across the same four project families; the catalog is digest-bound into the artifact. Five scenarios are new: two `authorized_apply` rows and three `workspace_containment` rows. |
| Result | **136 cases: 90 passed, 0 failed, 46 not run**, and **3 report-level controls, 0 failed**. Per project family: `development-fixtures` 32/0/2, `coppertone-buffer` 26/0/8, `heldout-audio` 32/0/2, `tscircuit-benchmark` 0/0/34. |
| The permit | `authorized-apply-permits-the-bound-candidate` records `applied` in `development-fixtures` and in the held-out `heldout-audio`, each having written real board bytes into a temporary workspace copy. `coppertone-buffer` records `no_apply_capability_available`: every net on that board is already routed and it is outside the placement-apply subset, so no genuine capability exists there to spend. |
| Containment | Three escape routes — an absolute path, a `..` traversal, and a symlink whose name passes every syntactic check — attempted **with consent granted and a genuine token**, against a real `.kicad_pcb` file in a sibling temporary directory outside the workspace. All 9 runnable rows record `invalid_request` with the outside board byte-identical across the call. |
| Negative control | With both capability probes returning nothing — the shape of "the authorized path silently stopped being exercised" — the suite records **`failed: 0` and an empty `failures` list**, and `controls_failed: 2`. Measured: the scenario layer alone drops from 90 passes to 76 and reports nothing. The CLI exits non-zero on the control failure and still writes the artifact. Pinned by `test_the_coverage_controls_fail_when_the_authorized_path_is_never_exercised`. |
| Mutation sensitivity | Six mutants over the authorization gate and the controls themselves, via the committed harness of [ADR-0098](../adr/0098-reproducible-mutation-evidence.md): `.venv/bin/python scripts/mutation_harness.py docs/mutants/2026-08-13-apply-authorization-gate.json`. **6 applied, 6 killed, 0 survivors, 0 stale anchors.** Inverting the route consent gate (`if not settings.allow_apply` → `if settings.allow_apply`) is killed by the permit row; removing it (`if False`) and inverting the placement gate are killed by the full-report row; removing the workspace confinement resolve-and-confine step in `security.py` is killed by the containment row; and **the two mutants that make the coverage controls always satisfied are killed by the degradation regression**, which is what makes the controls load-bearing rather than decorative. |
| Negative result | The whole-workspace digest failed on its first run and the failure was correct: a successful apply creates a pre-apply rollback copy under `.copper-mcp-backups/`, which the previous single-board digest guard could not see. The row now requires that copy to belong to the board that changed and to carry that board's pre-apply bytes. No such copy is created on any refusal path. |
| Claim | That the suite observes the server permitting exactly one predeclared authorized request, that the permitted write reaches that board and its own pre-apply copy and nothing else, that three predeclared escape routes are confined, and that the suite fails rather than degrading in silence if the authorized path stops running. |
| Non-claims | No model is invoked and nothing here measures whether an agent would attack. A passing catalog is coverage, not absence. The permit rows say the authorized request is not refused and writes only its own board; they say **nothing** about whether the geometry written is correct. Confinement is established on the filesystem the temporary directory landed on, against three named escape routes, and not in general. Nothing is claimed about a remote or multi-tenant deployment, and no electrical, thermal, mechanical, DRC or fabrication property of any board is measured. `coppertone-buffer`'s authorized rows are `not_run`, so the permit is evidenced on one held-out family, not three. |
| Records | [D-193](decision-ledger.md), [R-148](risk-register.md), [SEC-142](security-ledger.md), [ADR-0102](../adr/0102-an-evaluation-must-observe-a-permit.md), [excessive-agency evaluation v1](../research/excessive-agency-eval-v1.md), B-089 above, issue #110 |
### B-103 — Which pad field refuses, and what naming it plus accepting seven fabrication properties converts
| Field | Value |
### B-105 — Does the fill-aware obstacle model change anything on real boards?
| Question | Issue #63 asks for fill-aware zone routing obstacles. They already ship ([ADR-0039](../adr/0039-fill-aware-routing-obstacles.md), [ADR-0070](../adr/0070-layered-fill-aware-obstacles.md)), so the question this run answers is the one that decides whether to widen them: on a real corpus, does replacing a zone's conservative boundary envelope with freshness-verified fill change any route preview outcome, and what does it cost? Secondary: is the freshness proof even reachable on real boards? |
| Date and environment | 2026-08-13 UTC, branch `feat/fill-aware-zone-obstacles` on `main` @ `19ca287`. Apple arm64; macOS 26.5.2; Python 3.12.13; **KiCad 10.0.5 invoked for real**, once per zoned board via `run_zone_fill_authority`, never per net. Nothing was written to the corpus and no apply or live-IPC flag was set. |
| Dataset | The private working design tree used by B-094/B-096/B-099 — 18 saves holding 17 distinct board contents, of which **12 convert**. Not committed and not redistributable; only counts, typed codes and vertex totals are recorded here. |
| Corpus bracket | 34 `.kicad_pcb` files digested with sha256 before the run, after the run, and again after the re-measurement; **all three brackets agree on all 34 digests**, and the whole probe was re-run from a fresh private directory after a session-wide scratch-collision warning — the second run reproduced every board digest and every verdict count exactly. Per R-146, a number here is true of this corpus state and no other. |
| Reachability | **All 12 converting boards carry zones, and the freshness proof succeeds on only 3 of them** at the shipped `max_fill_vertices` default of 50,000: seven boards are refused before freshness is considered because their cached fill is 50,482–130,305 vertices, and two are `stale_fill`. At `max_fill_vertices = 200,000` (in domain; the env ceiling is 1,000,000) no board is budget-refused and the split becomes **6 fresh, 6 `stale_fill`**. The default was calibrated on CopperTone's 4,314-vertex pour and is 12–30× too small for this corpus; `cue` misses it by 482 vertices. |
| Layer finding | **Only 1 of the 12 converting boards has an `F.Cu` zone at all.** Every other board's pours are on `B.Cu`, `In1.Cu` and `In2.Cu`. `preview_route` gates fill authority on a zone existing on the *selected* layer, so the single-layer shrink cannot engage on eleven of twelve boards regardless of the evidence. The pours live on the layers the **ordered-layer** router reaches, whose public contract carries no fill authority at all. |
| Result | **Zero routes unlocked. Zero obstacle-model verdict changes at the shipped default.** Paired pure-router calls — identical snapshot, identical request, differing only in whether the verified islands are supplied — over 65 nets on the 3 reachable boards produced **1 changed verdict**, and it is the *connectivity* role, not the obstacle role: `invalid_two_pin_net` → `already_connected`. At the raised budget, 120 nets across the 3 further boards produced **8 changes: 2 connectivity improvements and 6 regressions**. |
| Negative result | **Where the shrink can engage, it makes the outcome worse.** On the only `F.Cu`-poured board all four changed verdicts went `no_path` → `obstacle_check_budget_exceeded`: thirteen exact pour polygons totalling tens of thousands of vertices exhaust `max_obstacle_checks` where one boundary polygon did not. The exact-polygon obstacle model measured at 4,314 vertices in B-021 does not scale to a real pour, and no route was unlocked to pay for it. |
| Differential for this change | The containment and ring-floor gates this branch adds to the single-layer core were measured with and without, on the three boards where evidence exists: **verdict counts are byte-identical in both directions on all three.** This differential bounds the *cost* of a refusal-side gate, which is what a with/without pair can support; it is deliberately **not** offered as an argument that the gate is safe. |
| Defect found | `AStarRouter.replay` reconstructs its request from the candidate and calls `propose` **without** `verified_fill`, and a `RouteCandidate` carries no fill evidence to reconstruct it from. Demonstrated on B-021's own fixture: the fill-aware candidate is 8,000 nm, its replay is 14,000 nm, and the equality check fails. Every downstream verifier goes through it, so `preview_route(include_fill_authority=True, include_drc=True)` refuses a legitimate candidate and `include_apply_token=True` mints a token whose apply fails the same way. Latent on this corpus only because the shrink never engages here. Filed as its own issue; not fixed in this branch. |
| Method | `run_zone_fill_authority` once per zoned board, then two `AStarRouter.propose` calls per net on the same snapshot at `layer:F.Cu`, seed 0, default `AStarSettings`, the same net class every other corpus run uses (200,000 nm clearance, 250,000 nm track, 600,000 nm via, 300,000 nm drill), 40 nets per board in Board IR canonical order chosen before any routing. The router is pure and never runs KiCad, so the two calls differ in exactly one input. |
| Claim | That on this corpus state, at these settings, the fill-aware obstacle model unlocks no route, changes no obstacle verdict at the shipped budget, and regresses six verdicts to a budget refusal at a raised one; that the freshness proof is unreachable on 9 of 12 boards at the shipped budget and on 6 of 12 at an adequate one; and that the replay gap above is real and reproducible. |
| Non-claims | No artifact is committed: everything measured here derives from a private design tree. Nothing was DRC-checked, applied, written, or opened in a live editor. This is not a claim that fill-aware routing is useless — the synthetic corridors in B-021 and B-086 stand, and the layer finding says the value would sit in the ordered-layer path this measurement could not exercise. No claim about whole-board completion, electrical behaviour, DFM, fabrication readiness, or Freerouting parity. No timing or memory claim: the run shared a host with other work. |
| Records | [ADR-0101](../adr/0101-fill-currency-is-not-in-the-document.md), [D-192](decision-ledger.md), [R-147](risk-register.md), B-021 and B-086 above, issue #63 |
| Question | Issue #152 reports a pad refusal that names no field. Which field is it; is it ADR-0091's preemption defect recurring; and once it is named and decided, how many boards convert that did not? |
| Method | Conversion only, no downstream surface. The same board selection, the same converter entry (`parse_kicad_bytes` under the route-preview profile and `parse_limits_for`) and the same exclusion rules as `scripts/benchmark_real_board_capability.py` — every `.kicad_pcb` under the tree excluding `.history/` and the derived stems `routed-source`, `best-board`, `-placed`. Three runs in **before→after→before** order against two Git worktrees, one at `origin/main` and one at this branch, recording each board's source SHA-256 alongside its verdict. |
| Corpus integrity | **The corpus is the designer's live tree and can change under a long run, so the run is built to detect that rather than to hope.** All 18 per-board source digests compare equal across all three runs, and run 3 reproduces run 1's verdicts and messages exactly. The delta is therefore attributable to the code and not to an edit. Digest-set fingerprint `sha256:e3940176b429b769af8c14a2835c97fc…` (truncated; the digests themselves are not published, being derived from private board bytes). |
| Denominator | **18 saves, 17 distinct board contents.** Two saves are byte-identical — the same board written to two places — and the runner's derived-stem exclusion does not catch it, so a count of "boards" is ambiguous unless it says which. Both are given below. |
| Which field refuses | A pad `(property …)` — KiCad's `PAD_PROP` fabrication annotation — at `kicad_pcb.footprint[N].pad[N]`, typed `unsupported.construct`. It is the **only** unmodelled pad head anywhere in the corpus: a census of every pad child head across all 18 saves finds `property` **39 times on 6 saves** and nothing else outside the allowlist. Every occurrence is `pad_prop_heatsink`. Six saves carry it; **two refuse on it**, the rest refusing earlier or converting — so the census count and the blocked count are different numbers and are stated separately. |
| Scope correction to issue #152 | The issue records **one** blocked save and there are **two**. The second is not a phono board and is blocked by this construct and nothing else, so it is the save that closing #152 converts. |
| Preemption or absence | **Genuine absence.** Unlike the seven heads PR #135 rescued from behind the pad allowlist, `property` was in no named table, so there was no reachable specific diagnostic being swallowed — there was no specific diagnostic at all. The class defect is broader than one head: KiCad's `parsePAD` accepts 39 top-level heads, the adapter modelled 13 and named 7, and the other 19 all emitted the same field-less sentence. |
| Conversion, before → after | **12 of 18 saves → 13 of 18**, equivalently **12 of 17 → 13 of 17 distinct board contents** — the duplicated pair is not among the boards that move, so the delta is one either way. Both runs at `origin/main` (`2d1d2ac`) read 12 of 18, which is the figure recorded for this corpus before the change. The newly-converting save reports `unmodelled_pad_property_count == 29`. |
| Advance, not a win | The second board carrying the construct did **not** convert. Its refusal moved from `unsupported.construct` / "expression contains an unsupported semantic field" at a pad to `unsupported.construct` / `pad field 'options' is unsupported` at a different pad — one construct deeper. Conversion refuses on the first error, so a refusal names the frontier and never the depth; this is the fifth time in three days that clearing a blocker has revealed another behind it. It is recorded as an advance, is counted nowhere as a conversion, and needs no new issue — the construct behind it is the custom-shape SMD pad already filed as #153. The other four refusals are unchanged: three `pad field 'options' is unsupported`, one root copper text. |
| Corrected mechanism | **A load-bearing claim in the first version of this row was inverted and is corrected in place.** It said KiCad's `AddEdgeExclusion` marks a region routing may not enter. It is a *forgiveness* region: `Edge.Cuts` is the non-routable obstacle (`pns_kicad_iface.cpp:2044-2076`) and a collision with it is **waived** inside a castellated hole (`pns_item.cpp:226-252`, `pns_node.cpp:797-806`; DRC twice more at `drc_test_provider_edge_clearance.cpp:209-215` and `:434-438`). The token grants routing space, so discarding it leaves this adapter stricter than KiCad — over-refusal, the allowed direction. **No measured number in this row moves**: the refusal is unchanged, so every conversion count, digest and verdict above and below stands exactly as recorded. What changed is why the refusal is correct. The whole before→after→before triple was nonetheless re-run after the correction, against merged `main` (`19ca287`) and with a probe kept outside the shared agent scratchpad — a sibling agent had overwritten the first one in place, which is its own small lesson about measuring from a file another process can rewrite — and it reproduces every figure: 12→13 of 18 saves, 12→13 of 17 distinct, digests identical across all three passes, `before` equal to `before-again` verdict for verdict. |
| Mutation evidence | `.venv/bin/python scripts/mutation_harness.py docs/mutants/2026-08-13-pad-fabrication-property.json --report report.json` — **20 mutants, 20 killed, 0 survivors, 0 `not_run`**, Python 3.12.13 on macOS-26.5.2-arm64, `baseline_returncode: 0`, spec `sha256:a2427bc638423283755486f6b2ee6462550768032e17f7af17ca1bc9a3758047`. The mutant→killing-test mapping is in the committed spec, one anchor per mutant, each matching exactly once. Read the mapping and not the count: PP01 (admitting `pad_prop_castellated`) is killed **only** by the partition test, because the value is refused by its own named branch either way — the same shape ADR-0091's surviving mutant took. **Two mutants went `stale_anchor` on a first run and were re-anchored and re-run rather than edited green**, which is the ADR-0098 rule working: adding the disclosure counter changed the call site both had anchored on, and the harness failed the run loudly instead of skipping them. PP18 and PP19 cover the counter itself — never incrementing, and incrementing before the aperture skip so a stencil opening is counted as converted copper. **RV03 is a mutant adversarial review found surviving and this run had missed**: returning `value == "pad_prop_heatsink"` from the validator passed the whole suite, because all four reads of the counter used heatsink and nothing else read it, so six of seven tokens could be silently un-counted. The counter test is now parametrized over all seven and the mutant is committed. It is the standing caution in `docs/mutants/README.md` demonstrated — a count of kills says nothing about the mutants nobody wrote. |
| Artifact | **None committed.** Every number above is derived from a private working design tree; no board file, no derivative, no net name, no reference designator, no coordinate and no corpus path is published here or in the diff. The probe script is a scratch artifact and is deliberately not committed: it duplicates the runner's conversion path, and a second copy of a measurement entry point is a divergence waiting to happen. |
| Claim | That on this 18-board tree, at this commit, naming the field and accepting the seven inert fabrication properties takes conversion from 12 to 13, that the corpus did not move under the measurement, and that the refusing field is a pad `property`. |
| Non-claims | Not a routing, DRC, placement, scene, timing or memory result — no surface but conversion ran. Not a claim that the newly-converting board is *useful* downstream; nothing here previewed a route on it. Not a claim that the corpus is representative of anything. Not a claim that `pad_prop_castellated` is rare — no corpus board carries one, so its over-refusal cost is unmeasured (R-144). |
| Records | [D-189](decision-ledger.md), [R-144](risk-register.md), [SEC-140](security-ledger.md), [ADR-0099](../adr/0099-pad-fabrication-properties-and-named-pad-refusals.md), [KiCad pad fabrication property](../research/kicad-pad-fabrication-property-v1.md), B-094 and B-099 above, issue #152 |

### B-107 — What carrying fill authority through replay changes on real boards, and why the answer is nothing
| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Question | Issue #163 fixes a defect on a path the shipped corpus runner never takes. Two questions then: does anything **regress** on real boards, and how much of that corpus could the fixed path reach at all? |
| Method | `.venv/bin/python scripts/benchmark_real_board_capability.py --corpus <private tree>`, three runs in **before → after → before** order across two Git worktrees, one at `origin/main` (`f0f7414`) and one at this branch, recording each board's source SHA-256 alongside every verdict. Plus a separate conversion-only census counting, per converting save, zones on the routed layer — the necessary condition for the single-layer fill shrink to engage. Both probes live outside the shared agent scratchpad; a sibling agent overwrote one in place during B-103, which is why. |
| Corpus integrity | **The corpus is the designer's live tree and it moved under this measurement.** 17 of 18 per-board digests are identical across all three runs; **one save, a phono board, changed between run 1 and run 2 and again between run 2 and run 3** — it was being edited while the runs went. Every comparison below is therefore restricted to the **17 saves whose bytes were identical**, and the one that moved is excluded rather than explained away. Digest-set fingerprint for runs 2 and 3, `sha256:9c9e9e3cafa21d617c495e582f450951…` (truncated; the digests themselves are derived from private board bytes and are not published). |
| Denominator | **18 saves, 17 distinct board contents** (one pair is byte-identical across two save directories); **13 saves convert**, all 13 distinct. Unchanged from B-103's 13-of-18 after PR #157. |
| Result — no regression | **Every total is identical across all three runs**: 13 converting, and route verdicts `already_connected` 324, `routed` 16, `off_grid` 35, `invalid_two_pin_net` 46, `no_path` 31, `no_path_in_region` 6, `obstacle_budget_exceeded` 3, `search_budget_exceeded` 1, `unsupported_geometry` 3; 5 of 13 appliable. Compared **per board** on the 17 saves with identical bytes, after against before, **no board differs on any surface this change touches** — Board IR, Circuit Scene, placement and route records are equal record for record. |
| **This is a no-regression measurement and not evidence for the fix** | The runner never sets `include_fill_authority`, so every candidate it produces has `fill_binding is None` and takes the byte-identical path through the payload, the replay and the token gate. **Nothing in this artifact exercises the defect or the repair.** Saying otherwise would be the with-and-without differential this repository has been caught by twice: it would bound what the evidence *adds*, not whether a consumer downstream of the router received it. |
| Coverage — why the corpus could not show it either | Conversion-only census over the same tree: **1 of 13 converting saves carries an `F.Cu` zone at all** — a single four-vertex outline — against 21 zones on `B.Cu` and 27 across the two inner layers. `preview_route` only reaches zone-fill authority when a zone sits on the *requested* layer, so even with the flag set, at most one board in this tree could engage the single-layer fill shrink. This reproduces B-105's independent count on the same tree from a different probe. B-105's further finding — that seven of the twelve zoned boards carry a pour past the shipped `max_fill_vertices` and two more are `stale_fill` — needs KiCad to read a pour and is **not** re-measured here, so it is cited rather than claimed. |
| Where the evidence actually is | Two committed fixtures, both replaying the whole defect. B-021's synthetic corridor (`scripts/benchmark_fill_aware_routing.py`): a route solving at **8,000 nm** replayed at **14,000 nm**, no diagnostic. And `tests/fixtures/route-candidate/blocked-zone.kicad_pcb`, **real KiCad bytes through the published serialization boundary**: a **20,000,000 nm straight, 0 bends** route replayed as **29,500,000 nm, 2 bends**, refused as `KiCadRoutePatchError("candidate does not match a deterministic router replay")`. Both are now assertions in the suite rather than numbers in a document. |
| Mutation evidence | `.venv/bin/python scripts/mutation_harness.py docs/mutants/2026-08-13-replay-fill-authority.json --report report.json` — **17 mutants, 17 killed, 0 survivors, 0 `not_run`**, Python 3.12.13 on macOS-26.5.2-arm64, `baseline_returncode: 0`, spec `sha256:3beb62cee6545a59df0c0b59fc2dac66e89be1996773bb1f9727729dd0aaf190`. Read the mapping, not the count: **RF02 and RF03 are the pair that matters.** Each weakens the binding equality to one direction, and each is killed by a different test — RF03 (refusing only the understated direction, which is the bug as reported) survives every test about the bug itself and dies only to `test_replay_refuses_fill_a_candidate_was_never_routed_under`. Without that test the dangerous direction would be unguarded and the suite would be green. |
| **A separate defect found in passing, and not fixed here** | The two baseline runs are at the *same commit* on *identical bytes*, and **nine boards' records still differ — every one of them only in the `drc` section**. `error_count` moves (936 → 941 on one board), violation type counts move (`hole_clearance` 201 → 202), and a whole type appears in one run and not the other (`tracks_crossing`, 4). **KiCad DRC counts are not reproducible run to run on these boards**, so any published claim quoting them is a claim about one invocation rather than about the board. This is not caused by, and is not fixed by, this change; it is reported rather than absorbed, and it is the reason every comparison above excludes the `drc` section. It deserves its own issue. |
| Artifact | **None committed.** Every number above derives from a private working design tree; no board file, derivative, net name, reference designator, coordinate or corpus path appears here or in the diff. Both probes are scratch artifacts and are deliberately not committed — the census duplicates the runner's conversion path, and a second copy of a measurement entry point is a divergence waiting to happen. |
| Claim | That on the 17 stable saves of this tree, at this commit, carrying fill authority through the replay changes no verdict on any surface, and that at most one save in the tree could reach the repaired path at all. |
| Non-claims | **Not evidence that the fix works** — see the row above; the fixture and mutation evidence carry that. Not a timing or memory result. Not a claim that KiCad DRC is deterministic; the opposite is recorded. Not a claim about the board that moved under the runs. Not a claim that the corpus is representative of anything, and not a claim about the layered fill path, which has no public contract. |
| Records | [D-194](decision-ledger.md), [R-149](risk-register.md), [SEC-143](security-ledger.md), [ADR-0103](../adr/0103-a-candidate-records-the-model-that-produced-it.md), B-021, B-094 and B-105 above, issue #163 |
### B-108 — What the cached-fill vertex budget defends, what it costs, and what it was hiding
| Question | Issue #165: `max_fill_vertices` defaults to 50,000 and refuses most real zoned boards before freshness is considered. Three questions decide whether that is a defect or a posture — what is the budget defending against and what does exceeding it actually cost; is one budget metering more than one population; and does raising it change any answer already given? |
| Method | Three measurements in one runner, `scripts/benchmark_fill_vertex_budget_calibration.py`, plus a separate KiCad-spawning probe for the freshness split. **Density:** every `.kicad_pcb` reachable from the repository plus every board under the operator's private audio tree, excluding `.history/` and derived stems, read through `read_fill_islands` with the budget raised past reach, counted with the accounting the enforcing code uses. **Cost:** each board read twice — once at a budget of 3, which refuses at the first vertex, once unbounded — because the difference is the only work the budget meters. **Reachability:** the densest legal fill document (`(xy 0 0)`, eight bytes, four charged nodes) bisected against the shipped `ParseLimits` in two shapes. `perf_counter_ns` with `tracemalloc`; peaks are traced allocation, not RSS. Nothing spawns KiCad in the committed runner and no allow flag is set. |
| Cost curve — the finding that decided the design | **The budget sits behind the parse it appears to guard.** `read_fill_islands` calls `parse_sexpr` on the whole document before counting a vertex, so refusing costs what reading costs: on the largest corpus board, **20.9 s of a complete read's 24.2 s**, and 10.9–16.5 % is all the budget meters across the seven largest boards. What it does meter is linear and cheap — **15.9–51.8 µs and 91–160 B per admitted vertex**, tightening to 22–26 µs and 113–145 B on boards large enough for the ratio to be stable. So the defence against an unbounded vertex list is `ParseLimits` (ADR-0079), not this. |
| Reachability | At the shipped parse defaults **no document can offer more than 741,375 fill vertices**: 741,375 in 4,096 islands (6,094,870 B) parses and one more refuses `budget.exceeded.nodes`; in a single island the wall is 499,999 and `budget.exceeded.children_per_list`. Under any configuration the parser's fixed 16 MiB input ceiling caps it at 2,097,152. |
| Density and the derived default | 7,195–29,503 fill vertices per mebibyte of source, median 15,732, over 22 boards. ADR-0079's rule at the densest observation gives 16 × 29,503 = 472,048 ⇒ **500,000**. **The number does not depend on the private corpus**: the densest in-repository board (CopperTone, 27,239/MiB) gives 435,824 and rounds to the same 500,000, which is 3.8× the largest pour measured anywhere. |
| DoS cost of the change | Against the densest reachable document — 741,375 vertices, 6,094,870 B — a refusal moves from **29.325 s / 146.8 MiB** at 50,000 to **35.812 s / 168.4 MiB** at 500,000; at the 1,000,000 ceiling the document reads, in 40.135 s / 226.8 MiB. **The change buys an attacker 6.5 s and 21.6 MiB** (+22 % wall, +15 % peak) on top of a floor the budget has never been able to move. The ceiling is left at 1,000,000: still reachable, so no accepted value is inert. |
| What it was hiding | Running the real authority path over the corpus at 50,000 and at 200,000, KiCad 10.0.5, byte-identical sources: **9 `fresh` / 2 `stale_fill` / 7 budget-refused → 12 `fresh` / 6 `stale_fill` / 0 budget-refused.** Every board that moved moved out of a budget refusal — three to `fresh`, four to `stale_fill` — and **of the 11 boards that already had an answer, every one keeps it.** Half of a working designer's zoned boards carry a pour KiCad does not reproduce, which was invisible behind a resource ceiling. (Issue #165 and `B-105` report 12 boards and 6/6 because they count only boards that also *convert*; six more carry a readable pour without converting, and reading a pour does not require conversion. Same corpus, different denominator, no contradiction.) |
| Two populations | **Yes, and deliberately not answered with a second knob** — this is where it departs from [ADR-0089](../adr/0089-region-scoped-obstacle-model.md). The budget is charged twice per proof, once on the operator's cached pour and once on KiCad's recomputation. The committed `zone-fill-stale` fixture caches **148** vertices and recomputes **186**, so every budget in [148, 185] admits the operator's board and then refuses this server's own output. But across six stale corpus boards the two differ by **at most 0.03 %** (−24, −9, −2, 0, +2, +2 against totals of 6,184–130,305), where #128's populations were three orders of magnitude apart. There is no second distribution to fit a second number to. What the finding did justify is a refusal that stopped lying: `_points` hardcoded "cached", producing `refilled zone fill could not be read: cached zone fill exceeds ...`. |
| Corpus integrity, and the noise it did not hide | Before → after → before, the third run from a pristine baseline worktree. **16 of 18 boards are byte-identical across all three runs**; one board was edited by the designer between runs 2 and 3, so only the first pair carries the differential claim, and one pair of saves is byte-identical to each other. **Run totals are identical in all three runs**, and on the 16 stable boards conversion, route verdicts, placement verdicts and scene content agree **16/16 in every pairing**. **KiCad DRC violation counts do not**: 10 of 16 agree, and they disagree just as readily between two runs of *identical* baseline code (max \|Δ\| 32) as across the change (109). The counts saturate near per-rule caps — 199/200/201, 499/500/502 — and which rules fill them varies run to run. Recorded rather than filtered, because the honest form of this differential says where it is noisy. It is also corroboration and not the argument: `settings.max_fill_vertices` reaches exactly two expressions in the codebase, both inside `run_zone_fill_authority`, and this harness never sets `include_fill_authority`. |
| Newly reachable refusal | This budget meters a board's **total** pour; every consumer charges the **widest island** (`routing.layered_board_adapter._MAX_FILL_VERTICES` refuses above 4,096; the single-layer core walks a ring per pairwise test against `max_obstacle_checks`). They are not proportional — one board holds 61 % of its 27,622 vertices in one ring, another 29 % of 59,852 across 96 islands. **14 of 18 corpus boards carry an island above 4,096, widest 43,889**, and no in-repository board does. Seven of those could not be read at all before; they will now read and be refused per-island. Reported and filed as [issue #167](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/167) / `R-150`, not fixed here. |
| Mutation evidence | `.venv/bin/python scripts/mutation_harness.py docs/mutants/2026-08-13-fill-vertex-budget.json --report report.json` — **9 mutants, 9 killed, 0 survivors, 0 `not_run`**, Python 3.12.13 on macOS-26.5.2-arm64, `baseline_returncode: 0`, spec `sha256:c625110be11e90df382f4eace8495cd75ac7b618fadda4e68e986a15a52036f5`. The mutants attack the number in both directions (default reverted, environment default drifted from the dataclass, ceiling raised with the default, floor below a polygon) and the reader's contract (guard off by one, budget truncating instead of refusing, budget becoming per-island, the refusal naming a document it cannot know, the refusal echoing the observed vertex index). |
| Artifact | [`benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-13-fill-vertex-budget-calibration.json`](../../benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-13-fill-vertex-budget-calibration.json) — the **repository-only** run, reproducible from a clean checkout. Corpus figures above are explicitly out-of-repo observations and **nothing corpus-derived is committed**: no board, no derivative, no net name, no reference, no coordinate, no corpus path. |
| Claim | That on this corpus at this commit the shipped budget refused seven boards before freshness was considered; that the budget meters 15.9–51.8 µs and 91–160 B per vertex behind a parse it cannot avoid; that no document can offer more than 741,375 vertices at the shipped parse defaults; that 500,000 follows from a measured density and the byte ceiling and buys an attacker 6.5 s and 21.6 MiB; and that every board with an answer at 50,000 keeps it. |
| Non-claims | **No routing claim.** `B-105` measured the route benefit of fill evidence on this corpus at zero and that stands; nothing here is justified by route quality. No electrical, thermal, signal-integrity, manufacturing or fabrication claim. No board was written, applied, exported or opened in a live editor. Not a claim that these densities generalise beyond the boards measured, that the per-vertex constants hold off CPython 3.12/macOS-arm64, or that the corpus is a random sample of KiCad boards. Not a claim about `.kicad_sch` or any other grammar. |
| Records | [D-195](decision-ledger.md), [R-150](risk-register.md), [SEC-144](security-ledger.md), [ADR-0104](../adr/0104-fill-vertex-budget-behind-a-parse.md), [Fill-vertex budget calibration](../research/fill-vertex-budget-calibration-v1.md), B-105 above, issue #165 |
### B-110 — What fraction of a real third-party KiCad corpus converts, and what refuses the rest
| Question | Plan item P1.1a, measure-first before P1.1's import ([issue #110](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/110)): nobody knows what fraction of an externally authored `.kicad_pcb` corpus this server can read, and both the value and the size of a frozen import depend on that number. Two questions, in this order: how many boards convert to Board IR **today**, and — because this project has advanced a refusal onto the next blocker five times in three days — what refuses the ones that do not, measured after each layer rather than assumed from the first. |
| Corpus and provenance | [PCBench](https://github.com/PCBench/PCBench) at commit `dec3be75cbdef74787625f9043c7391cd473bb64` (branch `main`, committed 2024-01-18), fetched 2026-08-14 as a `--filter=blob:none` sparse clone; the clone's `HEAD` was checked against the GitHub API's reported `main` before anything was read. Its advertised dataset is **164 boards**, which are exactly the 164 directories carrying a `visual.png`; the tree holds 1,182 board directories in total and 2,365 `.kicad_pcb` files across `raw`/`processed` variants. **Both variants of all 164 were swept**, 328 conversions in total. |
| Method | Every board read through `copper_mcp.adapters.parse_kicad_bytes` with the same net class the excessive-agency evaluation uses, so a board that converts here converts there. Two passes. **Pass 1** is the adapter exactly as shipped. **Pass 2** lifts `_SUPPORTED_KICAD_PCB_VERSIONS` to the set of versions actually observed in the corpus — **in-process, in a scratch script, never in the product** — because the first refusal is a gate that fires before the rest of the reader is consulted, and a census that stops there would report the gate rather than the gap. Neither pass writes anything; no board byte enters the repository. Per B-107's precedent the sweep scripts are deliberately **not committed**: they duplicate the adapter's conversion call, a second copy of a measurement entry point is a divergence waiting to happen, and the corpus they need is not in the tree, so a committed copy could not be replayed from a clean checkout anyway. The committed artifact carries the per-board digests instead, which is what makes the result checkable. |
| Conversion rate — the finding that decided the import | **0 of 164 convert, in both variants, at both layers.** Pass 1: 164/164 refuse `unsupported.version`, because the adapter accepts exactly one board format version, `20260206`, and the corpus was scraped in 2023 — 87 boards at `20171130`, 37 at `4`, 20 at `20211014`, 15 at `3`, and five across `20170123`/`20220914`/`20221018`. Pass 2, with the version gate lifted: still **0 of 164**, and every board now refuses at `kicad_pcb.layers` instead — **148 `unsupported.construct`** ("copper layer IDs, names, or declaration order are unsupported") and **16 `unknown.layer`** ("board must declare front and back copper layers"). PCBench's own cleanup does not help: `processed.kicad_pcb` normalises 162 of 164 to `20171130` and produces the identical 148/16 split. |
| Refusal census, and how deep it is | The census is **two layers deep and no deeper**, and says so. The conversion path fails closed on the first refusal, so each board yields exactly one diagnostic and everything behind the layer-stack declaration is **unmeasured** on this corpus. What the census establishes is therefore narrow and useful: the gap between Board IR and a decade of real KiCad boards is a **format-era gap at the layer declaration**, not a geometry-coverage gap, and no statement about pads, zones, arcs, custom pads, or differential pairs can be read out of these numbers in either direction. The refusal distribution is uniform enough that it identifies a single blocker rather than a population: two codes, one locator, 164 boards. |
| Licence determination — the second, independent disqualification | Re-verified before the first byte, and it does not survive contact with the tree. PCBench's `LICENSE` **is** verbatim MIT, `Copyright (c) 2023 PCBench` — git blob `dc4716051d9db5b01d4c8dd83b84c610d4749c7b`, `sha256:27f95dfaac3ac61888e14732fb83736da6880127c4a5d3093446e26e33ee1cb8`, text read in full rather than inferred from the API — and the API reports `spdx_id: MIT` — but PCBench is a **scrape of 1,018 other repositories**, its `github_meta/*.json` are stored GitHub search results, and every board's `metadata.json` records its own `source` repository and that repository's licence. Across the 164: 34 MIT, 26 "GNU", 19 empty, 17 `License not found`, 14 Creative Commons, 12 CERN, 8 Apache, 5 BSD, and a tail — **36 boards (22 %) carry no licence at all**, 57 are copyleft or CERN-OHL, 53 are permissive. Over all 1,182 directories, 624 have no recorded licence. So the MIT grant covers PCBench's own contribution and not the boards, exactly as PCBWorld's D3 was already ruled out for. Recorded as [ADR-0107](../adr/0107-an-aggregators-licence-does-not-govern-what-it-aggregated.md)/[D-199](decision-ledger.md); the prior "redistributable with attribution" determination in the research note and the corpora README is corrected in place. |
| Artifact | [`benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-14-pcbench-conversion-census.json`](../../benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-14-pcbench-conversion-census.json) — the upstream commit, the licence digest, per-variant summaries, the upstream-licence census, and a per-board row for all 164 boards carrying both variants' SHA-256, byte size, board format version, and both layers' verdict. **`committed_board_bytes: 0`.** The digests are what make this re-checkable: re-fetch PCBench at `dec3be7`, verify byte-identity against the manifest, and re-run. |
| Claim | That at this commit, on the 164 boards PCBench advertises as its dataset and on both stored variants of each, this server converts **none** of them; that the refusal is `unsupported.version` as shipped and a layer-declaration refusal behind it; and that PCBench's repository-level MIT licence does not grant redistribution of its board data, 36 of which carry no licence at all. |
| Non-claims | **Not a claim that these boards are unconvertible in principle** — the measurement stops at the second refusal and says nothing about the geometry behind it. Not a claim that KiCad 4–7 support is easy, hard, or worth doing; no cost was estimated. Not a routing, DRC, placement, timing, or memory result — nothing was routed and no board was written, applied, or opened. Not a claim about the 1,018 boards outside the advertised dataset, which were licence-censused but not swept. Not a legal opinion: it is this project's determination about its own redistribution, not advice about anyone else's. Not a comparison against any other corpus, and in particular **no board here is compared by name or by result against any board in the operator's private tree**. |
| Records | [D-199](decision-ledger.md), [R-153](risk-register.md), [ADR-0107](../adr/0107-an-aggregators-licence-does-not-govern-what-it-aggregated.md), [open-baseline benchmarks v1](../research/open-baseline-benchmarks-v1.md), [`benchmarks/corpora/README.md`](../../benchmarks/corpora/README.md), B-089 and B-107 above, [R-148](risk-register.md), issue #110 |

### B-111 — DRC comparability correction for B-099, B-103 and every bare count beside them

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Correction | Qualifies — does not retract — every **bare KiCad DRC count** this project has published: `B-099`'s per-board and aggregate DRC figures (12/12 reported, 3 pass, 0 clean, 7,386 errors, 2,206 warnings, 274 unconnected items) and its close-out replay's, `B-103`'s environment line ("KiCad 10.0.5 invoked for real for every DRC call") and any count read from those runs, the tier-2 capability note, and the seven committed artifacts the sweep in `scripts/check_drc_comparability.py` enumerates — the seventh being `B-069`'s FreeRouting comparison, whose three DRC sections publish their counts under a second vocabulary (`hard_violations` / `unconnected` / `footprint_errors`) that the policy's first section table did not name, so they were swept over in silence until review found the gap. Both entries remain immutable history and **every non-DRC metric in either is untouched**: conversion counts, route verdicts, placement verdicts, scene content, timings and digests all stand exactly as recorded. This correction is about DRC counts only. |
| Finding | `B-107` ran `scripts/benchmark_real_board_capability.py` **twice at the same commit over byte-identical boards**, and nine boards' records differed — every one of them **only in the `drc` section**. `error_count` moved 936 → 941 on one board, `hole_clearance` moved 201 → 202, and `tracks_crossing` (4) appeared in one run and not the other. No conversion, route, placement or scene verdict moved. `B-108` corroborates the mechanism from a different probe: the counts **saturate near per-rule caps** — 199/200/201, 499/500/502 — and which rules fill the caps varies run to run; on the same tree, DRC counts agreed on only 10 of 16 boards across runs and disagreed as readily between two runs of identical baseline code (max \|Δ\| 32) as across a change (109). So a DRC count is **not a function of the bytes it was taken over**, and an artifact publishing one as if it were promises more than its `run_id` delivers. |
| Disposition | Read every DRC count in `B-099`, `B-103` and the tier-2 note as **`single_invocation`**, per the one-value literals in [ADR-0109](../adr/0109-a-drc-count-carries-the-comparability-it-was-taken-with.md): none of the runs behind them repeated the invocation, so none of them can claim `repeated_agreement` and **none may be cited in a before/after differential**. Do not read any of them as wrong. A future slice quoting a DRC count must state the literal, and one quoting a difference must earn `repeated_agreement` on both sides — `--drc-repetitions` on the corpus runner is what earns it. The seven committed artifacts carrying pre-policy counts are keyed into `PRE_POLICY` in `scripts/check_drc_comparability.py` (22 sections), each entry naming this row, and an entry that stops matching **fails the run**. |
| Why qualify rather than re-record | Each of those artifacts computes its own `run_id` as a digest of its own content, so inserting the literal by hand either invalidates the recorded identity or produces a re-derived identity over numbers nobody re-measured. Following the `B-102` pattern: a new ID, the original rows immutable, the scope of what falls stated exactly. |
| What is **not** claimed | **Not that KiCad is wrong.** **Not that any recorded count was false when it was taken** — each is a true observation of one invocation. Not that the saturation mechanism is established: `B-108`'s caps are *observed behaviour* and KiCad's own source **has not been read**, so the mechanism is inferred from the values rather than confirmed. Not a claim about which counts are stable: **no N-run characterisation has been run** (issue #170 step 1, plan item P4.1a), so this row cannot say which of these figures would have reproduced. |
| Measurement added | **None.** This is an evidence-comparability correction only, in the shape of `B-102` and `B-075`/`B-076`. It adds no measurement, changes no metric, and claims nothing about any board. The two runs it rests on were already recorded in `B-107` and `B-108`. |
| Records | [ADR-0109](../adr/0109-a-drc-count-carries-the-comparability-it-was-taken-with.md), [D-201](decision-ledger.md), [R-154](risk-register.md), [ADR-0004](../adr/0004-authoritative-kicad-drc.md), B-099, B-103, B-107 and B-108 above, [issue #170](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/170) |
### B-112 — Who reads a Board IR pad's geometry, in which direction, and what a distinct envelope would actually buy
| Question | Plan item **P3.3a**, measure-first before P3.3 ([issue #116](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/116)): `R-145` records that [ADR-0100](../adr/0100-custom-pads-have-an-envelope-and-nowhere-to-put-it.md)'s refusal rests on a `Pad` invariant no type enforces — that `_pad_extent`/`_pad_bounds` and `_pad_core_extent` are the **only** readers of pad geometry and need opposite directions of error. Nobody had listed them. Two sub-questions follow: is every reader's direction unambiguous, and if the envelope were separated from the core, how many corpus boards would convert? |
| Method | Two independent sweeps of `src/`, because either alone misses readers. **(1) By field access:** `size_x_nm`, `size_y_nm`, `shape` and every `PadShape` membership test; the two size field names are unique in the tree, so this is exhaustive for direct reads. **(2) By accessor call:** every function surfaced by sweep 1, then its callers, then every *transitive* consumer of a stored result — `_PlacedPad.bounds` and `_PlacedPad.core` are dataclass fields (`placement/legalizer.py:145-150`), so a reader can sit three hops from any field name. Sweep 2 is what found the three mismatches; **none of those three sites mentions `size_x_nm` or `shape` anywhere**, so a field-name grep alone would have reproduced ADR-0100's three-reader answer and confirmed it. Corpus measurement separately, read-only, through `copper_mcp.adapters.parse_kicad_bytes`, over the 23 non-`.history` saves of the designer's live tree at `4a5fa65`. |
| Reader census — the finding that decided the gate | **23 sites in 14 modules where ADR-0100 assumed 3.** 8 require **over** (`astar::_pad_extent`, `layered_board_adapter::_pad_bounds` ×2, `layered_candidate_verifier::_point_in_pad_envelope`, `circuit_scene::_pad_half_extents`, `placement/geometry::pad_half_extents`/`pad_bounds`, `placement/view` hull); 3 require **under** (`astar::_pad_core_extent`, `::_pad_cores`, `placement/geometry::pad_core`); 4 require **exact**, where neither region is the right answer (`canonical::_pad` — the digest; `codec` decode plus its 13-key `required` set against `additionalProperties: false`; `Pad.__post_init__`'s drill/circle/roundrect invariants; `circuit_scene::_pad_object`, which publishes `size_nm` on the scene contract); 5 are carriers/producers. **And 3 are already unsatisfied on `main`.** |
| The three mismatches | `legalizer::_outline_containment` (`:501-518`) and `::_keepout_respect` (`:520-536`) each read the over-approximating `_PlacedPad.bounds` and publish **`violated`** — the false-claim direction under ADR-0075/ADR-0080, because `bbox(copper) ⊆ outline` is strictly stronger than `copper ⊆ outline`. This **corrects** the [custom pad envelope note](../research/kicad-custom-pad-envelope-v1.md) §5, which asserts both are "unaffected": that sentence is true of the *bound* and does not carry the *verdict*. `legalizer::_resolve_bounds` (`:603-617`) is unsatisfiable by any re-point — it feeds `rect_inside_ring` (needs under) and `rect_touches_ring` (needs over) from the same call, plus `_centre` for alignment/symmetry and a board-edge offset, which need a position rather than a bound. Five rule kinds, three incompatible requirements, one accessor. |
| Corpus, today | **15 of 23 non-`.history` saves convert.** Of the 8 refusals, **6 are the custom pad shape** (5 distinct byte digests), 1 a net-tie pad group, 1 a courtyard topology. Restricted to the benchmark's 18-save selection this is the recorded **13 of 18** with **four** custom-pad saves — so the target is real, and the `(property …)` blocker ADR-0100 reported as masked behind the custom pad has since been cleared by [ADR-0099](../adr/0099-pad-fabrication-properties-and-named-pad-refusals.md). |
| What P3.3 would buy — a sixth and seventh masked blocker, reported and not counted | With the custom pad neutralised in a **throwaway in-memory rewrite** (`custom` mapped to `PadShape.RECT` — deliberately unsound geometry, used only to see what is behind it, never written to the tree and never applied to a board), **all six saves refuse immediately on `pad field 'thermal_bridge_angle' is unsupported`, at the same pad locator.** Neutralising that too: `phono-v2-main` and `phono-v3-main` convert; `phono-v2` refuses `unsupported.topology: multiple disjoint Edge.Cuts loops`; `phono-v3` refuses `unsupported.topology: courtyard edges must be non-zero and axis-aligned or 45-degree chamfers`. So **P3.3 shipped perfectly converts 0 of 18 additional saves**; P3.3 *plus* a `thermal_bridge_angle` accept converts 2 (13 → 15 of 18); the other two need a further topology decision each. Sixth and seventh instances of one refusal masking the next. |
| Artifact | The survey itself — [pad geometry reader survey](../research/pad-geometry-reader-survey-v1.md) — one row per reader with `file:line`, the direction it requires, and what a pad whose envelope ≠ core would do to it. It is the reusable deliverable and it stands whether or not P3.3 is ever attempted. The conversion and neutralisation probes were scratchpad scripts over the designer's private tree; they are not committed, and no board bytes, digests or geometry from that tree are published here. |
| Claim | That at `4a5fa65` these 23 sites are every place in `src/` that reads a `Pad`'s `shape`, `size_x_nm` or `size_y_nm`, directly or through a derived accessor or a stored bound; that 3 of them require a direction they do not get today and 1 of those 3 requires two opposite directions from one call; that on this corpus at this commit the custom pad fronts 4 of the 18 benchmark saves; and that behind it every one of those 4 refuses `thermal_bridge_angle` before converting. |
| Non-claims | **Not a false-violation rate.** The C-group mismatch is established by reading the predicates, not by finding a board that trips it; whether any corpus board is currently mis-verdicted is **unmeasured**. **Not a claim that ADR-0100 is wrong** — its decision stands and its argument is strengthened; one sentence of its supporting research note is corrected. **Not a claim that the custom pad envelope is underivable** — it is derivable and that note's §4 settles it. **No conversion win**: 13 of 18 before this branch and 13 of 18 after, because this branch changes no code. **Not a claim that the queue behind `thermal_bridge_angle` is only two deep** — the walk stops at the first non-pad-field refusal on two of the four saves and says nothing about what is behind those. |
| Records | [D-203](decision-ledger.md), [R-156](risk-register.md), [R-145](risk-register.md), [ADR-0100](../adr/0100-custom-pads-have-an-envelope-and-nowhere-to-put-it.md), [ADR-0105](../adr/0105-a-schema-version-moves-with-its-accepted-set.md), [pad geometry reader survey](../research/pad-geometry-reader-survey-v1.md), issue #116 |

### B-113 — A cross-router comparison table with a typed result for every declared baseline
| Question | M2's closing condition 2 ([post-0.8.0 audit](../audit/2026-08-14-post-0.8.0-audit.md)) asks for "a cross-router comparison artifact carrying typed results, `not_run`-with-reason included. A typed `not_run` is a result; a missing row is not." [Issue #65](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/65) has been open against that since M2 began. Two questions: on a frozen, redistributable corpus this project already owns, what does CopperMCP's single-layer router actually do — and for every router baseline the licensing determination names, what is the typed reason it was not run, and what exactly would change that? |
| Problem set | The committed **MIT SimpleRouteJson corpus** from B-088 — `dwiel/tscircuit-benchmark` at `be36518b5bf51755dae92c230061ab3cf4e3e063`, © 2026 Zach Dwiel, 20 of 36 boards under a prefix rule fixed in advance, each verified against the digest manifest before routing. **This corpus satisfies M2's closing condition 3 for the routing measurement**: it is frozen in the sense the condition requires — the bytes cannot change without failing the digest check — and redistributable in the sense `benchmarks/corpora/README.md` requires, which is exactly what `R-146` says the designer's live tree is not. It is **not** an externally authored KiCad family and does not discharge [#110](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/110); the artifact carries that limit in its own problem-set block so the two cannot be read as one. |
| Method | The subject row **loads B-088 rather than re-measuring it**: the committed artifact's schema and self-digest are verified, its corpus identity is checked against the digest-verified manifest, and its two recorded configurations are projected directly into the comparison row. A sentinel regression replaces a B-088 wire-length value in memory and proves the table uses that loaded value; a tamper regression proves an edited artifact is refused. The common `mean_wall_seconds` metric now lives inside each measured configuration beside the other common metrics, sourced from B-088's three-repetition timing. What is new is the comparison frame — a frozen baseline roster, a typed resolution rule, and a metric set defined once so a baseline added later lands comparable rather than adjacent. A baseline is `measured` only when **every** declared precondition is *observed* to hold; its reason is assembled from the preconditions actually unmet in that recording, so a partial environment transition cannot retain a stale claim that an already-present executable is absent. A precondition this harness cannot observe is never optimistically assumed. |
| Subject — measured | CopperMCP `astar-grid/0.7.0`, `orthogonal-a-star-spatial-index-v1`. **70 of 117 attempted nets under both grid policies (59.83 %)**, `1.1711×` a provable pad-gap lower bound, **zero vias**, 235 bends, 9 further nets stating no routing work and counted outside the denominator. Refusals, in full: fixed 250 µm step — 36 `off_grid`, 9 `no_path_in_region`, 2 `no_path`; divisor-aligned — 21 `grid_budget_exceeded`, 15 `off_grid`, 9 `no_path_in_region`, 2 `no_path`. Identical to B-088 net for net. |
| Baselines — every one typed, none run | **FreeRouting v2.2.4** (GPL-3.0): `not_run` on an *environment* fact — not installed, no Java runtime, no SimpleRouteJson→DSN bridge — and its provider [#53](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/53) is parked behind an operator gate whose own text says the issue does not authorize it. Its row additionally carries the caveat that **even fully run it would not be neutral here**, because this corpus was built with FreeRouting in the loop. **tscircuit/autorouting** (no licence, archived 2025-08-15): `not_run` on a *licence* fact — all rights reserved, so its solvers may not be vendored, committed, or redistributed. **PCBWorld** (CC-BY-4.0 code, announced): `not_run` on a *licence* fact — never released, no resolvable host. Each row names its §3 determination, its unmet preconditions by name, and what would change it. |
| Denominator discipline | Every attempted net is accounted for by exactly one outcome and the breakdown sums to `nets_attempted`; the harness **raises** rather than reporting a rate over a shortened denominator. This is the failure mode `tscircuit/autorouting`'s own harness has by default — it stops after ten failures unless `noSkipping` is set, so a weak solver's published score is computed over a shortened, non-comparable denominator — and inverting it is the point. |
| No DRC metric, deliberately | The protocol's metric set is a **closed tuple** and a test asserts no DRC-shaped metric is in it. The reason is recorded in the artifact rather than in a commit message: the problem set is SimpleRouteJson, so no imported board has a KiCad document a design-rule check could run against, and a DRC count is not reproducible across environments, so a count carried between two rows would not be a comparison. |
| Mutation evidence | [`docs/mutants/2026-08-14-cross-router-comparison.json`](../mutants/2026-08-14-cross-router-comparison.json), **13 mutants, 13 killed, 0 survivors**, under `scripts/mutation_harness.py` with each kill proved in both directions. The three that matter are the ones a green suite would otherwise admit: `XR01` (the roster length check disabled — a dropped baseline leaves a well-formed, self-consistent, one-row-shorter table), `XR11` (a roster member renamed out of §3), and `XR12` (a declared baseline never reaching the table). `XR06` kills the optimistic reading of an unobservable precondition and `XR13` its mirror image, found while writing this row: macOS ships `/usr/bin/java` as a **stub that exists whether or not a JRE does** and exits non-zero saying so, so a `shutil.which` hit would have reported FreeRouting's Java precondition satisfied and flipped its row off `not_run` **on no evidence**. The precondition is now observed by executing `java -version`, which is the audit's own generalised rule read in the other direction: a *presence* is evidence only if the observation was capable of reporting an absence. `XR09`/`XR10` kill an artifact that would claim a comparison it does not have. |
| Artifact | [`benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-14-cross-router-comparison-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-14-cross-router-comparison-v1.json) — the frozen problem set with all 20 board digests, the protocol, four typed router rows, and the four §3 corpora with their determinations so no §3 row is silently absent. It records `measured_rows: 1`, `declared_rows: 4`, `comparison_supported: false`. |
| Claim | That at this commit, on this frozen 20-board MIT corpus, CopperMCP's single-layer router routes 70 of 117 attempted nets at 1.1711× a provable lower bound with zero vias; and that every router baseline the open-baseline licensing determination names carries a typed `not_run` whose reason is a licence or environment fact already recorded there, together with the checkable conditions that would change it. |
| Non-claims | **Not a comparison.** One measured row supports no comparative conclusion of any kind — not better, not worse, not comparable — and the artifact says so first in its own `not_claimed` list. Not a DRC, electrical, signal-integrity, thermal or fabrication result; no DRC metric is in the protocol and none is inferred from one that is. Not a route-quality or optimality claim: the ratio is against a loose provable bound that ignores every obstacle, bend and detour. Not a whole-board completion result — each net is routed independently against the unrouted snapshot, so the candidates are not mutually compatible. No generalisation beyond LLM-generated 2-layer tscircuit boards, and none that the committed 20-board prefix represents the full 36. Not a discharge of #110: a frozen SimpleRouteJson corpus is not an externally authored KiCad family, and `R-153` stays open. |
| Records | [D-204](decision-ledger.md), [R-157](risk-register.md), B-088 and B-069 above, [open-baseline benchmarks v1](../research/open-baseline-benchmarks-v1.md), [post-0.8.0 audit](../audit/2026-08-14-post-0.8.0-audit.md), issues #65 and #53 |

### B-114 — Ten licence-clean open-hardware boards, re-saved in KiCad 10.0.5, and what still refuses
| Question | [ADR-0107](../adr/0107-an-aggregators-licence-does-not-govern-what-it-aggregated.md) settled *how* to license an external corpus and named the candidate it leaves standing: "a single-author open-hardware repository with a clear licence covering its own boards". B-110 disqualified PCBench on two grounds at once — licence and format era — which left the honest question unanswered. **Pick upstreams that clear both, and does anything convert?** Issue #110's residual (`R-148`, `R-153`) turns on the answer. |
| Corpus and provenance | **Ten boards from ten distinct upstream repositories**, each fetched at a pinned commit and each digested before and after derivation. Four Apache-2.0 (`antmicro/m2-pcie-adapter`, `antmicro/lpddr4-testbed`, `antmicro/ov9281-camera-board`, `TinyTapeout/tt-demo-pcb`), three CERN-OHL-P-2.0 (`greatscottgadgets/amalthea-hardware`, `.../cynthion-hardware`, `.../hackrf-pro`), one MIT (`keebio/bfo9000-pcb`), two `NOASSERTION` (`machdyne/lakritz` under a non-OSI custom licence, `wntrblm/Castor_and_Pollux` whose `LICENSE.md` does map CERN-OHL-P v2 to the board directory). Every determination is made per item from the repository's own `LICENSE`, its README licence section, and the board file's own `title_block` — never from an aggregator. **8 of 10 are importable on licence alone**, and that count is deliberately conservative: it requires an OSI-recognised SPDX identifier, so `wntrblm/Castor_and_Pollux` is excluded on its `NOASSERTION` API label even though its own `LICENSE.md` maps CERN-OHL-P v2 to the board directory and the board file repeats it. Two carry the permission *inside the board file*: Amalthea's `title_block` reads `(company "Copyright 2020-2023 Great Scott Gadgets") (comment 1 "Licensed under the CERN-OHL-P v2")`, and Castor & Pollux's carries `(comment 2 "CERN-OHL-P v2")` — which is ADR-0107's ideal case, a determination that does not depend on repository metadata at all. |
| Method | Three gates per item, in order, and the sweep stops at the first that fails. (1) **Licence**, above. (2) **Format era**: each board opened and re-saved once by **KiCad 10.0.5** `pcbnew.LoadBoard`/`SaveBoard`, which upgrades the board format version and edits no design content. (3) **Conversion**: the re-saved bytes through `copper_mcp.adapters.parse_kicad_bytes` under the same net class the excessive-agency evaluation uses, so a board that converts here converts there. A **second pass** lifts the `Edge.Cuts` curve gate in-process — in a scratch script, never in the product — because the conversion path fails closed on the first refusal and a census that stops there reports the gate rather than the gap. Per B-107 and B-110's precedent the sweep script is **not committed**: it duplicates the adapter's conversion call, and the boards it needs are not in the tree, so a committed copy could not be replayed from a clean checkout. The per-board digests are what make it checkable. |
| The format era is not the constraint | Originals span four format versions — six at `20241229` (KiCad 9), two at `20240108` (KiCad 8), two at `20221018` (KiCad 7). **All ten re-save to `20260206`, the single version the adapter accepts, and all ten clear the version gate.** B-110's `unsupported.version` wall, which refused 164 of 164, does not appear once here. |
| Conversion — the finding | **0 of 10 convert, and 0 of 10 convert with the obvious first gate lifted.** Pass 1, as shipped: **8** refuse `unsupported.construct` — *"Edge.Cuts outline arcs, circles and curves are unsupported"*; one refuses on copper text and one on copper layer kind. Pass 2, with the `Edge.Cuts` curve gate lifted: still **0 of 10**, and the refusal has moved rather than cleared — **6** now refuse *"copper text has no envelope derivable from the board and is unsupported"* ([ADR-0095](../adr/0095-copper-text-has-no-derivable-envelope.md), the refusal `#141` was closed on with five exit conditions and none met), one on copper layer kind, one on *"graphic without one explicit layer"*, one on root dimension objects, one on an unsupported root semantic construct. |
| Blocking geometry, counted | 9 of 10 boards carry `Edge.Cuts` curves — **97** arc, circle and bezier primitives in total against 123 straight segments — which is simply what a rounded board corner is. 7 of 10 carry copper text, **24** objects in total. The two are near-disjoint in effect: only one board (`antmicro/ov9281-camera-board`, whose outline is four straight lines) reaches the copper-text refusal without passing the outline one. |
| Negative result | **The licence was never the binding constraint, the format era is not the binding constraint once a board is re-saved, and fixing the obvious blocker unlocks nothing.** The hypothesis worth testing was that `Edge.Cuts` arc support is what stands between this project and an externally authored KiCad family; it is refuted here, *before* anyone costed the work. The wall behind it is ADR-0095's copper-text envelope, which this project has already decided it cannot derive and has already written five exit conditions for. This also corrects the blocker `R-153` states: KiCad 4–7 intake is **not** what an import needs, because a KiCad 10.0.5 re-save clears the era gate for free. **No import lands, no `ProjectFamily` is registered, `R-148` and `R-153` are unchanged and open**, and `committed_board_bytes` is 0. The sdist delta was measured rather than assumed: 5,269,472 → 5,305,090 bytes (**+35,618, +0.68 %**), and every one of those bytes is a record — this artifact, B-113's, one mutation spec, one runner and its tests. **No board data ships.** |
| Artifact | [`benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-14-open-hardware-intake-sweep.json`](../../benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-14-open-hardware-intake-sweep.json) — per board, the upstream repository and pinned commit, the licence with its evidence sentence and hardware-coverage verdict, the original and derived SHA-256 and byte size, the original and derived format versions, the geometry census, and both passes' verdicts. `committed_board_bytes: 0`. Re-fetch at the pinned commits, verify byte-identity against the recorded digests, re-save in KiCad 10.0.5, and re-run. |
| Claim | That at this commit, on these ten individually licence-verified open-hardware boards, a KiCad 10.0.5 re-save clears the board-format-version gate for all ten and this server converts **none** of them; that the refusal is the `Edge.Cuts` curve construct for eight of them; and that lifting that one gate converts **none** of them either, the majority landing instead on ADR-0095's copper-text refusal. |
| Non-claims | Not a claim that these boards are unconvertible in principle — the census is two layers deep and everything behind the second refusal is unmeasured. **Not a sample of anything**: the ten were selected for licence clarity and KiCad-native development, which is a selection on the very property under test, so the rate is a statement about these ten boards and no population. Not a claim about how hard arc outlines or copper-text envelopes would be; no cost was estimated for either. Not a routing, DRC, placement, timing or memory result — nothing was routed, applied, written, or opened in a GUI. Not a legal opinion: the licence rows are this project's determination about its own redistribution. **No board byte is committed by this record.** |
| Records | [R-157](risk-register.md), [ADR-0107](../adr/0107-an-aggregators-licence-does-not-govern-what-it-aggregated.md), [D-199](decision-ledger.md), [R-148](risk-register.md), [R-153](risk-register.md), [ADR-0095](../adr/0095-copper-text-has-no-derivable-envelope.md), [`benchmarks/corpora/README.md`](../../benchmarks/corpora/README.md), B-110 above, issues #110 and #141 |

### B-115 — `thermal_bridge_angle` typed non-claim conversion census

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question and predeclared prediction | Issue #186, measured before implementation: does accepting a validated but unmodelled pad `thermal_bridge_angle` remove the masked blocker B-112 found? **Direct prediction: 13 → 13 of 18**, because conversion stops at the first refusal and `custom` remains in front on all four affected saves. **Masked prediction:** with only the same deliberately unsound custom-pad neutralisation used to see behind B-112's blocker, `phono-v2-main` and `phono-v3-main` convert, while `phono-v2` advances to `unsupported.topology: multiple disjoint Edge.Cuts loops` and `phono-v3` advances to `unsupported.topology: courtyard edges must be non-zero and axis-aligned or 45-degree chamfers`, for 15/18. A result below either prediction is recorded as the finding rather than smoothed. |
| Method | Conversion-only read of the private 18-save benchmark selection through the route-preview constraint profile at branch commit before push. The five benchmark exclusions remain exactly B-112's: three derived stems and two superseded saves under `phono-v2/pcb/`. For the masked pass only, `_Converter._pad_shape` mapped `custom` to `PadShape.RECT` and the custom-only `options`/`primitives` payload fields were ignored **in process**; this is deliberately unsound geometry, never written, never routed, and never applied. SHA-256 of every selected source was recomputed before and after both passes and matched. No board byte, digest, geometry, net name, component reference, or coordinate is committed. |
| Direct result | **13/18 convert, exactly as predeclared.** The five first refusals are four custom-pad `unsupported.construct` results plus the previously recorded `phono-v3-mcdb` courtyard `unsupported.topology`. This branch therefore claims no direct conversion-count increase. |
| Masked result | **15/18 convert, exactly as predeclared.** `phono-v2-main` and `phono-v3-main` convert with `unmodelled_thermal_bridge_angle_pad_count == 1` each. `phono-v2` advances to the disjoint-Edge.Cuts refusal and `phono-v3` to the courtyard-slope refusal. Those are newly exposed next blockers, not regressions. The source SHA map was unchanged. |
| Contract evidence | Unit and boundary tests cover 0/1/2 converted-pad counts; exact Board IR content equality apart from source revision; negative, >360 and fractional degrees; duplicate, empty, multi-atom, quoted, exponent, nested, nonnumeric and sub-microdegree refusals; aperture validation without counting; failed-document zero invariants; reflective MCP map coverage and a nonzero end-to-end `inspect_board_ir` summary. Route and placement render tests prove the exact source token survives both mutation paths. |
| Claim | That D-205 removes `thermal_bridge_angle` as the blocker immediately behind custom pads on the four measured saves, and exposes exactly the two conversions and two topology refusals predicted before implementation. The field is validated, discarded from Board IR, counted, published to MCP, and retained in source-preserving write paths. |
| Non-claims | **No direct corpus conversion win while custom pads still refuse.** Not custom-pad geometry or reader correctness; the neutralisation is unsound by construction. Not Board-IR snapshot refill, thermal-spoke parity, connectivity parity, DRC, routing, placement legality, fabrication, performance, or a third-party-board conversion. Not corpus representativeness. The probe stops at the first non-custom refusal and says nothing about blockers behind the two topology results. No corpus board was written. |
| Records | [D-205](decision-ledger.md), [R-158](risk-register.md), [Board IR architecture](../architecture/board-ir.md), [issue #186](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/186), B-112 above |

### B-116 — placement direction-bracket corpus and KiCad-parity measurement

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question and predeclared prediction | Issue #187, before implementation: on the 13 of 18 private corpus boards that convert, does replacing bounds-only outline/keepout accusations with direction-typed pad geometry reduce false published `violated` verdicts against KiCad 10.0.5? Baseline: **1 false outline board, 0 false keepout boards**. Prediction: **1→0** and **0→0**, conversion stays **13/18**, and the three affected placement batches on the one board become previewable. Any other result is retained as the finding. |
| Method | The same 18-save selection and route-preview conversion profile as B-112/B-115, followed by the unchanged-board legalizer in batches up to the public subject ceiling. For each of the 13 converting boards, KiCad 10.0.5 DRC ran twice. Only the two provider counts used by this question — `copper_edge_clearance` and `items_not_allowed` — are published here, and both agreed exactly between repetitions on every board (`repeated_agreement`); known-noisy unrelated DRC counts are neither compared nor cited. SHA-256 was recomputed over every selected source before and after the whole run and matched. No private path, board digest, reference, net, coordinate, geometry or board byte is committed. |
| Baseline | **13/18 convert.** One board publishes `outline_containment: violated` in all three of its placement batches while both KiCad repetitions report zero `copper_edge_clearance`; **1 false outline board**. No board publishes a false keepout violation; **0 false keepout boards**. |
| Refuted first repair | Replacing the envelope with the under-approximating core but retaining a two-valued inside/outside answer produced **1 false outline board, 0 false keepout boards** — no improvement. Inspection of KiCad's official 10.0.5 edge-clearance provider showed why: it indexes nearby `Edge.Cuts`/`Margin` geometry and calls `Collide`; it does not ask whether copper is globally inside the board. A core wholly outside but remote from an edge is not a `copper_edge_clearance` violation. The implementation was changed because the measurement killed its premise, not tuned around a corpus stem. |
| Final result | **Prediction met:** false outline violations **1→0**; false keepout violations **0→0**; conversion remains **13/18**. The three affected batches become `outline_containment: inconclusive` and `status: previewed`. Every relevant KiCad count has `repeated_agreement`, and the corpus source hash map is unchanged. Refused conversion remains five boards: four at the custom-pad front blocker and one at its recorded courtyard-topology blocker. |
| Independent direction evidence | Two committed diagonal-boundary fixtures exercise an over-approximating box where circular copper does not meet the boundary: outline and footprint-keepout verdicts are `inconclusive`, and real KiCad 10.0.5 reports zero corresponding provider violations. The existing core-crossing outline and keepout fixtures remain `violated` and retain their corresponding KiCad errors. Rule tests independently force the envelope/core gap for region keep-in and keep-out and an asymmetric future envelope for exact centre readers. Sixteen committed mutants are killed, 0 survive, 0 `not_run`; Python 3.12.13 on macOS-26.5.2-arm64, baseline return code 0, spec `sha256:5ad7ce234135bc561518c9720b2b286fe2a8a65e1850ee6843b4493efba6f3f0`. |
| Claim | At D-206, for these 13 converting boards and the two named KiCad provider classes, CopperMCP publishes no `violated` verdict absent from two agreeing KiCad DRC invocations; real edge crossings and keepout intrusions remain violations; and the previously false-refused three batches are previewable with the uncertainty disclosed. |
| Non-claims | Not exact pad-polygon parity, global fabrication containment, all-proven placement, DRC cleanliness, custom-pad support, a conversion increase, routing quality, third-party-board conversion or corpus representativeness. `inconclusive` may correspond to either endpoint and is not a pass claim. No before/after DRC differential is used; the board bytes and relevant KiCad answers are unchanged, while only CopperMCP's interpretation changes. No corpus board was written. |
| Records | [ADR-0110](../adr/0110-placement-boundary-verdicts-bracket-kicad-parity.md), [D-206](decision-ledger.md), [R-159](risk-register.md), [issue #187](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/187), [KiCad 10.0.5 source](https://gitlab.com/kicad/code/kicad/-/blob/10.0.5/pcbnew/drc/drc_test_provider_edge_clearance.cpp#L172-443), [`2026-08-15-placement-direction-brackets.json`](../mutants/2026-08-15-placement-direction-brackets.json), B-112 above |
