### B-120 — B-088 external candidate disposer corpus closure

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question and predeclared criterion | Re-express every one of B-088's 70 routed candidates through the production disposer. Both halves are mandatory: all 70 unperturbed patches accepted, and a fixed 1 nm obstacle incursion, dropped middle segment, wrong-pad endpoint and undeclared-layer via refused with four distinct typed codes. Acceptance alone is not a pass. |
| Representability finding | The pre-run probe found v1 can represent **0/70** source candidates without deleting topology: all 70 nets are multi-pin, every patch carries 2–20 paths, and no emitted path is a pad-centre-to-pad-centre v1 route. ADR-0113 therefore preserves v1 and adds the closed multi-path v2 rather than laundering one leg into a candidate. |
| Dataset and method | The same committed MIT 20-board prefix, adapter, fixed 250,000 nm grid policy, seed and request ceilings as B-088. Re-run the deterministic reference proposal for every attempted net; for each of its 70 routed outputs, serialize only the closed v2 input in memory, verify it against the immutable imported snapshot and trusted request, and retain source candidate ID, document digest, redacted result and result digest. Candidate documents and geometry are not committed. |
| Result | **70/70 accepted.** Fixed perturbations return `obstacle_violation`, `discontinuous_path`, `endpoint_mismatch` and `undeclared_layer`, respectively. The input set and result set are independently digest-bound; every result states `physical_validation: not_run`. Four committed mutation controls were killed with zero survivors or stale anchors (`docs/mutants/2026-08-17-external-route-patch-v2.json`, SHA-256 `1c007a57014848cd24f1adf1e0f07ef82800d16448ae4e9b5cf7cd96a863adac`). |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-17-external-candidate-disposer-corpus-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-17-external-candidate-disposer-corpus-v1.json), self-digested and replayed by `tests/test_benchmark_external_candidate_disposer.py`. The artifact contains no coordinates, segments, paths, vias, board bytes or apply token. |
| Claim | For the 70 B-088 single-layer patches under their recorded Board IR snapshots and budgets, the production core accepts every unmodified topology and distinguishes the four predeclared corruptions. This closes #99's first corpus-calibrated Board-IR disposer slice. |
| Non-claims | Not KiCad DRC, physical validation, electrical, SI/PI/thermal/DFM or fabrication correctness; not whole-board compatibility because the 70 routes were proposed independently; not MCP/CLI availability, persistence, apply, repair or mutation authority; not generalisation beyond this corpus. Issue #99 remains open for authoritative DRC and public intake. |
| Records | [ADR-0113](../adr/0113-external-route-patches-preserve-multi-pin-topology.md), [D-210](decision-ledger.md), [R-162](risk-register.md), [SEC-151](security-ledger.md), [issue #99](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/99) |

Exact 500,000 is admitted; 500,001 returns `invalid_request` before bounds or hashing; an aliased 11,000,000 aggregate returns `obstacle_check_budget_exceeded` before vertex access. The committed harness killed 5/5 with zero survivors or stale anchors. |

### B-124 — whole-board negotiated routing census on the external corpus

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question and predeclared stop rule | Issue #90's remaining gate is held-out evidence for ADR-0117's opt-in local-repair transaction, and that transaction fires from exactly one place: the whole-set physical-clearance gate returning `CLEARANCE_VIOLATION` on a **complete** allocation with **at least two** violating nets. Nobody had ever run `negotiate_routes` whole-board on a real corpus — only synthetic or single-fixture runs (B-036, B-058, B-063, B-087 and B-095; B-095's 16 corpus boards are routed **net by net**, and its `negotiate_routes` calls use a synthetic congested snapshot) — so the precondition's frequency was unmeasured and possibly zero. The stop rule was fixed before the run: **if 0 of 20 boards reach the precondition, no repair-enablement or tuning slice follows and #90 re-parks**; if one or more do, those boards name the follow-on differential's population. Predeclared per board: terminal status, unrouted set, iterations, rip-ups, physical pair checks, and whether the run terminates in the exact precondition. |
| Environment and method | Apple arm64, macOS 26.5.2, Python 3.12.13. The 20 committed MIT SimpleRouteJson boards (`dwiel/tscircuit-benchmark` at `be36518b5bf51755dae92c230061ab3cf4e3e063`, © 2026 Zach Dwiel), each verified against the digest manifest before import. **Repair is never enabled**: `repair_settings` is not passed, so the census measures how often the *other three* conjuncts hold under the legacy coordinator. Each board submits its B-088-routable nets as one whole-board envelope at B-088's unchanged fixed 250,000 nm step, seed 0 and request ceilings, under declared envelope budgets (8 iterations, 20,000,000/5,000,000 nm penalties, 2,000,000 expansions, 10,000,000 obstacle checks, 2,000,000 physical checks). The precondition is **observed, not inferred from the terminal status**: a pure pass-through recorder is installed on the coordinator's physical-gate symbol, and every board is *additionally* run uninstrumented with the two published projections required to be identical. Admissibility is decided twice — once by an independent predicate over public snapshot data, once by the coordinator — and a disagreement raises. The re-derived submitted set is checked board-for-board against the self-digest-verified B-088 artifact. Two replays per configuration agreed exactly. No KiCad, network, private board or DRC was invoked. |
| Result | **0 of 20 boards reach the repair precondition, and the coordinator makes 0 physical-gate calls, because 0 of 20 boards are admitted at all.** Every board terminates before the first router call. 16 boards are refused by the coordinator at `each negotiated net must expose exactly two pads on the selected layer`; the other 4 carry fewer than two reference-routed nets and cannot form a two-request envelope. The declared **two-pad control** — every exactly-two-pad net, regardless of reference routability — moves the block to the other conjunct: 6 boards form an envelope and all 6 are refused at `all negotiated pad centres must share one world-coordinate grid`, the remaining 14 carrying fewer than two such nets. **The two populations are disjoint.** All 70 nets B-088 routed have three or more pads, and all 36 exactly-two-pad nets in the corpus refused — they are precisely B-088's 36 `off_grid` refusals, so `two_pad_nets_the_reference_routed` is **0 of 36**. The negotiated router's first number on real boards is therefore **0 nets completed against the per-net reference baseline's 70 of 117**, and that zero is an admission refusal, not a route. |
| Direction of error | The recorder sees the candidate tuple handed to the gate but not the connection map, so completeness is tested with the solo reference's `already_connected` count as an **upper bound** on connections. Over-counting connections can only make the completeness conjunct *easier* to satisfy, so a recorded zero cannot be an artefact of the bound. Under the primary configuration the bound is exact: every submitted net produced a candidate in the solo reference, so none can be already connected. |
| Mutation evidence | `docs/mutants/2026-08-20-negotiated-corpus-census.json`, SHA-256 `f400bab3c4a737fad8c806ba52c5101a4288e7555821bea9e0df309d2f0c209f`, seven mutants over the harness's own guards: treat any gate failure as a clearance violation, drop the completeness conjunct, relax the two-violating-net conjunct to one, remove the B-088 baseline drift check, remove the observer-effect control, remove the admission cross-check, and stop checking refusal attribution against the coordinator's diagnostic. The committed harness killed 7/7 with zero survivors or stale anchors. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-20-negotiated-corpus-census-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-20-negotiated-corpus-census-v1.json), self-digested run `sha256:c72cf0a6061b90efb15cf7e61701e4caebd0e0baeb2311e6017c7ef43d6b5df2`; runner and reference-runner hashes are bound in the artifact and the whole `metrics` block is replayed against a fresh run by `tests/test_benchmark_negotiated_corpus_census.py`. Because the corpus reaches no gate call, that suite proves the instrument could have reported a positive: it exercises the top rung of the stage ladder with constructed observations and proves the recorder observes a real gate call, and changes nothing, on the committed two-net KiCad crossing fixture. |
| Claim | At the recorded grid policy and declared budgets, the negotiated coordinator's first-slice admission contract accepts **no board** of this corpus, so the local-repair transaction has no input to fire on and its precondition frequency is measured zero rather than assumed. **The predeclared stop rule fires**: no repair-enablement or tuning slice follows, and issue #90 re-parks on this row. What would change the answer is named rather than attempted — a negotiated slice that admits multi-pin nets, or a corpus whose two-pad nets sit on one shared world grid at a common step. |
| Non-claims | This measures the coordinator's behaviour at these budgets on this corpus. It is **not a routing-quality claim** about the negotiated coordinator, the reference router, or any other router, and it **supports no claim about local repair until repair runs** — no repaired candidate was constructed, validated or published. The 0-versus-70 comparison is not a like-for-like completion result: B-088 routes each net independently against the unrouted snapshot, so its candidates are not mutually compatible, while a negotiated envelope must satisfy one shared allocation. No grid step, budget, layer selection or net-selection rule was tuned to move a number; the two-pad control was added after an exploratory probe and is declared as a control, and it can only add a refusal reason, never convert a refusal into a route. Not KiCad DRC, electrical, SI/PI/thermal/DFM, fabrication or hardware evidence, and no generalisation beyond LLM-generated 2-layer tscircuit boards or from the committed 20-board subset to the full 36. No board was read, written, applied or opened. |
| Records | [D-218](decision-ledger.md), [ADR-0117](../adr/0117-local-exact-repair-is-an-opt-in-verified-transaction.md), [integration gate](../research/exact-local-repair-negotiated-integration-gate.md), B-088 and B-087 above, [issue #90](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/90) |
| Amendment — 2026-08-29, mutation anchors re-run against the successor runner | The historical artifact and its 7/7 result are unchanged. This slice strengthened the same runner's B-088 guard from routed-count equality to complete per-board authority and strengthened recorder parity from a public projection to the complete immutable result, so the committed spec's exact strings and four killing-test names could not truthfully remain frozen at their old locations. The spec was re-anchored rather than waived and two missing behavior-specific attribution tests were restored. Current spec SHA-256 `147b572084fd449424d918b77b94c075e1a49103770857852d33640961f49439`; standard `scripts/mutation_harness.py` run on Python 3.12.13 / macOS-26.5.2-arm64-arm-64bit, green baseline return code 0: **7 killed, 0 survivors, 0 stale anchors, 0 invalid runs**. Every application was followed by a byte-identical restoration; B-124's artifact was not regenerated. |

### B-129 — fixed-point masking census over public and private counterfactual cohorts

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question | After B-114/B-118 showed that first-error diagnostics mask later gates, how deep is the stack when exactly three already-declared direct-root classes are removed in immutable memory: `Edge.Cuts` curves, copper text, and root dimensions? Measure ten pinned public upstream boards plus three private topology controls, stop at every other construct, and do not turn a counterfactual into product support. |

Six public KiCad 10.0.5 re-saves reproduced B-114's derived digest exactly; four were newly reserialized and are therefore a new derived cohort, not an exact B-114 replay. No manifest, board, per-board digest, path, name, geometry, or transformed bytes are committed. |

The runner captures each source once through the bounded no-follow workspace reader, verifies the closed 10-public/3-private manifest and independently pinned fingerprint, repeatedly converts, removes one whole matching predeclared root class by CST spans in memory, and re-reads all sources before publication. Unknown, nested, setup, layer, footprint, courtyard, byte-offset, and ambiguous diagnostics stop as `unmaskable`. Work is capped at 16 class passes, 64 MiB per board, and a 64 KiB manifest. |
| Result | **0 of 13 convert after the allowed masks.** Public depths are 0:1, 1:2, 2:5, 3:2; terminal public blockers are setup semantics 6, copper-layer kind 1, footprint graphic layer 1, graphic layer arity 1, and root semantic construct 1. All three private controls stop at depth 0: two courtyard-topology refusals and one disjoint-outline refusal. The dominant newly exposed public direction is therefore a setup-field census, not implementation of curves, text, or dimensions. |

The aggregate artifact is self-digested as `sha256:b07e887fafacd654948eb95470abc9008a1ee4614aa1cef91fd8560696c6165e` and binds runner digest `sha256:fb0f53a397ea7e9f6290c01153c8fa8698496472ba86c8f69a95303e9be506fe`. ### B-130 — the closed public setup-field surface behind B-129's six terminals

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question | B-129 named `setup_semantics` as 6 of 10 public terminal blockers but could say nothing about *what* those boards write, because the adapter's refusal names the block and not the field. Before deciding anything, measure the closed field surface: which `setup` children the six carry, how often, on how many boards, and whether anything falls outside a vocabulary declared in advance. |
| Cohort | The **exact** B-129 13-board cohort, replayed rather than re-derived: the same digest-bound saves under the same predeclared fingerprint `sha256:bfec8210d6d4eb746ffdbfb3b70309ce`, all 13 manifest digests re-verified byte-for-byte before the run. This is a stronger replay than the recorded refetch method, which produces derived bytes: no board was refetched, re-saved, or reserialized, so the four saves B-129 flagged as newly reserialized are the same four bytes here. The six measured boards are selected by independently rerunning B-129's own fixed-point classifier and keeping exactly the entries whose terminal is `unmaskable` with blocker `setup_semantics`. |
| Method | Clean commit `09bdf7b1214988568c7fdd39061644fec97771dc`; Python 3.12.13 on macOS arm64; KiCad was not invoked. The instrument merged in #216 was run for the first time here. Its `PREDECLARED_SETUP_SELECTION_COMMITMENT` was **unassigned**, so it refused to run at all; it is assigned in this branch to the observed selection and the constant is a **freeze, not a prediction** — it binds *which six*, so a later rerun over a drifted classifier or a swapped manifest row fails rather than silently re-aggregating a different population. The runner asserts its accepted-vocabulary constant equals the adapter's `_SETUP_METADATA_HEADS`, re-reads every source after measurement, and publishes through an anchored, no-follow, create-exclusive staged link. |
| The field set, and it is closed | **`other` is 0 at all three levels** — direct setup children, stackup children, and stackup-layer children — so the predeclared vocabulary covers everything these six boards write, and the surface is a closed set rather than a sample of one. Layer children: `type` 86 (6/6), `thickness` 62 (6/6), `material` 24 (6/6), `epsilon_r` 24 (6/6), `loss_tangent` 24 (6/6), `color` 12 (3/6). Every direct and nested field is single-atom except `aux_axis_origin`/`grid_origin` (two atoms) and the container heads. |

Self-digested `sha256:f06de27dd41128e6c54e4a2fe808d34fecbb87e251d97b801cacff266059158c`, binding runner digest `sha256:41e2240cf507040c6003b5f592b695351919e52d4d65b35fb9e7ae704686718c`. |

### B-131 — what accepting the setup fields bought, measured against B-129 with the same instrument

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question and predeclared prediction | Before implementing D-227, and recorded in the pull request before the differential was run: (1) `setup_semantics` public terminals **6 → 0**; (2) **0 of 13 convert**, unchanged; (3) the six **redistribute inside B-129's existing closed blocker vocabulary**, named guess `footprint_graphic_layer` for the majority, then `root_semantic_construct`, then `graphic_layer_arity`; (4) public gate-stack depths shift **deeper** for the six; (5) the three private controls unchanged. Two further quantities were measured before the prediction and are labelled as measured, not predicted: direct public conversion 0/10, and the frozen own 18-save corpus 15/18. |
| Method | **The same instrument, byte-for-byte, at two source commits.** `scripts/benchmark_fixed_point_masking_census.py` is unmodified by this branch and its recorded runner digest `sha256:fb0f53a397ea7e9f6290c01153c8fa8698496472ba86c8f69a95303e9be506fe` is identical to B-129's; the cohort is B-129's exact 13 digest-bound saves under the same predeclared fingerprint `sha256:bfec8210d6d4eb746ffdbfb3b70309ce`, re-verified before the run. Only the adapter moved, from `1de5ca7` to `78f9a29`. Python 3.12.13 on macOS arm64; KiCad was not invoked; clean worktree enforced by the runner before and after. Conversion of the unmasked cohort and of the frozen own corpus was measured separately by a scratch probe on the same commits, source SHA-256 re-checked and unchanged. |
| Direct result — **no board converts, and this slice never could have made one** | **Public 0/10 before and after. Own frozen corpus 15/18 before and after**, with `unmodelled_setup_field_count` and `unmodelled_stackup_layer_count` **0 on every one of the 15**, because no board in that corpus writes any of the five accepted heads. Every one of the ten public boards refuses **in front of** `setup` — eight at `Edge.Cuts` outline curves, one at copper text, one at copper layer kind — so no first refusal moves. The setup surface is reachable only behind B-129's deliberately unsound masks, and that was stated before the work rather than discovered after it. |
| Masked differential — predictions 1, 2 and 5 met | `terminal_blocker: setup_semantics` **6 → 0** on the public cohort. **0 of 13 convert**, unchanged. The three private controls are byte-identical: depth 0, two `courtyard_topology`, one `disjoint_outline_topology`, and none writes a newly accepted head. |
| Predictions 3 and 4 are **refuted**, and the refutation is the finding | The six did **not** redistribute into the existing vocabulary and the depths did **not** move. Public `gate_stack_depth` is byte-identical before and after — `{0:1, 1:2, 2:5, 3:2}` — and all six land in the census's `other` bucket, which was 0 before. A scratch read of the terminal diagnostics shows why: all six now stop at **`footprint contains an unsupported semantic field`** at `kicad_pcb.footprint[N].unsupported`, unanimously, at exactly the depth they previously stopped at. **The setup wall was one instance of a structural class, not a construct**: a container-level allowlist refusal whose diagnostic names the container and no field, which is precisely why B-129 could not mask it. The next wall has the identical shape one level down, in `footprint`, and is exactly as unmaskable for exactly the same reason. The other four boards are unchanged: `root_semantic_construct`, `graphic_layer_arity`, `footprint_graphic_layer`, `copper_layer_kind`, one each. |
| The instrument reported the gap rather than hiding it | `other: 6` is the closed blocker vocabulary correctly declining to name a class it does not have a member for. A census that had bucketed the new terminal into the nearest existing class would have reported a redistribution that did not happen, and the prediction above would have looked met. The `other` bucket is why it does not. |
| Integrity | Source hashes unchanged across both runs and `committed_board_bytes` is 0. The differential artifact is self-digested `sha256:622a0e167612496068f4d644e0bbbc75dba005ff4c70e7d1835cdec1861048e1`, at commit `78f9a29dc1aab3b13d3bade263fefb5084b49a40`. The branch head moved past that commit afterwards, and the delta is **test-only** — one added assertion pinning the counted setup heads against the accepted ones — so no measured behaviour is downstream of evidence taken before it. Said rather than left for a reviewer to diff. Contract evidence for the change itself: unit tests in both directions per field class — accepted-and-counted, accepted-with-zero-counts, refused-at-its-own-field, explicitly-neutral-accepted, closed grammar at all three levels, `zone_defaults` still refused, the repeated-head asymmetry, and the post-machining premise pinned on pads and on vias. **17 mutants, 17 killed, 0 survived, 0 `stale_anchor`, 0 `invalid_run`**, baseline return code 0, spec `sha256:cd695e816113cd76b4b5622c5ecee8735d3f54b220cf97394fdf195d3522503f`. One mutant survived the first run — restoring the census's old equality guard — because the killing test compared constants and never ran the guard; the coverage gap was real and a test that exercises `measure` closed it, rather than the claim being re-labelled. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-28-setup-acceptance-masking-differential-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-28-setup-acceptance-masking-differential-v1.json); runner [`benchmark_fixed_point_masking_census.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_fixed_point_masking_census.py), unmodified from B-129. Mutation spec [`2026-08-28-setup-field-semantics.json`](../mutants/2026-08-28-setup-field-semantics.json). |
| Claim | On this exact cohort and with the same instrument, D-227 removes `setup_semantics` from the public terminal set entirely (6 → 0) and converts **no board**, publicly or privately. The measured successor is a single class on all six, one structural level down. |
| Non-claims | **Not a conversion win, and not a first third-party board.** Not a claim that the six are now nearer conversion by any measure other than one gate; the depths did not move, so this slice bought a gate and not a layer. Not a claim about the four unaffected boards. Not a measurement of the footprint field surface — the successor is named from the terminal diagnostic, and what those footprints actually carry is the next census's question, not this row's. `other: 6` is an honest absence of a vocabulary member, not a new blocker class definition. Nothing routed, DRC-checked, applied, written or fabricated; no masked board is valid. |

### B-132 — the closed public footprint-field surface behind B-131's six terminals

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question | B-131 measured all six former `setup_semantics` boards landing on `footprint contains an unsupported semantic field`, at **unchanged** depth, and named the shape: a container-level allowlist whose refusal names the container and no field, which is precisely why B-129 could not mask it. B-131 explicitly declined to say what those footprints carry. This row answers that and nothing more: which `footprint` children the six write outside the adapter's accepted set, how often, on how many boards, what values the copper-bearing ones carry, and whether anything falls outside a vocabulary declared in advance. |
| Cohort | The **exact** B-129/B-130 13-board cohort, replayed rather than re-derived: the same digest-bound saves under the same predeclared fingerprint `sha256:bfec8210d6d4eb746ffdbfb3b70309ce`, all 13 digests re-verified byte-for-byte before the run, no board refetched or reserialized. **That these are B-130's six is checked, not asserted**: the selection rule differs — B-129's closed blocker vocabulary has no member for this wall and correctly reports `other` — so the instrument recomputes the *setup* census's commitment, with that census's own function under its own domain, over the boards this one selected, and requires equality with its frozen constant. `same_cohort_as_setup_census: true` is that check's result. |
| Method | Clean commit `71e9a2a2146f6306fb06095016a1a7ca6fee4ae4`; Python 3.12.13 on macOS arm64; KiCad was not invoked. A **sibling instrument**, not a parameterized one, and the reason is recorded rather than left implicit: the setup census's freeze story rests on its selection rule being a constant in the file whose digest the artifact binds, so parameterizing the rule would leave the commitment saying only "these six entries" and no longer "these six, selected this way". Output plumbing, path resolution and the shape helper are *imported* from that census rather than restated, so the create-exclusive anchored no-follow publish is the same already-tested code. Selection does not merely take B-129's `other` bucket — a catch-all that would admit any future unnamed terminal: it re-walks the same mask loop from `benchmark_fixed_point_masking_census`'s own `_convert`/`_mask_first_blocker`, requires the terminal diagnostic to be the footprint wall at a `kicad_pcb.footprint[N].unsupported` locator, and requires the walk's depth to agree with the classifier's. Sources re-read unchanged after measurement. |
| The vocabulary is derived from the grammar, not from the boards | `REFUSED_FOOTPRINT_HEADS` is the **union of the top-level `case T_…` arms of `PCB_IO_KICAD_SEXPR_PARSER::parseFOOTPRINT_unchecked` on the KiCad `9.0` and `10.0` release branches** (47 arms on 9.0, 54 on 10.0), minus the accepted heads, minus the layer-routed `fp_*`/`property`/`point` branch, minus `zone`. 28 heads, of which **20 occur zero times and are reported at zero**. A vocabulary read off the cohort could not have reported an absence; this one does. Three `master`-only (11.0-development) heads are deliberately excluded, so a board written by that version would land in `other` rather than be silently covered. |
| The field set, and it is closed | **`other` is 0 at both levels** — direct footprint children and footprint-local group children — so the predeclared vocabulary covers everything these six boards write. Across **794 footprints**: `sheetfile` **6/6 boards** (748), `sheetname` **5/6** (707), `solder_paste_margin` 1/6 (17), `solder_paste_margin_ratio` 1/6 (4), `group` 1/6 (4), `zone_connect` 1/6 (4), `clearance` 1/6 (1), `solder_mask_margin` 1/6 (1). By class, board presence: `provenance` 6, `stencil_mask` 3, **`copper_interacting` 2**, `grouping` 1, `own_geometry` **0**, `other` **0**. Every direct refused field is single-atom except `group`, which is `mixed` on all four. |
| The values the copper-bearing fields carry, which is the decision's input | Six closed payload **predicates**, never payloads -- the artifact records `atom_values_committed: 0`. Each head's buckets are a **total partition of its occurrences**, and `measure` reconciles each partition's sum against that head's occurrence count. **`zone_connect_attaching: 4`, `zone_connect_detaching: 0`, `zone_connect_invalid: 0`**: all four occurrences carry an attaching mode, `0` -- the one written value that detaches a pad from its pour -- is absent, and nothing is malformed or outside the written domain, so the partition ADR-0091 draws one level down on a pad transfers intact. **`clearance_zero: 0`, `clearance_nonzero: 1`, `clearance_invalid: 0`**: the single `clearance` occurrence is well formed and is **not** the inert zero that `DRC_ENGINE::EvalRules`' `if( override_val )` guard falls through, so a narrowed acceptance admitting only zero would clear **no** board on this cohort. That is the measurement that made the narrowed option declinable on evidence rather than on taste. |
| The predicate vocabulary was widened in review, and the reason is the row's own standard | **Codex review of [#226](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/pull/226) (thread `PRRT_kwDOTrPIR86c_upw`) found the first version of this instrument violating the rule this ledger applies everywhere else.** It emitted `clearance_zero` or *nothing*, so a malformed payload -- `(clearance garbage)`, `(clearance nan)`, `(clearance "0")`, `(clearance)`, `(clearance 0 0)`, a nested child -- produced **no predicate at all** and was indistinguishable in the artifact from a well-formed non-zero clearance; `zone_connect` had the same gap outside its four written modes. An absence is evidence only if the observation could have reported a presence, and that observation could not. The vocabulary went from three buckets to six, every occurrence now lands in exactly one, and the partition sum is checked rather than trusted. NaN is handled explicitly rather than incidentally: `float("nan") == 0.0` is `False`, so the float-first implementation would have filed `(clearance nan)` as an ordinary non-zero clearance. **Re-run by the recorded procedure at the fixed instrument: not one pre-existing count moved** -- `clearance_zero` 0, `zone_connect_attaching` 4, `zone_connect_detaching` 0 -- and every other aggregate is identical, so this row's claim is unchanged. What changed is that `clearance_nonzero: 1` is now *counted* where the row previously had to infer it from `clearance_zero: 0` against an occurrence count of 1, and the two `_invalid` zeros are now meaningful rather than vacuous. |
| Inside a footprint-local `group` | 4 nodes on 1 board, two child heads only: `members` (4, `many_atoms`) and `uuid` (4, `one_atom`). **`locked`, `lib_id` and the pre-KiCad-8 `id` spelling occur 0 times on 0 boards** — the child that carries a real constraint is absent from this cohort, which is what makes the existing root-group refusal on it cost nothing here. |
| Integrity | Source hashes re-verified unchanged after measurement and `committed_board_bytes` is 0. Aggregate-only by construction: `atom_values_committed`, `board_identities_committed`, `board_paths_committed` and `board_digests_committed` are all 0, so no margin, coordinate, sheet name, file name, UUID or board identity is published. Self-digested `sha256:d545e4c0ea664b184837636738798539f92bfe122fcd22681e6860834a9a8366`, binding runner digest `sha256:9e861922c4763f72b028f78f10ac5b4322c6bca32c847c56bdf61d058ac803eb` -- both re-taken after the review widening below, at commit `71e9a2a2146f6306fb06095016a1a7ca6fee4ae4`. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-28-public-footprint-field-census-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-28-public-footprint-field-census-v1.json); runner [`benchmark_public_footprint_field_census.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_public_footprint_field_census.py). |

Nothing outside the predeclared KiCad-9/10 grammar appears. |
| Non-claims | **No footprint-field support, and no conversion.** A field's presence is not a decision to accept it and this row implies none; direction of error is [D-228](decision-ledger.md)'s question, not this instrument's. Not a claim about any board's *first* refusal — all ten public boards refuse in front of `footprint`, so this surface is reachable only behind B-129's deliberately unsound masks. **Not a measurement of the pad-level allowlist** one structural level further down, which is a different instance of the same class and is not touched here. Not a sample: six boards selected for licence clarity and then for one terminal. Not a claim that `other: 0` holds outside this cohort — a seventh board may write a head this vocabulary buckets as `other`, which is exactly what the bucket exists to report; nor that it holds for a KiCad 11 board, whose three new heads are deliberately outside the vocabulary. No board write, apply authority, editor mutation, routing, DRC, or fabrication result. |

### B-134 — the `Edge.Cuts` curve surface behind eight of ten public first refusals

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Question | B-114 counted **97** `Edge.Cuts` curve primitives across ten licence-clean public boards and said no more about them than "arc, circle and bezier", because the adapter's refusal named a category rather than a construct. Eight of the ten stop there and nowhere earlier, so it is the front gate of issue #188. Before deciding anything, measure the closed surface: which curve heads the boards write, in what payload shape, whether the shapes chain into a board at all once arcs count as edges, and — the question [ADR-0076](../adr/0076-segment-assembled-edge-cuts-outline.md) left open — which way each arc bends relative to the board it bounds. |
| Cohort | The **exact** B-129/B-130/B-132 13-board cohort, replayed rather than re-derived: the same digest-bound saves under the same predeclared fingerprint `sha256:bfec8210d6d4eb746ffdbfb3b70309ce`, all 13 digests re-verified byte-for-byte before the run, no board refetched or reserialized. Ten public, three private; only the ten public boards are measured here. |
| Method | Read-only census on a clean tree. Every measurement is exact integer or rational arithmetic — circumcentres as `Fraction`s, the half-turn test as one integer dot product, chaining by **exact** endpoint coincidence with zero epsilon — so no floating-point tolerance enters any verdict. Chaining walks `gr_line` and `gr_arc` together as edges of one cycle, because a chord and its arc share endpoints and topology cannot depend on the bend. Convex/concave is read from the chord ring's signed area and is therefore reported only for the boards whose chords close. Sources re-read unchanged after measurement. |
| Head census — **the 97 are all arcs** | `gr_arc` **97**, `gr_line` 123, and **zero** `gr_circle`, `gr_curve`, `gr_bezier` or `gr_poly` on `Edge.Cuts` on any of the ten. Board presence: `gr_line` 10/10, `gr_arc` 9/10. One board additionally carries footprint-local `Edge.Cuts` graphics (6 `fp_line`, 3 `fp_arc`), which a different gate refuses. B-114's "arc, circle and bezier" was a category name, not a measurement, and this resolves it. |
| Payload grammar | Closed and uniform across all 97: `start`, `mid`, `end` (two atoms, no children each), `stroke` (no atoms, two children), `layer` and `uuid` (one atom each). **Zero positional atoms on any of the 97.** `stroke.type` is `solid` 61 times and `default` 36. Nothing writes the legacy scalar `width`, and nothing writes `angle`. |
| Topology — **the arc gate is not the only outline wall** | Counting arcs as edges, **7 of 10** boards form exactly one closed non-branching loop. Three do not, and the separations say why: two are sub-micron near-misses whose smallest unpaired-endpoint separations are **17 nm** and **19 nm**, and one is a genuine **69.6 mm** opening. KiCad would chain the first two under its own `aChainingEpsilon`; this adapter refuses them, because closing a gap adds area no drawn shape encloses (ADR-0076). Saying so with a number is the point of this row. |
| Direction of bend — **13 of 51 cut into the board** | Of the 51 arcs on the seven boards that close: **all 51 minor** (span at most a half turn), **all 51 with rational, non-integer circumcentres**, radii 1.0–5.0 mm, sweeps 45°–90°, and **38 convex** against **13 concave**, the 13 falling on exactly two boards. A convex arc may be replaced by a polyline inside the chord-and-arc region; a concave one may not, and this is the first time anyone knew whether the cohort had any. |
| Pre-code prediction | Recorded in `PREDECLARATION.md`, `sha256:29c3336173a47e5b0674151487c88de48188540ed0fb1bcb2bc6913aec6ff2f8`, written and hashed against a **clean** worktree at base `bdd6589c85ebf9bc80d7bfcdebbb3e624eda1770` before one line of the adapter was touched. A chord-substitution probe — every root `Edge.Cuts` `gr_arc` replaced in memory by a `gr_line` between the same drawn endpoints, the coarsest ring the planned implementation can produce — measured **0/10 converting**, and predicted per board where each of the eight would land instead: six on copper text, one on root dimension objects, one on a root unsupported semantic construct. Predicted also that no board reaches the outline topology walls above, and that concave-arc support would therefore buy **zero** conversions. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-28-public-edge-cuts-curve-census-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-28-public-edge-cuts-curve-census-v1.json) — self-digested, cohort fingerprint re-verified, aggregate-only, `committed_board_bytes: 0`. |
| Claim | On these ten boards at this commit: every `Edge.Cuts` curve primitive is a `gr_arc`; their payload grammar is the six heads above with no positional atom; seven boards' `Edge.Cuts` shapes chain into exactly one closed loop by exact coincidence and three do not; and of the 51 arcs on those seven, all are minor, 38 bulge away from the board and 13 cut into it. |
| Non-claims | **No support for any measured construct, and no conversion.** A construct's presence is not a decision to accept it; direction of error is [D-229](decision-ledger.md)'s question, not this instrument's. Not a claim about the 46 arcs on the three non-closing boards, whose direction is undefined without a consistent interior. Not a claim about footprint-local `Edge.Cuts` graphics beyond their count — they are a frame-transform problem, not an approximation one. **Not a sample of anything**: ten boards selected for licence clarity and KiCad-native development, which is a selection on the property under test. No board write, apply authority, routing, DRC or fabrication result. |
| Records | B-114, B-118, [B-129](#b-129--fixed-point-masking-census-over-public-and-private-counterfactual-cohorts), [B-135](#b-135--what-inscribing-the-outline-arcs-bought-measured-against-the-base-commit-with-the-same-instrument), [D-229](decision-ledger.md), [R-180](risk-register.md), [SEC-166](security-ledger.md), [ADR-0124](../adr/0124-an-outline-arc-is-inscribed-and-a-cut-is-refused.md), [research note](../research/kicad-edge-cuts-outline-arcs-v1.md), [issue #188](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/188) |

### B-135 — what inscribing the outline arcs bought, measured against the base commit with the same instrument

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Question and predeclared prediction | Recorded in `PREDECLARATION.md` (`sha256:29c3336173a47e5b0674151487c88de48188540ed0fb1bcb2bc6913aec6ff2f8`) against a clean worktree **before the adapter was touched**, and deliberately predicting *against* a conversion, because B-114's pass-two result is the null hypothesis: (1) public conversion **0/10**, unchanged — **no first third-party conversion**; (2) the `Edge.Cuts` curve gate is **gone from all eight** boards that stopped there, each moving to a construct behind it, named per board — six to copper text, one to root dimension objects, one to a root unsupported semantic construct; (3) **no board reaches the outline topology walls** B-134 found, because `_semantic_preflight` refuses in front of the outline assembly; (4) concave-arc support would therefore buy **zero** measured conversions; (5) the frozen own 18-save corpus stays **15/18** with the new `outline_inward_deviation_nm` **0 on all 15**; (6) **no committed Board IR snapshot digest moves**; (7) the three private controls unchanged. Two quantities were measured before the prediction and are labelled measured, not predicted: direct public conversion **0/10** and the frozen own corpus **15/18**, both taken on `origin/main`. |
| Method | **The same conversion call at two source commits, with no mask and no substitution.** Unlike B-118/B-119/B-129, nothing here is deleted or replaced in memory: the adapter itself changed, so the differential is a real before/after rather than a counterfactual. Base `bdd6589c85ebf9bc80d7bfcdebbb3e624eda1770` was measured in its own detached worktree; the cohort is the same 13 digest-bound saves under fingerprint `sha256:bfec8210d6d4eb746ffdbfb3b70309ce`, re-verified before each run. Python 3.12.13 on macOS arm64; KiCad was not invoked. The own-corpus regression uses `select_frozen_corpus`'s 18-save selection with its predeclared material fingerprint `sha256:afe5d9d09b4aa89ffa8d5ae84df284ee` re-checked and found unchanged. |
| Direct result — **the gate is gone, and no board converts** | **Public 0/10 before and after.** Before: **8** boards refuse at *"Edge.Cuts outline arcs, circles and curves are unsupported"*, one at copper text, one at copper layer kind — exactly B-114's split. After: **0** boards refuse at any `Edge.Cuts` outline construct, and the first-refusal histogram is **6× copper text**, 1× root dimension objects, 1× root unsupported semantic construct, 1× footprint graphic without one explicit layer, 1× copper layer kind. Predictions 1, 2, 3, 4 and 7 met; the per-board destinations match the predeclared list board for board. |
| **There is no first third-party conversion, and this row says so plainly** | Six of the eight boards land on [ADR-0095](../adr/0095-copper-text-has-no-derivable-envelope.md)'s copper-text refusal — the wall B-114 predicted on 2026-08-14 and B-118 measured behind a mask. This slice replaces that mask with a real, proved implementation and reaches the same wall. **A capability that converts nothing is still a capability**, and the honest headline is that its measured aggregate is indistinguishable from the previous slice's; predicting that in advance is the only thing separating it from a null result nobody expected. |
| Own corpus and identity stability | **15/18 before and after**, with `outline_inward_deviation_nm` **0 on every one of the 15** — no board in that corpus draws an `Edge.Cuts` arc — and the three refusals unchanged and unrelated (one `disjoint_outline_topology` on phono-v2, two `courtyard_topology`). **No committed snapshot digest moves and no golden identity pin changes**, because nothing new enters `BoardIRSnapshot`: predictions 5 and 6 met. |
| What did move, and it is not in the aggregate | Five refusal messages are new or changed, and one is retired. A concave arc, a major arc, an `Edge.Cuts` circle, a Bézier and a polygon each now refuse with their own sentence; the shared *"Edge.Cuts outline arcs, circles and curves are unsupported"* is gone. A board with a rounded rectangular outline converts where none did before, which is visible in the own corpus and the public cohort only as the *absence* of a gate. |
| Evidence for the change itself | Both-direction unit tests per surface: a convex arc inscribed and every vertex checked against a **hand-derived** disc rather than any polygon the code produced; a concave arc, a major arc and four closed-shape heads each refused by name; the arc payload grammar refused in three malformation classes; a 17 nm endpoint near-miss refused; two shapes on one pair of endpoints refused; a self-crossing chord ring refused *before* its orientation is read; a segment-only outline reporting **0** deviation and still reaching a placement verdict, against an arc outline reporting a non-zero one and being refused. **17 mutants, 17 killed, 0 survived, 0 `stale_anchor`, 0 `invalid_run`**, baseline return code 0. Four guard invariants a green suite would otherwise have admitted: `OA02` reads the interior side backwards, `OA03` skips the chord-ring simplicity check that the direction verdict depends on, `OA06` measures the deviation at the polyline's vertices rather than along its chords, and `OA17` trusts a floating-point sample on a near-collinear arc. **Four needed intervention rather than relabelling, and each is recorded because the alternative is a passing check that tests nothing.** Two came back `stale_anchor` because their anchors matched twice in a file that now carries two ring assemblers, and were **re-anchored, not removed**. Two survived: the first was a sub-nanometre rounding inversion the reported integer genuinely cannot distinguish, replaced with the defect class the bound actually guards against; the second showed that a degenerate arc is caught twice, so the test it lacked was written — one that pins the refusal naming the *cause* rather than the broken loop that is its symptom, which is ADR-0123's lesson one construct over. |
| Integrity | Source hashes unchanged across both runs; `committed_board_bytes` is 0; no board byte, path, digest or coordinate is committed. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-28-outline-arc-acceptance-differential-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-28-outline-arc-acceptance-differential-v1.json) — self-digested, embedded predeclaration hash, cohort fingerprint, per-board outcome and first refusal, `committed_board_bytes: 0`. Mutation spec [`2026-08-28-edge-cuts-outline-arcs.json`](../mutants/2026-08-28-edge-cuts-outline-arcs.json). Per the B-114/B-118/B-119 precedent the measuring script is **not** committed: it duplicates the adapter's conversion call and the boards it needs are not in the tree, so a committed copy could not be replayed from a clean checkout. |
| Claim | At these two commits, on this fixed 13-board cohort: the `Edge.Cuts` outline curve gate is removed from all eight public boards that stopped there; **0 of 10 public boards convert, before and after**; the eight redistribute onto named constructs behind the gate, six of them onto ADR-0095's copper-text refusal; and the frozen own 18-save corpus is unchanged at 15/18 with the new disclosure at zero throughout. |
| Non-claims | **No board converts, so nothing here is a first third-party conversion.** Not a claim that concave arcs, major arcs, outline circles, Béziers, polygons or footprint-local `Edge.Cuts` graphics are supported — each is refused by name. Not a claim that the inscribed outline is exact: it is an under-approximation bounded by 5,000 nm and disclosed as such, and the one surface publishing a two-directional outline claim refuses on it. Not a sample of any population. No routing, DRC, placement, apply, board write or fabrication result. |

### B-136 — the closed public footprint-graphic-on-copper surface behind B-133's two terminals

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question | B-133 recorded the six former container-allowlist boards splitting across three refusals that already name their construct, **two** of them at `footprint graphic on a copper layer is unmodelled copper`. B-133 declined to say what those two boards draw. This row answers that and nothing more: which layer-bearing `footprint` children they put on a copper layer, of which primitive kind, in what grammar, and — because a graphic's head says only that a shape exists — what geometry a conservative envelope would have to bound and what that envelope would cost. |
| Cohort | The **exact** B-129/B-130/B-132 13-board cohort, replayed rather than re-derived: the same digest-bound saves under the same predeclared fingerprint `sha256:bfec8210d6d4eb746ffdbfb3b70309ce`, all 13 digests re-verified byte-for-byte before the run, no board refetched or reserialized. Selection is frozen by a domain-separated commitment over the two selected manifest entries, `sha256:5cded48f9b024054682bfdda759843757d1d3912877169caf25d71476698b32a`, so a same-count membership swap fails the run. |
| Method | Clean commit `c51f437568ab009bdb7f9a1beb52494418bbc3a0` — **before this branch touches the adapter**, so the wall this census decomposes is still standing when it is measured. Python 3.12.13 on macOS arm64; KiCad was not invoked; clean worktree enforced by the runner before and after. A **sibling instrument**, not a parameterized one, for the reason [B-132](#b-132--the-closed-public-footprint-field-surface-behind-b-131s-six-terminals) records: a frozen selection commitment means "these entries, selected *this way*", and a runtime-parameterized rule would leave the constant saying only "these entries". Output plumbing, path resolution and the shape helper are *imported* from the setup census rather than restated. Selection does not take B-129's `other` bucket, which is a catch-all: it re-walks the same mask loop from `benchmark_fixed_point_masking_census`'s own `_convert`/`_mask_first_blocker`, requires the terminal to be this sentence at a `kicad_pcb.footprint[N].graphic` locator, and requires the walk's depth to agree with the classifier's. Sources re-read unchanged after measurement. |
| Continuity with B-133 is computed, not asserted | B-133 reported the six boards splitting **2 / 3 / 1** across `footprint graphic on a copper layer is unmodelled copper`, `footprint-local zones are unsupported` and `footprint graphic on Edge.Cuts is unsupported`. This instrument re-walks **every** public board's terminal, buckets it into those three named successors, and **requires that exact partition before it aggregates anything**. The run reports `b133_successor_partition: {copper_graphic: 2, footprint_zone: 3, edge_cuts_graphic: 1, other: 4}` — B-133's split reproduced by a different instrument at a different commit, which is the continuity link a prose cross-reference could not have been. |
| The vocabulary is derived from the grammar, not from the boards, and that is what makes the zeros mean something | `GRAPHIC_HEADS` is the union of the **layer-bearing** top-level `case T_…` arms of `PCB_IO_KICAD_SEXPR_PARSER::parseFOOTPRINT_unchecked` on the KiCad `9.0` and `10.0` release branches — the ten heads the adapter routes through `head.startswith("fp_") or head in {"property", "point"}`. The `fp_ellipse`/`fp_ellipse_arc` pair is reachable only on `master` (11.0 development) and is deliberately excluded, so a board written by that version lands in `other` rather than being silently covered. **`other` is 0** at both the head level and the `fp_poly` child level, so the predeclared grammar covers everything these two boards write. |
| The surface is one primitive kind, and five others are present in quantity and absent from copper | Across **102 footprints** the two boards write **34,208** layer-routed graphics. The layer partition — total, and reconciled against that count — is `non_routing` **34,034**, `courtyard` **118**, `single_copper` **56**, and **`multi_copper` 0, `edge_cuts` 0, `absent_or_malformed` 0**. Of the 56 on copper, **`fp_poly` is 56 and every other head is 0**. That zero is the row's sharpest number and a cohort-derived vocabulary could not have produced it: the same two boards carry **32,532 `fp_line`, 1,064 `property`, 243 `fp_rect`, 184 `fp_text`, 50 `fp_circle` and 23 `fp_arc`** — every one of them on a layer that is not copper. Five kinds observed in quantity, reported at zero *on copper*, is an absence the observation could have contradicted. |
| The grammar the 56 are written in, and it is closed | Four **total** payload partitions over the 56, each reconciled against that population by `measure` rather than trusted: `fill_yes` **56** (`fill_no`, `fill_absent`, `fill_invalid` all 0); `stroke_positive` **56** (`stroke_zero`, `stroke_absent`, `stroke_invalid` all 0); `pts_xy_only` **56** (`pts_with_curved_child`, `pts_absent`, `pts_invalid` all 0); `outside_net_tie_footprint` **56** (`in_net_tie_footprint` 0). The child grammar is `fill`/`layer`/`pts`/`stroke`/`uuid` at 56 each, with `locked` **0**, `tstamp` **0** and `other` **0**; every child is `one_atom` except `pts` and `stroke`, which are `many_children`. **Every occurrence carries an explicit non-zero stroke**, which is the opposite of the net-tie polygon's `(width 0)` and is the reason an envelope here must inflate by the stroke half width rather than stop at the vertex hull. |
| The geometry, which is the decision's direct input | Over the 56: **54 concave, 2 convex**; vertex counts `5–16` 2, `17–64` 14, `65–256` 34, `>256` 6; **26 self-intersecting**, 28 simple, 2 above the stated 400-vertex check cap and reported `not_checked` rather than guessed; and **`all_distinct` is 0** — 30 repeat only the closing vertex, 26 repeat an interior one. Two consequences, and they are the decision: a Board IR `Ring` **rejects a repeated vertex outright**, so no exact region model is even expressible for any of the 56; and a self-intersecting outline's filled area is not determined by the source at all — it depends on a fill rule the document never names — so the only model that is correct without choosing one is a model that contains the shape under *every* fill rule. |
| What a bounding envelope would cost, measured rather than assumed | Shape area over vertex-bounding-box area: 26 in `[0.25, 0.50)`, 28 in `[0.50, 0.75)`, 2 at `≥0.90`, and **none below 0.25** — so no envelope overstates its own shape by more than about four times. Against the board: **all 56** envelopes are `< 5%` of the board's `Edge.Cuts` bounding box, and **both boards'** whole envelope union — computed exactly by coordinate compression, not summed — is also `< 5%`. That is the number that made the coarser option declinable and the finer one unnecessary on evidence rather than on taste. |
| Where the copper sits | **4 carrier footprints**, and **all 4 are padless**. The copper these two boards refuse over is artwork in footprints that contribute no pad, not part geometry. |
| Integrity | Source hashes re-verified unchanged after measurement and `committed_board_bytes` is 0. Aggregate-only by construction, and one step stricter than B-132: **every ratio reaches the artifact as a membership count in a predeclared bucket**, so `ratios_committed` and `coordinates_committed` are 0 alongside `atom_values_committed`, `board_identities_committed`, `board_paths_committed` and `board_digests_committed`. Self-digested `sha256:a2fb92619075bb536d2439747846e51b22ebe7dcbeb3414fbc9127a98cab51c5`, binding runner digest `sha256:d25ff294e2bc804b5bc144f4e16f15e3eb471a2ae634cee5e5c7f61fd942d8cf`. Every payload and geometry partition is total, and its sum is reconciled against the population it partitions — the #226 review lesson applied before the review rather than after it. **Two defects in this instrument were found by its own checks during development, and neither moved a count.** The first reconciliation summed the *raw counter* rather than the closed projection `_closed_counts` publishes, so a classifier returning a bucket outside the vocabulary would have published a partition of zeros while the check passed; reconciling what the artifact will actually say closes that, and the `fp_poly` child grammar — the one partition whose total is its own projection — is where that check is load-bearing. Fixing it immediately exposed the second: a polygon with no readable three-vertex ring has **no** area ratio and no envelope, and the first version filed it into a ratio bucket anyway, fabricating a number the measurement does not support. It is now counted as `unbounded`, the two ratio partitions are partitions of `bounded`, and both counts are published. Re-run by the recorded procedure at the fixed instrument: **every aggregate came back identical except `envelope_cost`, which gained the two new counts at `bounded_polygons: 56` and `unbounded_polygons: 0`** — so no measured quantity in this row is downstream of either fix. |
| The instrument deliberately carries no adapter drift guard, and the absence is the honest option | Its sibling censuses check *containment* of a frozen accepted vocabulary, because a field census can keep reading a surface the adapter has quietly widened. This one cannot be fooled that way: its entire population is selected by one refusal sentence at one locator shape, so an adapter that stops emitting them makes the selection **empty** and the run fails before a single aggregate is computed. The only guard that could sit on top would re-derive the head vocabulary from a hardcoded copy of the adapter's branch predicate and compare it against itself — a check that passes whatever the adapter does. That is written down here rather than quietly omitted, because a check that cannot fail is not evidence. |
| Amendment — 2026-08-29, frame error found in review (Codex P1 on [#230](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/pull/230), thread `PRRT_kwDOTrPIR86dHkgF`) | **The envelope-union figure in the row above was computed in the wrong frame and is withdrawn.** `pts` coordinates are footprint-**local**, and the instrument appended every envelope box to one board-wide list without ever applying its carrier's `(at x y [angle])`. Two polygons drawn at the same local coordinates in different carrier footprints were therefore unioned as if they occupied the same board area, so the published `board_envelope_union_over_board_bounding_box` **under-reports** the area covered, and the claim line's *"each board's whole envelope union is also `< 5%`"* does not stand. The recorded bucket was `lt_0_05` on both boards; **no replacement value is published here**, because the digest-bound cohort was staged outside the repository and is no longer present, so the recorded procedure cannot be re-run at this time. It is withdrawn rather than corrected in place. **What is unaffected, and it is now checked rather than argued**: every head, layer, payload and geometry aggregate is computed from a single polygon and is invariant under any rigid motion of its carrier -- `test_the_per_primitive_aggregates_do_not_depend_on_the_frame` asserts exactly that by measuring the same board twice with its carrier moved and rotated. **What is qualified**: `area_over_bounding_box` and the per-polygon `envelope_over_board_bounding_box` are *areas*, invariant under translation and under a quarter turn but not under an arbitrary angle; this census did not record carrier rotations, so whether they moved cannot be settled from the artifact. The fixed instrument now publishes a total `carriers.rotations` partition (`quarter_turn` / `other_angle` / `absent_or_malformed`), so a re-run answers that question instead of leaving it open. **The decision this row fed does not rest on the withdrawn number**: D-230 turns on `all_distinct: 0` and the 26 self-intersecting rings, both frame-free, and the envelope-cost figure was a sanity check beside it rather than a premise. The production adapter was audited for the same omission and does **not** have it -- it transforms every vertex before bounding, which mutant `CG21` now proves is load-bearing. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-28-public-copper-graphic-census-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-28-public-copper-graphic-census-v1.json); runner [`benchmark_public_copper_graphic_census.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_public_copper_graphic_census.py). |
| Claim | On exactly these two boards, the copper-layer footprint-graphic surface is **one primitive kind** — a filled, explicitly stroked `fp_poly` on one declared copper layer, written in a closed five-child grammar, none of it net-tie copper. Nothing outside the predeclared KiCad-9/10 layer-bearing grammar appears. None of the 56 admits an exact Board IR region model, and a bounding envelope over all of them costs under 5% of either board's area. |
| Non-claims | **No copper-graphic support, and no conversion.** A construct's presence is not a decision to accept it and this row implies none; direction of error is [D-230](decision-ledger.md)'s question, not this instrument's. Not a claim about any board's *first* refusal — both boards refuse in front of `footprint`, one at copper text and one at `Edge.Cuts` outline curves, so this surface is reachable only behind B-129's deliberately unsound masks. **Not a claim about the three boards refusing at footprint-local zones or the one at `Edge.Cuts`**, which are different constructs and are not touched here. Not a sample: two boards selected for licence clarity and then for one terminal. Not a claim that `other: 0` holds outside this cohort, nor for a KiCad 11 board whose two new graphic heads are deliberately outside the vocabulary. The area and union ratios are bucket memberships, not measurements of any particular board. No board write, apply authority, editor mutation, routing, DRC, or fabrication result. |

### B-137 — what accepting the copper polygons bought, measured against B-133 with the same instrument

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question and predeclared prediction | Recorded **before `_refuse_footprint_routing_graphic` was touched**, at commit `c51f437568ab009bdb7f9a1beb52494418bbc3a0`, and deliberately predicting *against* movement in the aggregate because B-133's own result is the null hypothesis. **(1)** Both boards clear the copper-graphic gate: B-136 shows all 56 of their copper graphics are filled, explicitly stroked `fp_poly` on one declared copper layer in a closed child grammar, so no narrowed refusal fires. **(2)** **0 of 13 convert**, unchanged. **(3)** Public `gate_stack_depth` stays byte-identical at `{0:1, 1:2, 2:5, 3:2}` — the successors in this family are unmaskable named refusals, which the cohort already demonstrates: three boards terminate at `].zone` locators and one at a `].graphic` locator, so clearing one unmaskable terminal to reveal another buys a gate and not a mask pass. **(4)** The closed blocker histogram does not move either — `other` stays at **6**, because B-129's vocabulary has no member for any successor in this family. **(5)** The successor for both, **named as a guess so it can be wrong**: `footprint-local zones are unsupported`, the sibling terminal three of the six already sit at; the named alternative is `footprint graphic on Edge.Cuts is unsupported`. **(6)** The three private controls are unchanged at `{0:3}`, two `courtyard_topology` and one `disjoint_outline_topology`. **(7)** The own frozen 18-save corpus stays **15/18** and the new `footprint_copper_graphic_envelope_count` is **0 on every one of the 15** — not because that corpus has no copper polygons, but because all six of them are net-tie copper on the pre-existing ADR-0092 path this change must not touch. **A 6 there would mean the change had captured net-tie copper and is wrong**, which is what makes the zero a test rather than a formality. |
| Measured before the prediction, and labelled measured | Taken on the same pre-change commit by a scratch probe. Public **direct** conversion **0/10**, with **cohort-00 refusing first at copper text and cohort-01 at `Edge.Cuts` outline curves** — both in front of `footprint`, so no first refusal can move. Own frozen 18-save corpus **15/18**, the three refusals being courtyard topology and disjoint `Edge.Cuts` loops. Own corpus copper footprint graphics: **6 `fp_poly` across 5 saves, every one inside a net-tie footprint**, which is the fact prediction (7) rests on. |
| Method | **The same instrument, byte-for-byte, at two source commits.** `scripts/benchmark_fixed_point_masking_census.py` is unmodified by this branch and its recorded runner digest `sha256:fb0f53a397ea7e9f6290c01153c8fa8698496472ba86c8f69a95303e9be506fe` is identical to B-129's, B-131's and B-133's; the cohort is the same 13 digest-bound saves under the same predeclared fingerprint `sha256:bfec8210d6d4eb746ffdbfb3b70309ce`, re-verified before the run. The two commits are `f55c6eb` and `a845332`, and the only source between them that conversion reads is the adapter, its diagnostics dataclass and the service's measured-count map — the change itself. **Both this differential and B-136's census were retaken after the branch was rebased onto 0.11.0's release prep**, because that rebase rewrote every SHA the first runs had bound and a ledger cannot cite a commit that does not exist; every aggregate came back identical, so nothing recorded here is downstream of the rebuild. Python 3.12.13 on macOS arm64; KiCad was not invoked; clean worktree enforced by the runner before and after. Conversion of the unmasked cohort and of the frozen own corpus was measured separately by a scratch probe at both commits, and the own corpus's material digest `sha256:afe5d9d09b4aa89ffa8d5ae84df284ee` was re-checked against the recorded frozen-census fingerprint and found unchanged. |
| Direct result — **no board converts, and this slice never could have made one** | **Public 0/10 before and after. Own frozen corpus 15/18 before and after**, with `unmodelled_group_count` unchanged. Both boards refuse **in front of** `footprint` — cohort-00 at copper text, cohort-01 at `Edge.Cuts` outline curves — so no first refusal moves, and the surface is reachable only behind B-129's deliberately unsound masks. That was stated before the work rather than discovered after it. |
| Predictions 1, 2, 3, 4 and 6 met — and the artifact is byte-identical to B-133's and B-131's | Public `gate_stack_depth` `{0:1, 1:2, 2:5, 3:2}`, `other: 6`, **0 of 13 convert**, private controls `{0:3}` with two `courtyard_topology` and one `disjoint_outline_topology`. Every `aggregates` key compares **equal** to B-133's artifact — which was itself equal to B-131's — so three consecutive slices now share one measured aggregate; only `commit`, `recorded_at_utc` and `run_id` differ. Prediction 1 is confirmed separately by the terminal walk: **both boards clear the copper-graphic gate**, and no narrowed refusal fires on either, which is what B-136's four total payload partitions predicted. |
| Prediction 7 met, and it was the sharp one | The own frozen corpus stays **15/18** with `footprint_copper_graphic_envelope_count` **0 on every one of the 15** — and that zero is a test rather than a formality, because **5 of those 18 saves carry copper `fp_poly` expressions, six in total, every one inside a net-tie footprint**. A 6 there would have meant the stray-copper path had captured net-tie copper and converted it twice. The measurement was taken before the prediction was written, which is why the prediction could be stated as a falsifiable one. |
| Prediction 5 is **refuted**, and the successor is one structural level *up* rather than down | The successor for both boards is neither `footprint-local zones are unsupported` nor `footprint graphic on Edge.Cuts is unsupported`. Both now stop at **a root `zone` refusal**: `expression contains an unsupported semantic field`, at `kicad_pcb.zone[0].fill` on one board and at `kicad_pcb.zone[4]` on the other. **That is a third instance of the class ADR-0123 names**, after `setup` and `footprint` — a container-level allowlist — and one of the two locators names **no field at all**, which is precisely the defect that ADR names. The guess was wrong because it reasoned about *which constructs a board contains* where the adapter reasons about *the first refusal in document order*, and the guess was named in advance so it could be. The one substantive thing this differential establishes beyond the null is therefore the same shape B-133 established: the next wall is a known class at a new site, and it is now measurable because the sentence it refuses with is decomposable at one of the two locators and is the ADR-0123 defect at the other. |
| What did move, and it is not in the aggregate | The terminal `footprint graphic on a copper layer is unmodelled copper` is **gone from both** boards, and gone from the adapter's reachable set except as a fallback for a head outside the closed table. B-129's vocabulary buckets both successors as `other` too, so `6 → 6` is the census correctly declining to name a class it has no member for, exactly as B-131 and B-133 recorded. The movement is visible only in the terminal diagnostics, read by a scratch probe, and is reported as such. |
| What this slice does **not** evidence, and the gap is structural | **No board in any measured corpus reaches the conversion.** Both B-136 boards clear the acceptance gate and then refuse at the root zone field, and `_zones_and_keepouts` runs before `_footprints_and_pads`, so `_footprint_copper_obstacle_segments` never executes on a cohort board. What real boards exercise is the *gate*; the *envelope geometry* is evidenced by the containment proof in that method's docstring and by a Hypothesis property test that asserts, for generated polygons at nanometre-precision stroke widths inside a quarter-turned footprint, that every convex combination of the vertices pushed out by the full stroke half width in both axes lands inside the segment's modelled extent. Saying so is the point of this row. |
| Integrity | Source hashes unchanged across both runs and `committed_board_bytes` is 0. The differential artifact is self-digested `sha256:05025829bed537ed55f6dbf7bf01b8d3ba4aea7406980d77ddb36d10e00b6df3`, at commit `a845332a7403d9e4e5f5fdd386f54883440c2c82`. Contract evidence for the change itself: unit tests in both directions per decision — bounded-and-counted, the generalization landing on the net-tie segment for the net-tie fixture, each of the five malformation classes refused by its own name, each unmodelled primitive kind named separately, footprint copper text separated from the root sentence, net-tie copper untouched, the vertex charge, and the containment property. **20 mutants, 20 killed, 0 survived, 0 `stale_anchor`, 0 `invalid_run`**, baseline return code 0. **One survived its first mapping and was killed by writing the test it lacked rather than by relabelling it**: `CG18` disables the census's raw-against-projection reconciliation, and the payload-gap test named for it was already failed by the *other* reconciliation loop, so the check under mutation was never reached — the `fp_poly` child grammar is the one partition whose total is its own projection, and a test driving that is what kills it. **Two committed mutants in other specs were re-anchored, not removed**, when this change moved the lines they sat on: `CPG10` (the custom-pad vertex reservation, which now shares its subtraction with the graphic charge) and `FF10` (the measured-count map, which now has a tenth entry below the one that mutant deletes). |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-28-copper-graphic-masking-differential-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/board-ir/2026-08-28-copper-graphic-masking-differential-v1.json); runner [`benchmark_fixed_point_masking_census.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_fixed_point_masking_census.py), unmodified from B-129. Mutation spec [`2026-08-28-footprint-copper-graphics.json`](../mutants/2026-08-28-footprint-copper-graphics.json). |
| Claim | On this exact cohort and with the same instrument, D-230 removes `footprint graphic on a copper layer is unmodelled copper` from the public terminal set entirely and converts **no board**, publicly or privately. Its measured aggregate is indistinguishable from B-133's and B-131's, which was predicted. The successor is a **root `zone` container refusal** — the third site of ADR-0123's class — and at one of the two locators it names no field. |
| Non-claims | **Not a conversion win, and not a first third-party board.** Not a claim that the two are nearer conversion by any measure other than one gate; the depths did not move, so this slice bought a gate and not a layer. **Not a claim that the envelope has been exercised on a real board** — no cohort board reaches the conversion, and the geometry's evidence is a proof and a property test, not a corpus measurement. Not a claim about the four unaffected boards, the three at footprint-local zones, or the one at `Edge.Cuts`. `other: 6` is an honest absence of vocabulary members, not a blocker-class definition. Not a measurement of the root `zone` allowlist, which this differential shows **is** the next wall on these two boards but does not decompose. Nothing routed, DRC-checked, applied, written or fabricated; no masked board is valid. |
| Amendment — 2026-08-28, on merging #228 | **The depth histogram above is not reproducible on merged `main`, and the reason is a second slice rather than a defect in this one.** #228 (`606f0006`) accepts convex `Edge.Cuts` arcs, which was the *first* refusal on eight of the ten public boards and therefore a construct B-129's masker had to splice away before it could reach anything else. With both slices present, the same frozen instrument at the same cohort fingerprint reports public `gate_stack_depth` **`{0:3, 1:4, 2:3}`** rather than `{0:1, 1:2, 2:5, 3:2}` — fewer mask passes to reach the same walls, not different walls. **Everything this row actually claims is unchanged**: the public and private blocker histograms are byte-identical (`other: 6`, `copper_layer_kind: 1`, `footprint_graphic_layer: 1`, `graphic_layer_arity: 1`, `root_semantic_construct: 1`; private `{0:3}` with two `courtyard_topology` and one `disjoint_outline_topology`), **0 of 13 still convert**, and public direct conversion is still **0/10**. Measured on the merge commit by the same unmodified runner. The row above stays as recorded, because it states what was measured at `a845332`, where it remains true. |
