### B-138 — the first real-editor IPC observation, and what CopperMCP's own transport did with it

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| What this closes, and how narrowly | The post-0.8.0 audit's M3 entry criterion **E3** asks for "one real-editor IPC observation, **or** a recorded park of `#68`". This is the observation. It is **one** session, **one** board, **one** KiCad build, **one** host, and it is read-only. It is not an apply, not a mutation, not `#68`, and not a corpus result. `docs/handoff/project-state.md` previously recorded that **no successful real-editor IPC oracle run had ever happened**; that line is now false and is corrected in the same change. |
| The operator gate, which is the reason this could happen at all | The workstation's KiCad IPC API server was disabled for the whole life of this project, and enabling it is a change to the operator's machine that no agent may make. The operator enabled it and consented to this run. `COPPER_MCP_ALLOW_LIVE_IPC=1` was set for the measuring process only; it is not persisted anywhere in the repository. |
| Environment | KiCad **10.0.5** (self-reported `10.0.5 (10.0.5)`) running `pcbnew` with `hardware/coppertone-buffer/coppertone-buffer.kicad_pcb` open. `kicad-python` (`kipy`) **0.7.1**, whose bundled API version is **10.0.1**. macOS 26.5.2 arm64, Python 3.12.13. Neither `KICAD_API_SOCKET` nor `KICAD_API_TOKEN` was set in the measuring process: the connection went to `kipy`'s default local endpoint. |
| The committed board, digested but not read over IPC | `hardware/coppertone-buffer/coppertone-buffer.kicad_pcb`, `sha256:3bcd01ec4942fccabfaf1c21bdae050a31a7bf99af7ab1bcb0dbb3d0aabcfb94`, 166,070 bytes. |
| **The headline result: three of CopperMCP's four live surfaces refused this real editor, and the refusals are the substance** | The observation was deliberately taken through **CopperMCP's own transport** rather than raw `kipy`, so what is recorded is what this project's surfaces do. They do not agree with each other. `inspect_live_board` on its **default** path — the MCP-facing one — raises `KicadIpcVersionError`, *"connected KiCad is newer than the installed kicad-python API"*, because 10.0.5 > 10.0.1 and `check_version()` raises `FutureVersionError`. `inspect_live_editor_context` refuses identically **and has no escape hatch at all**: `capture_live_editor_context` accepts `allow_future_api`, but the MCP-shaped wrapper never passes it. `probe_live_kicad_ipc`, the capability oracle, returns `status: skipped`, `capability: kicad_plugin_environment_absent` — it requires the KiCad-launched plugin environment (`KICAD_API_SOCKET` **and** `KICAD_API_TOKEN`), which an ordinary shell does not have, so it declined a session that was in fact reachable. **The first real contact with a real editor produced two typed refusals and one skip before it produced any observation.** |
| What was observed, through the documented non-MCP development escape hatch | With `allow_future_api=True` — which `capture_live_board`'s docstring already describes as an operator/development probe and deliberately *not* an MCP argument — `inspect_live_board` returned: `kicad_version` **10.0.5**, `api_version` **10.0.1**, `compatibility` **`future_api_unverified`**, `board_digest` `sha256:7e0ea855d98ae63f8dbb96db88c29b5963ed5609d1d9fa2567c714d4c1d6ec22`, `board_bytes` **165,571**, `socket_kind` `default-local-ipc`, `read_only` true, and a `session_revision` of `pbkdf2-hmac-sha256:91a8fafa…`. `capture_live_editor_context` bound the same `board_digest` and added `active_layer_index` **3** / `active_layer_name` **`F.Cu`** with an **empty** selection. |
| Two facts a reader should not skim past | **(1) The live in-memory board is not the committed file.** 165,571 bytes over IPC against 166,070 on disk, and the digests differ. Nothing is wrong: this is exactly the gap [ADR-0074](../adr/0074-live-ipc-one-undo-commit-apply.md) names — `get_as_string` binds a content identity for the in-memory document and never to the on-disk file, because the difference between them *is* the dirty flag the protocol omits. It is recorded here because it is the first time the gap has been *measured* rather than argued. **(2) `object_counts["nets"]` is 0 while the editor reports 15 nets.** Both are correct and they are not the same quantity: `_count_serialized_items` counts top-level `(net …)` declarations, and this KiCad 10 document carries **none** — its 117 net references are all by name on items. `kipy`'s `Board.get_nets()` reads KiCad's in-memory netlist and returns 15. A reader taking `nets: 0` as "this board has no nets" would be wrong, and nothing in the redacted observation says which quantity it is. |
| Method | `scripts/probe_live_text_shapes.py`, run once at commit `7dc662142017b65ee65717061dd3d5a052988184` against a clean worktree, verified clean again after the run with the runner bytes re-checked. The instrument refuses loudly and publishes **no artifact** when no editor answers, when `kipy` is absent, or when `COPPER_MCP_ALLOW_LIVE_IPC` is unset — the refusal path was exercised against a nonexistent socket before the real run and produced exit 1 with no file. It never calls a mutating API, never saves, never issues an editor command, and never calls `get_selection_as_string`. |
| Integrity and redaction | Self-digested `run_id` `sha256:7bc4e0eea60b1231cb823aed76b9fcf5e7eeef524ad671b4524de629dfbac5f6`, binding the runner digest recorded in the artifact's own `runner_digest` field. **That digest names the instrument as it stood at the run, and the instrument has since been hardened** (review of #234 added a revision binding, `--board` validation before read, and a census cap; see the amendment below). The artifact is deliberately **not** regenerated: its measurements bind the session that produced them, and a re-run would be a different observation rather than a correction of this one. A reader comparing `runner_digest` against `HEAD`'s runner will find they differ, and that is the correct outcome. **`committed_board_text_strings: 0`** — no board text string reaches the artifact; the board's four text items are identified by document order and reported as character counts, layer index and shape counts only. No net name, no UUID, no coordinate from a board item, no socket path and no token value is committed. The board's own bytes were digested from the committed file, never exported over IPC into the artifact. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-29-live-editor-text-shape-probe-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/capability/2026-08-29-live-editor-text-shape-probe-v1.json); runner [`probe_live_text_shapes.py`](../../scripts/probe_live_text_shapes.py). |
| Claim | A real KiCad 10.0.5 editor was observed, read-only, through CopperMCP's own transport, and the transport's behaviour against it is now measured rather than assumed: **the default MCP path refuses on a version check**, the editor-context path refuses with no override available, the capability oracle skips for want of a plugin environment, and an observation is obtainable only through a development flag that stamps the result `future_api_unverified`. E3 is **satisfied as an observation**. |
| Non-claims | **Not an apply, not a mutation, not `#68`.** Nothing was written, no commit/undo transaction was opened, and no live apply precondition was exercised. **Not a claim that the live path is production-ready** — it is measured here to refuse by default, which is the opposite claim. Not a corpus result: one board, one session, one build, one host, one operating system. Not a DRC, routing, placement, conversion or fabrication result. Not evidence that `session_revision` detects an editor restart — that property is argued from KiCad's source in `kicad_ipc.py` and was **not** tested here, because testing it would mean restarting the operator's editor. Not a claim about any KiCad other than 10.0.5, or any `kipy` other than 0.7.1. |
| Amendment — 2026-08-29, three guards added in review (Codex P2s on [#234](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/pull/234), threads `PRRT_kwDOTrPIR86dU_u1`, `PRRT_kwDOTrPIR86dU_u5`, `PRRT_kwDOTrPIR86dU_vA`) | **The instrument was hardened; the artifact was not regenerated, and no recorded number moved.** All three findings were correct and none of them invalidates the recorded run — they close ways a *future* run could publish something dishonest. **(1) One revision, checked rather than assumed.** `inspect_live_board` confirms stability within a single call, but nothing tied the four surface probes and the whole text census to each other, so an operator editing the board or restarting KiCad mid-probe could have produced one artifact spanning two revisions or two sessions — precisely the unbindable evidence [D-231](decision-ledger.md) warns about. The run now binds `board_digest` **and** `session_revision` up front and re-verifies at two named checkpoints; a move refuses and publishes nothing. The session half is load-bearing on its own: a reopened, unmodified board has an identical digest and only the session identity sees the restart. **(2) `--board` is validated before it is read.** Containment (lexical *and* `realpath`, which catches a symlinked parent that `lstat` would follow), regular-file, non-empty, a 16 MiB cap charged from `st_size`, and git-tracked — all in front of `read_bytes`, so `--board /dev/zero` is refused rather than read forever, and an untracked path can no longer be labelled 'the committed board'. **(3) The census is capped and the run is time-boxed.** A 256-item ceiling and a 180 s wall-clock budget shared across *every* IPC call — the client's per-call `timeout_ms` bounds one request and says nothing about a loop of them. Both refuse loudly, naming what had and had not been measured; neither truncates, because a truncated census would read as a complete one. Artifact schema goes to `0.2.0` for future runs and gains `revision_binding` and `budget`. **16 refusal-path tests, none needing a live editor; 7 hand-applied mutants (revision check, size cap, item cap, budget check, containment, regular-file, tracked), 7 killed, baseline green** — so the tests are load-bearing rather than vacuous. This probe carries no committed mutation spec, so the mutants were applied and reverted by hand and are reported as such, not as a recorded spec run. |
| Records | [B-139](#b-139--kicads-text-to-shape-api-measured-against-adr-0095s-five-exit-conditions), [D-231](decision-ledger.md), [R-182](risk-register.md), [SEC-168](security-ledger.md), [ADR-0074](../adr/0074-live-ipc-one-undo-commit-apply.md), [issue #68](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/68) |

### B-139 — KiCad's text-to-shape API, measured against ADR-0095's five exit conditions

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| The lead this tests, and who wrote it down | Section 5.2 of the [copper text envelope research note](../research/kicad-copper-text-envelope-v1.md) recorded `GetTextAsShapes` as a *research lead* for [ADR-0095](../adr/0095-copper-text-has-no-derivable-envelope.md)'s refusal, in these words: **"with board context available, KiCad can expand text and expose `GetTextAsShapes`"**. That sentence contains a prediction and this row measures it. |
| The real symbol, read from the installed package rather than recalled | **`kipy.kicad.KiCad.get_text_as_shapes(texts) -> list[CompoundShape]`**, at `kipy/kicad.py:241` in `kicad-python` 0.7.1, sending wire command **`kiapi.common.commands.GetTextAsShapes`** and reading `GetTextAsShapesResponse.text_with_shapes`. It is a method on the **client**, not on `Board`. The `.pyi` contract states the return shape directly: *"Depending on whether the text is using the KiCad stroke font or a custom font, the response will be a compound shape containing a set of polygons or a set of segments."* Measured: exactly that. |
| It works, and here is what it returns | On the live board's four root texts (all `F.SilkS`, so this board has **no copper text** — the census establishes the API, not a copper case): 2, 7, 3 and 37 characters returned **4, 47, 27 and 275 `Segment`s** respectively, in **absolute board nanometres**, each carrying `stroke.width` 180,000 nm — the declared 0.18 mm. The response's `GraphicShape` carries exactly two fields: `attributes` and `segment`. |
| **Determinism: yes, within one session.** | Three consecutive calls over all four texts returned byte-identical protobuf: `sha256:5806d72f63da688eec782d97616930facf7c4f19228bffa438672c580be1a06f` three times. The outline-font path repeats identically too. Under [ADR-0109](../adr/0109-a-drc-count-carries-the-comparability-it-was-taken-with.md)'s `repeated_agreement` discipline this clears the *repetition* bar — **within one session, one build and one host**, which is the whole of what was varied. It says nothing about agreement across sessions, builds, hosts or font caches, and no differential is offered on those axes. |
| **Condition 1 — REFUTED, and this is the headline.** `${…}` is **not** expanded. | The command carries a bare `Text` proto and **no document or project reference**, and KiCad renders the **literal** string. Measured by width, because segment counts alone would not settle it: `${MYVAR}` (8 chars) plots **6.7990 mm** against 8 literal `A`s at **6.8467 mm** — the same string length, the same width to within one glyph's difference. `${CURRENT_DATE}` (15 chars) plots **13.1324 mm**; had it resolved to a 10-character date it would plot near the measured 10-character width of **8.5610 mm**. It does not. So this API **neither resolves a text variable nor refuses one** — it silently renders `$`, `{` and `}` as glyphs. The research note's premise that "with board context available, KiCad can expand text" is **wrong for this call**, and condition 1 is untouched by it. |
| **Condition 2 — inherited, not solved.** The copper is still a function of the rendering machine. | `mmmm` at the board's text size: stroke default **5.0371 mm** (52 segments); `Helvetica` **4.4816 mm** (4 `Polygon`s); a face this machine does not have, `NoSuchFaceXYZ`, **4.9992 mm** — **11.5 % wider than `Helvetica`**, because fontconfig *substitutes* rather than failing; `Times New Roman` 4.3355 mm; `Courier` 3.3763 mm. The API faithfully reports whatever the host's font stack produced and carries **no font identity in the reply**. That is ADR-0095 reason 2 reproduced through a second, independent instrument — the note measured it with `kicad-cli`, this measures it over IPC — and it is not weakened by the API's existence. |
| **Condition 3 — the one condition that genuinely moves, and only partly.** | Condition 3 asks that glyph extents be *a read value, not a measured one*. Over a live session they **are** read: the actual plotted geometry comes back per glyph, so no vendored Newstroke table and no sampled ASCII ceiling is needed. The probe confirms why that matters — `漢` (U+6F22) plots **1.4181 mm** wide against `m` (U+006D) at **1.0371 mm**, **36.7 % over** the ASCII glyph, exactly the "false ceiling one step outside the sample" ADR-0095 warns about. What it does **not** do is make the read value *bindable*: it is read from a session, not pinned by a digest, and condition 3's own wording asks for a table "CopperMCP vendors and pins by digest, or one KiCad publishes as a contract". A live read is a third thing, and D-231 is where that is decided. |
| **Condition 4 — unmet, and unmeetable from this response alone.** | Condition 4 requires that a font-build mismatch **refuse** rather than pass. The response's `GraphicShape` carries `attributes` and `segment` and **nothing else**: no font name, no font-build version, no Newstroke version, no KiCad build stamp. Nothing in the reply lets a client detect that a later KiCad's glyph table differs from the one that produced these coordinates. Without condition 4, condition 3's live read is sound only for the session it was taken in — which, as the ADR says of the measured case, "is the same thing as unsound" for anything replayed. |
| **Condition 5 — corroborated in direction, not independently established.** | A synthetic `TextBox` with corners at (20, 30)–(60, 32) mm returned copper far outside them in every case tried — a descender string overflowing left by ~22 mm, a wrapping string by ~84 mm, an unbreakable 52-character word by ~42 mm, all growing with the string exactly as ADR-0095 §4.4 measured by plot. **This is reported as corroboration and not as a measurement of the ADR's claim**, because the `TextBox` was constructed by this instrument rather than read from a board, and its anchor/justification behaviour was not verified against a board-resident text box; the absolute overflows are therefore not trustworthy as figures. The direction agrees; the magnitudes are not published as evidence. |
| A bonus finding: KiCad's own extents box is not a containing box, over IPC too | With the pen included, `(g)pqy` plots **0.0233 mm below** the bottom of the box `GetTextExtents` reports for the same text. `ABC` and `~{ABC}` stay inside. That reproduces ADR-0095 reason 3 through the IPC surface rather than the plotter, and it is why `get_text_extents` — the obvious shortcut sitting next to `get_text_as_shapes` — is **not** a candidate envelope either. |
| A defect in the official binding, found by using it | **`kipy` 0.7.1's `get_text_as_shapes` cannot be handed the board's own text objects.** `Board.get_text()` returns `BoardText`/`BoardTextBox`; the command accepts `common_types.Text`/`TextBox`, and the two are unrelated classes. The `isinstance(t, Text)` check therefore falls through to the `TextBox` branch and the call dies in `CopyFrom` with a `TypeError` naming the wrong type rather than the wrong argument. The caller must bridge explicitly with `BoardText.as_text()` / `BoardTextBox.as_textbox()` — which **drops the layer**, so the copper/non-copper distinction that ADR-0095 turns on is not even visible to this API. Recorded because a future reader will hit it, and because the layer loss is a contract fact, not an inconvenience. |
| Method | Same instrument, same run, same session as [B-138](#b-138--the-first-real-editor-ipc-observation-and-what-coppermcps-own-transport-did-with-it): `scripts/probe_live_text_shapes.py` at commit `7dc662142017b65ee65717061dd3d5a052988184`. Synthetic probe strings are the instrument's own input and are published verbatim so the run is reproducible; board strings are not. Widths are pen-inclusive extents in nanometres, converted for presentation only. |
| Artifact | [`2026-08-29-live-editor-text-shape-probe-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/capability/2026-08-29-live-editor-text-shape-probe-v1.json); runner [`probe_live_text_shapes.py`](../../scripts/probe_live_text_shapes.py). |
| Claim | KiCad's text-to-shape API **exists, works, and is deterministic within a session**, and it moves **one** of ADR-0095's five exit conditions — condition 3, partially, by making glyph extents a read value instead of a sampled constant. It **refutes** the research lead that motivated it: the call does not expand text variables, so condition 1 is untouched. Conditions 2 and 4 are measured to be *unaddressed by this API* — font substitution is inherited and no build identity is returned — and condition 5 is unaffected. **ADR-0095's refusal stands unchanged.** |
| Non-claims | **No copper text support, and no change to any refusal.** This board carries no copper text at all, so nothing here is a measurement of a copper case. Not a conversion, routing, DRC or apply result. Not a claim that the API is deterministic across sessions, builds, hosts or font caches — only three calls in one session were compared. The `TextBox` overflow magnitudes are **not** published as evidence. Not a claim that any offline board gains anything: every measurement here needs a live editor, and D-231 is where that tension is decided, not this row. |
| Records | [B-138](#b-138--the-first-real-editor-ipc-observation-and-what-coppermcps-own-transport-did-with-it), [D-231](decision-ledger.md), [R-182](risk-register.md), [ADR-0095](../adr/0095-copper-text-has-no-derivable-envelope.md), [ADR-0109](../adr/0109-a-drc-count-carries-the-comparability-it-was-taken-with.md), [copper text envelope research](../research/kicad-copper-text-envelope-v1.md), [issue #193](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/193) |

### B-140 — negotiated multi-pin admission and repair-target census

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question and predeclared boundary | B-124 measured the former exactly-two-pad/shared-world-origin admission contract at 0 of 20 boards and named multi-pin/request-local admission as the next capability that could change the answer. Before routing outcomes were measured, this successor fixed one prediction: on B-088's exact per-board submitted net sets, **16 of 20** boards would be admitted and four would remain unable to form the minimum two-request envelope. Terminal status, completion, physical-gate triggers and repair-target availability were explicitly not predicted. The run asks two later questions separately: how many admitted boards reach a complete-allocation physical-clearance trigger, and how many such triggers contain a violating two-pad candidate the existing repair transaction could actually select. |
| Source and integrity | Measured on clean commit `30692df496e0dc250d3b09bae5ad9b7b11a3d827` on 2026-08-29. The runner required a known, unchanged Git tree before measurement and again before its exclusive-create write; protected predecessor/reference artifacts could not be overwritten. Self-digested run `sha256:ef3724e6a58ba94df8a7e392a4e407029fb2720844fc5adcc4654cac8bbc3a31`, binding runner `sha256:eb4339e5e2264c62a1971958af6a6d5d037d5e5703a3609561c7f5f607279774` and reference runner `sha256:8fb5d05fb60a75b66e4720b3aa3ba9e0b28dbd8c3377ac159a239adbc4795fed`. Two deterministic repetitions were required, together with an uninstrumented production-result parity check before redaction. |
| Dataset and reference authority | The same committed 20-board subset of `dwiel/tscircuit-benchmark` at upstream commit `be36518b5bf51755dae92c230061ab3cf4e3e063`, MIT licence digest `5e1e61463320a61ebac4a326a3c6ea5608280c75e5b95c6931497e3a14ebb632`. B-088 is verified by document digest, snapshot revision, full outcome and candidate commitment in original problem-net order before its per-board routed sets are admitted as inputs; its artifact run remains `sha256:facf95ee9770ffab8c1bc403a32a403e55ca79f2c56d1eabc6679eb6ec4dfca3`, with 70 of 117 nets routed independently. B-124 remains immutable historical evidence and is never replayed as current behaviour. |
| Configuration | Production `negotiated-congestion-v2` coordinator and `astar-grid/0.7.0` backend; one selected signal layer and 250,000 nm grid step per envelope; 2–32 selected-layer pads per net; request-local grid origins; 2–32 distinct requests; 8 iterations; 2,000,000 total expansions; 10,000,000 obstacle checks; 2,000,000 physical checks; present/history penalties 20,000,000/5,000,000 nm; per-route caps 100,000 expansions, 250,000 nodes, 2,048 obstacles and 2,000,000 obstacle checks. `repair_settings` is null. The two-pad control uses the same production path and budgets. |
| Result | The admission prediction is confirmed exactly: **16 of 20** boards are admitted and four cannot form a two-request envelope. All 16 admitted boards reach a complete-allocation physical-clearance trigger; across eight iterations per board the whole-set gate is called **128** times. **0 of those 16 boards** contains a violating candidate with `pad_count == 2`, exactly the arity filter used by the current repair target selection. Consequently repair is never enabled, **0 negotiated nets complete**, and no candidate tuple is published. The corpus contains 36 exactly-two-pad nets, of which B-088 routed 0; the two-pad control admits 6 boards, makes 6 physical-gate calls and also completes 0 nets. Mean wall times across two repetitions were 84.990 s for the B-088-routable population and 3.746 s for the two-pad control; timing is descriptive, not a performance claim. |
| Semantics and direction of error | A physical trigger means `CLEARANCE_VIOLATION` on a complete allocation with at least two violating nets. Repair-target availability is narrower: a candidate in the gate's exact request-bound tuple must both name a violating net and have `pad_count == 2`. The recorder cannot see the connection map, so it uses the solo reference's `already_connected` count as an upper bound when deciding completeness. That can only over-count completeness and make the trigger easier to report; it cannot manufacture the recorded zero eligible-target count. Under the primary population the bound is exact because every submitted net produced a solo candidate. |
| Mutation evidence | [`2026-08-29-negotiated-multipin-corpus-census.json`](../mutants/2026-08-29-negotiated-multipin-corpus-census.json), SHA-256 `bf41fad917d871a239a00ac2526f00a328dc62ba30d819c151cac6aafa4fdd5e`. The standard committed harness separately deletes violating-net membership and the exact two-pad arity from repair-target recording; the mixed-arity/mixed-membership fixture kills each mutation. Python 3.12.13 / macOS-26.5.2-arm64-arm-64bit, baseline return code 0: **2 killed, 0 survivors, 0 stale anchors, 0 invalid runs**, with byte-identical restoration after each mutant. |
| Artifact and replay guard | [`2026-08-29-negotiated-multipin-corpus-census-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-29-negotiated-multipin-corpus-census-v1.json); runner [`benchmark_negotiated_corpus_census.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_negotiated_corpus_census.py). The focused suite pins schema, source commit, run ID, both immutable evidence roots and runner hashes, verifies the self-digest, replays the report against a fresh module-scoped measurement, reconciles every published aggregate, and scans the public output for private board, net, revision, path and geometry values. |
| Claim | The bounded 2–32-pad/request-local-origin coordinator contract removes B-124's admission wall on the predeclared 16 boards, and the mandatory physical gate then exposes the next blocker: the corpus's complete allocations violate clearance but contain no target the two-pin-only repair transaction may select. The next agent-executable capability is therefore a bounded multi-pin repair-target, window-provenance and complete-topology reconstruction contract under the existing atomic work and physical gates. |
| Non-claims | **No repair-effectiveness or routing-quality claim.** Repair was disabled; no repaired candidate was constructed, validated or published. The 0-versus-70 comparison is not like-for-like: B-088 routes nets independently against an unrouted snapshot, while negotiation must publish one mutually compatible allocation. No grid, budget, layer or selection rule was tuned to move a result. Not KiCad DRC, electrical, SI/PI/EMC, thermal, DFM, fabrication, apply, editor, hardware or general-corpus evidence. The aggregate artifact contains no board identity, digest, revision, path, net identity, candidate or geometry. |
| Records | [B-124](#b-124--whole-board-negotiated-routing-census-on-the-external-corpus), [ADR-0126](../adr/0126-negotiated-routing-admits-bounded-multi-pin-nets-on-request-local-lattices.md), [D-233](decision-ledger.md), [R-184](risk-register.md), [SEC-169](security-ledger.md), [issue #90](https://github.com/seunghyukchoe/copper-mcp/issues/90) |

### B-141 — repair-enabled differential on the negotiated multi-pin corpus

| Field | Recorded evidence |
|---|---|
| Question and predeclared boundary | B-140 established the exact successor population for the multi-pin repair question: 20 fixed B-088 boards, 16 constructible negotiated envelopes, four envelope refusals and 70 reference-routed submitted nets. B-141 predeclared a two-arm replay over those same immutable snapshots and request tuples: an uninstrumented control with `repair_settings: null` and a treatment with the default bounded repair profile. Both arms were required to run twice, preserve the same population partition, and expose completion and every refusal separately. A positive completion delta was the measurable target; routing quality, DRC, electrical, fabrication and generalisation were explicitly not predicted. |
| Source and integrity | Measured on clean source commit `e3828ecc16688bf0ad3050eebb4ea8c55c076797` on 2026-08-30. The closed runner is bound as `sha256:7308365981ffe3bdbf7707842dfa4e9136cbaa77a52770c7a981f0e172dfa7e0`; its configuration digest is `sha256:d7609e8dae5608cfed9127591e1dfb4f858f569f5136336d3e6963e095564daf`. The report run ID is `sha256:f90f410afa9337c13960fd5cb24676f30a8463d1e4103e68e86ee9d4c2adc7fe`, and its raw artifact SHA-256 is `sha256:6987374c1aec317a0a3eaf3067343823aeed7ac142cf3f82afd9a6dd88626d19`. The companion commitment has run ID `sha256:6bbac831c9f4c5a21908fc3587bbce1c8b4599a63f332209be5b5a90006674ce` and raw SHA-256 `sha256:7d1c9a07ad3f57d5c16dfb68fdfff9e2f753d06f31bbc573ee4c985740335a46`; both bind the same source, runner and configuration. |
| Dataset and reference authority | The exact B-140 population binding is preserved: 20 offered/imported boards, 16 admitted, four unable to form a two-request envelope, 70 submitted nets, and 70 nets routed by the B-088 reference baseline. The corpus manifest count is 20 with digest `sha256:f22673bb677b5cd82c9e7369dcef746d6bbdc9a59e1891628132d93a3dd71a0e`; B-140 remains the immutable admission authority and B-088 remains the independent per-net reference, not a like-for-like negotiated result. |
| Configuration and environment | Production `negotiated-congestion-v2` with `astar-grid/0.7.0`, seed 0, one selected signal layer, 250,000 nm fixed grid step, request-local lattice origins, 2–32 selected-layer pads, 8 iterations, 2,000,000 expansion ceiling, 10,000,000 obstacle-check ceiling and 2,000,000 physical-check ceiling. Router limits are 100,000 expansions, 250,000 grid nodes, 2,048 obstacles and 2,000,000 obstacle checks. Treatment uses one attempt with 4,096 local expansions, projection cells, validator edge checks, validator obstacle checks and window cells; control has no repair settings. Python 3.12.13 on Darwin/arm64; repetitions are 2 and deterministic replays are true. |
| Result | The population is unchanged in both arms: **20 offered/imported, 16 admitted, four envelope-refused and 70 submitted**. Control completes **0 boards / 0 negotiated nets**, with 11,326 physical checks and 128 iterations; its repair publication and `completed_with_repair` counts are both 0. Treatment completes **1 board / 2 negotiated nets**, with 18,758 physical checks and 121 iterations; it publishes one repair and one `completed_with_repair` result, with 43,750,000 nm wire. Treatment's one repaired board is the only completion; 15 admitted boards remain `no_path` and four remain `not_run` after envelope refusal. Mean arm wall times are 37.625 s control and 37.938 s treatment, descriptive only. |
| Differential | Treatment minus control is **+1 completed board, +2 negotiated nets, +7,432 physical checks and +43,750,000 nm wire**, with `positive_completion_delta`. The differential is a completion observation under the fixed bounded transaction, not a claim that repair improves route quality or that either arm is equivalent to B-088's independent per-net routes. |
| Mutation and focused evidence | B-141's committed evidence-contract spec [`2026-08-30-negotiated-multipin-branch-repair.json`](../mutants/2026-08-30-negotiated-multipin-branch-repair.json), SHA-256 `sha256:4421e029d35c0125ec2a146b004ef7284e69cdb889d676f959753266562afebd`, kills **22/22** contract mutants with zero survivors and zero control failures. The focused suite passes **94/94**. This is distinct from #238's **35/35** capability-mutant result for the production branch-repair implementation; the two counts are not added together or presented as one harness. |
| Artifact and replay guard | The self-digested aggregate report is [`2026-08-30-negotiated-multipin-branch-repair-v1.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-30-negotiated-multipin-branch-repair-v1.json) and the independent closed commitment is [`2026-08-30-negotiated-multipin-branch-repair-v1.commitment.json`](../../benchmarks/results/routing/2026-08-30-negotiated-multipin-branch-repair-v1.commitment.json); the runner is [`benchmark_negotiated_multipin_branch_repair.py`](../../scripts/benchmark_negotiated_multipin_branch_repair.py). Load/validate checks bind source, runner, configuration, artifact and corpus authority; the report and commitment contain no board, net, revision, candidate, path, coordinate or geometry payload. The two repetitions produce byte-identical arm projections and the artifact is create-only. |
| Claim | On the exact immutable B-140/B-088 population, enabling the bounded private multi-pin repair transaction produced one deterministic positive completion differential: one board and two nets completed in treatment versus none in control, with one published repair. The result closes the machine-executable B-141 differential evidence contract under ADR-0127; it does not close issue #90. |
| Non-claims | **No held-out routing-quality or generalisation claim.** The result does not establish shorter, better, more routable or more manufacturable routes; the wire and physical-check deltas are measured accounting, not quality scores. It is not KiCad DRC, electrical, SI/PI/EMC, thermal, DFM, fabrication, apply, editor, hardware or network evidence. It contains no human benchmark calibration and no claim about the four envelope-refused boards, any board outside the exact 20-board population, or B-088's independent route quality. #90 remains open for human review/calibration; the next agent-only direction is #91's private surrogate ranking with authoritative signoff. |
